# Wiki Index

Canonical list of all wiki pages, grouped by domain (the primary retrieval axis).
Regenerate after every ingest. `data/wiki/private/` is deliberately excluded.

**2117 pages** across 18 domains.

## Foundations

- [[A/B Test Lifecycle and Interval Decisions]] — A sound A/B test fixes the metric, the minimum detectable effect and the sample before launch, randomises and analyses at the same unit, checks the split, tests once, and decides by comparing the confidence interval with the effect worth shipping rather than reading the p-value.
- [[Access Control Below the User, Audit Logs and Breach Scope]] — A control the subject can alter is no control: enforce access below the user, log who read what with purpose, and keep an inventory so breach scope is a lookup.
- [[Action Chunking]] — A vision-language-action policy infers at 1–10 Hz while robot control runs at 50–1000 Hz, so it emits 25–50 actions per inference and replays them while the next chunk computes — smoother motion bought with blindness to sudden change; plus the discrete-token vs. flow-matching action representations and the planner + VLA split.
- [[Adam, AdamW and Decoupled Weight Decay]] — L2 regularisation inside Adam is not weight decay (weight_decay 0.1 collapsed to chance while AdamW trained fine), and learning rate matters more than optimiser choice: a sweep by factors of ten moves accuracy from 0.174 to 0.961 on one model.
- [[Adapting While Learning Tool Usage Adaptation]] — Adapting While Learning trains a model to answer directly after absorbing tool-derived knowledge, and to call scientific tools only on questions its own accuracy marks as hard; on custom science datasets an 8B model reports 59.31 against 30.20 for the base, with a tool-rate drop from about 91% to about 52% (self-reported).
- [[Adaptive Branching MCTS]] — AB-MCTS decides at each node whether to sample a new answer (wider) or refine an existing one using external feedback (deeper), via Thompson sampling; it had the best average rank in the paper, with margins often inside the error bars (self-reported).
- [[Adversarial Examples (Intriguing Properties of Neural Networks)]] — Szegedy et al. (2013) found that small, minimum-norm input perturbations found with L-BFGS flip a trained network's prediction, that the perturbed inputs transfer across models and training sets, and that training on them lowered test error in a preliminary run.
- [[Agent Fundamentals]] — Agent = LLM + Context + Tools, read as policy, observation space and action space — the five-part context and what breaks when each part is removed, the four-step tool call, why RL-trained 'model-as-agent' still needs a harness, the three places an agent can learn, and when a workflow beats an autonomous agent.
- [[Agent Lightning v1.0 Harnessed Agentic RL]] — Agent Lightning v1.0 trains agents behind a proxy that records token IDs per call, merges calls into sequences only when the exact token prefix survives, and computes advantage and loss per rollout; Qwen3.5-9B went from 41.8% to 56.4% on SWE-bench Verified (self-reported).
- [[Agent-FLAN Agent Tuning Data Design]] — Agent-FLAN tunes Llama2 for agent tasks by rewriting ReAct and JSON traces as chat, weighting data by capability and adding negative samples; it reports a 3.5-point overall gain over a re-implemented AgentTuning baseline and fewer hallucinated tool calls (self-reported).
- [[AgentArk Multi-Agent Distillation]] — AgentArk distils five-agent debate trajectories into a single student model by three routes (reasoning-enriched SFT, data augmentation, process-aware distillation with a process reward model and GRPO); the authors report about 4.8% average gain for a single agent, but most Qwen3-8B gains are under one point (self-reported, single runs).
- [[Agentic vs Agentive: Agent Model Position]] — A position paper separates agentic systems, whose competence sits in scaffolding and harnesses, from agentive ones that carry goals, identity and self-regulation inside the model, and proposes a Goal-Identity-Configurator architecture; it has no experiments, only theorems under stated assumptions.
- [[AgenticQwen Dual Data Flywheels]] — AgenticQwen trains small tool-use models with GRPO over three rounds on data from two loops, one that expands failed math problems and one that grows tool workflows into behaviour trees; the 8B model averaged 47.4 against 23.8 for its base (self-reported).
- [[AgentScaler Environment Scaling]] — AgentScaler builds function-calling environments automatically by treating every tool call as a read or write on a generated database, filters trajectories by final database state, and SFTs in two stages; its 30B model scored 70.4 on tau-bench Retail (self-reported).
- [[AgentTuning and AgentInstruct]] — Supervised fine-tuning of Llama-2-chat on 1,866 filtered GPT-4 agent trajectories (AgentInstruct) mixed 20/80 with ShareGPT; the paper reports large held-in gains and a held-out gain that is significant only at 70B (self-reported).
- [[Airflow 3 Cron Timetables and the Logical Date]] — From Airflow 3 a bare cron string means a trigger timetable whose logical date is the fire time, so ds names the day in progress; name CronDataIntervalTimetable to process the completed day.
- [[Anscombe's Quartet: Plot Before Summarising]] — Four datasets with identical means, variance, correlation and fitted line look completely different, so summary statistics alone cannot replace a plot.
- [[APIGen]] — APIGen generates function-calling training data and keeps only calls that pass format, execution and semantic checks; xLAM-7B trained on its output ranked 6th on BFCL (85.65 overall, June 2024 snapshot, self-reported).
- [[Artificial Hippocampus Networks]] — Artificial Hippocampus Networks (AHN) keep a lossless 32K sliding-window KV cache and fold each token that leaves it into a fixed-size recurrent state, cutting FLOPs and cache at 128K while matching or beating full attention on average scores, but not on exact recall (self-reported).
- [[Atomic Commit and Why Two-Phase Commit Blocks]] — Two-phase commit gets all-or-nothing across nodes with a coordinator, but a participant that voted yes is stuck holding locks if the coordinator dies; it is acceptable inside one system and a poor fit across heterogeneous ones.
- [[Attribution Credit vs Causal Share]] — Attribution divides each sale's credit among the channels on its path and must hand out every conversion; last touch and Shapley both overcredit channels that reach already-ready buyers, so credit is accounting, not cause.
- [[Auditing Selection Rates With Intervals and Intersections]] — Report impact ratios with counts and delta-method intervals, never as a pass mark; small groups can read anything, and margins can all clear 0.8 while a pre-chosen crossing fails.
- [[Autodata Agentic Self-Instruct]] — Autodata wraps data creation in an agent loop that accepts a question only when a weak solver fails and a strong solver succeeds; the resulting reinforcement-learning data beat single-pass data in three domains, though about half of one gain was truncation fixing.
- [[Autoencoder Bottleneck vs PCA and Anomaly Scores]] — An autoencoder beats PCA on reconstruction only at tight codes (2 to 8 dims) and loses at 32, a denoising variant trades clean accuracy for repair, and the best reconstruction is not the best anomaly detector: compute PCA first.
- [[AutoGym Blueprint-First Verifiable Gyms]] — AutoGym generates agent training environments from a blueprint that fixes the solution, requirements and verification before any environment exists, with ground truth computed by execution; moving only difficulty parameters raised a hard-task share from 10% to 39% (self-reported).
- [[B-Trees vs LSM-Trees]] — The two ways stores keep an index: B-trees update fixed pages in place behind a write-ahead log, LSM-trees append immutable sorted segments and merge them; trade-offs in write cost, read cost and tail latency.
- [[Backdoor and Front-Door Criteria]] — A set Z satisfies the backdoor criterion if it has no descendant of treatment and blocks every path into treatment; any valid set gives the same effect, and when the confounder is unmeasured the front-door criterion chains two identifiable effects through a mediator that carries everything.
- [[Backfills, As-Of Dimensions and Past-Dependent Tables]] — With idempotent per-period tasks a backfill is a rerunnable, parallelisable loop; the hard parts are which dimension version old periods should use, tables that read the previous partition, late data restatement, and reconciling afterwards.
- [[Bag of Words, n-grams and TF-IDF]] — Sparse count and TF-IDF document vectors: how vocabulary controls, n-grams and idf weighting shape features, and why the vectoriser must be fitted inside the training fold.
- [[Basel IRB Capital Vocabulary]] — The terms linking a default-probability model to bank capital (PD, LGD, EAD, expected vs unexpected loss, RWA) and the one-factor logic that makes capital grow far more slowly than expected loss as PD rises.
- [[Batch Norm Eval Mode and Small-Batch Failure]] — Batch norm uses batch statistics in train mode and running statistics in eval mode, so forgetting model.eval() costs about 9 accuracy points at batch 32, tiny batches break it, and layer norm removes the batch dependence.
- [[Batch Normalization]] — Batch Normalization (Ioffe & Szegedy, 2015) normalizes layer inputs using mini-batch statistics during training to reduce internal covariate shift — enables higher learning rates, reduces sensitivity to initialization, and acts as a regularizer. Rethinking BatchNorm (Wu & Johnson, 2021) exposes pitfalls with EMA population statistics, train/inference inconsistency, and domain shift.
- [[Batched Rerunnable Data Backfills]] — Fill a new column in small keyed batches whose predicate selects only unfilled rows so a dead job simply restarts; a backfill alone is wrong on a live table, so a trigger for new writes and a repair pass come first.
- [[Bayes Action and Loss Functions]] — A posterior is not a decision: write down what each wrong answer costs, average that loss over posterior draws and pick the minimiser; squared loss gives the mean, absolute loss the median, zero-one the mode, and asymmetric losses give answers no point estimate would.
- [[Bayes Rule and the Base Rate Fallacy]] — P(A|B) and P(B|A) share a numerator but not a denominator; Bayes' rule converts between them through the base rate, which is why a 99 percent accurate test of a rare event is mostly false positives and why a significant p-value is often a dud.
- [[Bayesian A/B Testing with Expected Loss]] — Draw from each arm's Beta posterior to get P(B > A), the lift interval and expected loss; ship when expected loss is below the smallest difference you care about, and report the lift size, since P(B > A) alone says nothing about it.
- [[Beta-Binomial Updating and Credible Intervals]] — The Beta prior is conjugate to a Binomial rate, so Bayesian updating is addition of counts; the posterior gives a credible interval that says what people wish a confidence interval said, and separates MLE, MAP and posterior mean.
- [[BG/NBD Customer Lifetime Value]] — For non-contractual customers, BG/NBD needs only frequency, recency and observation time to estimate the chance a customer is still active and expected future orders; Gamma-Gamma adds order value, and LTV needs an explicit horizon and discount.
- [[Bi-Mamba Binarized State Space Model]] — Bi-Mamba binarizes the input and output projections of Mamba-2 to 1-bit weights with learnable scale and shift, trained from scratch with distillation from a LLaMA2-7B teacher, and keeps most of full-precision accuracy where post-training binarization collapses.
- [[Bias-Variance Decomposition and Model Complexity]] — Expected squared error splits into bias squared, variance and irreducible noise; complexity trades the first two, and the symptom table says which side to fix.
- [[BiDAF Bi-Directional Attention Flow]] — BiDAF links a question and a paragraph with memory-less attention in both directions and passes per-word vectors on to a modeling layer instead of summarising the paragraph early.
- [[BitNet b1.58 Ternary Weights]] — BitNet b1.58 trains a language model natively with weights restricted to -1, 0 and 1; the 2B model trained on 4T tokens averages 54.19 against 55.23 for Qwen2.5-1.5B on 16 benchmarks with 0.4 GB non-embedding memory (self-reported).
- [[Blocking and Fellegi-Sunter Match Weights]] — Entity matching first cuts candidate pairs with cheap blocking keys, whose recall caps everything downstream, then scores each pair by summed log(m/u) evidence with a three-way link, review, reject decision.
- [[Blue-Green View Swap for Data Deploys]] — Build the new version of a table beside the old, audit it, then repoint a view in about a millisecond; rollback is the same swap while the old build is kept and nothing has been written on top.
- [[BM25 and the Lexical Gap]] — BM25 ranks documents by saturated, length-normalised term matches; both it and TF-IDF cosine are blind to synonyms, which is the gap dense encoders and hybrid retrieval fill.
- [[Bootstrap Resampling and Its Failures]] — The bootstrap resamples the independent unit with replacement to get a standard error or interval without a formula, but it fails for extremes, tails that the sample never saw, and dependent rows resampled one at a time.
- [[Bottleneck Adapters for Parameter-Efficient Transfer]] — Adapters are small trainable modules inserted into a frozen pretrained network: Rebuffi et al. (2017) added residual 1x1-convolution adapters to a ResNet for ten image domains, and Houlsby et al. (2019) added serial bottleneck adapters to BERT, both nearly matching full fine-tuning (all numbers self-reported).
- [[Bradley-Terry Preference Model]] — The pairwise-comparison model that converts human choices between two responses into a scalar reward — the shared formal core underneath RLHF reward models, DPO, and RLAIF preference models.
- [[Breaking Schema Changes and Expand-Contract]] — Diff contract versions in the producer's CI to flag removals, renames, type changes and tightened constraints as breaking, and ship an unavoidable break as add-new, migrate consumers, then drop the old column in a new major version.
- [[Build it Break it Fix it (Dialogue Safety)]] — Dinan et al. train an offensive-language classifier in rounds where crowdworkers write messages that fool the current model, then retrain on them; the adversarially trained model beat a standard-trained one on adversarial test rounds (self-reported).
- [[Cache vs Source of Truth: Write-Through and Write-Behind]] — A system of record holds the authoritative data and derived data can be rebuilt from it; write-behind silently turns a cache into the source of truth for its flush window, and the test is to delete the cache and see what breaks.
- [[Calibrated Score, Deterministic Decision Rule]] — A ranked score is not a decision: deterministic code turns a calibrated probability into an action via a cost-based threshold, so calibration is correctness and target definition (ability vs propensity vs uplift) precedes model choice.
- [[CALM Composition to Augment LLMs]] — CALM joins a frozen large anchor model to a frozen smaller specialist through learned projections and cross-attention over every fourth layer; the authors report gains on low-resource translation, maths and code over the anchor without the forgetting seen after further pre-training, and a synthetic task neither model can do alone (self-reported).
- [[Campaign Breakeven and Uplift Holdout]] — A contact campaign pays only for customers it persuades, not those who would buy anyway; compute the required lift as cost per contact over margin per extra order, then measure it against a randomised holdout, never against the contacted group's own past.
- [[Categorical Encoding Choices]] — One-hot, ordinal, frequency and target encoding suit different models and cardinalities; fit the encoder on training data and handle unseen categories.
- [[Causal Design Selection Beyond Randomisation]] — When you cannot randomise, choose a design by how assignment happened (rollout with a pre-period, threshold, randomised nudge, rich covariates, one treated unit), name its untestable assumption, and check difference-in-differences pre-trends, since diverging trends almost doubled an estimate.
- [[CCNet Web Crawl Corpus Pipeline]] — CCNet deduplicates Common Crawl paragraphs before language identification, then buckets each language's documents by perplexity under a Wikipedia language model.
- [[Chain of Hindsight]] — Chain of Hindsight fine-tunes a language model on sequences that pair outputs with templated 'Bad' and 'Good' feedback using the ordinary next-token loss, so no RL stage is needed; on GPT-J 6B it beat PPO-based RLHF in pairwise human preference.
- [[Chain-of-Agents AFM Distillation]] — Chain-of-Agents distils trajectories of a multi-agent system into one agent foundation model with supervised fine-tuning and then DAPO reinforcement learning, reporting 59.8 on AIME25 and 55.3 on GAIA for 32B models (self-reported, vendor team, small-sample evaluations).
- [[Change Data Capture and the Dual-Write Problem]] — Writing one change to two systems leaves them permanently different about half the time; CDC instead reads the database's own ordered change log, and a replay-safe consumer stores the log position per row and remembers deletes.
- [[Change the Channel, Not the Count]] — when per-source yield is an order of magnitude below target, mix channels by job and record rights at the source level on three default-deny axes
- [[Chart Colour Scales and Accessibility]] — Colour has three jobs (categorical, sequential, diverging) with one rule each; pin mappings by name, centre diverging scales on a real zero, and meet WCAG contrast, non-colour and table requirements.
- [[Chart Declutter and Direct Labelling]] — Strip every element that adds reader effort without understanding, set the style once, label data directly, and make the title state the finding; small multiples need a shared scale and grey context.
- [[Choosing a Chart from the Question]] — Pick the chart from the question being asked, and keep marks honest: bars start at zero, one y axis, several bin counts, uncertainty bands read as uncertainty, and titles that state the finding.
- [[Choosing a Metric From the Cost of Errors]] — A loss rewards a specific answer (mean, median, quantile), MAPE rewards forecasting low, balanced accuracy hides a cost ratio of N:P, and MCC cannot respond to costs; pick the metric from the decision first.
- [[Choosing a Store from the Access Pattern]] — Pick a data store by describing how records are found, how much one query touches, how data changes, its size and who operates it; row vs column is the first split, and each of ten store kinds is a bet that fits some patterns and hurts under others.
- [[Choosing an Encoder vs CNN vs LSTM]] — Decide by score at your data size, fit to the problem's structure, and cost at your length: a CNN won on small digits at every size, the encoder aced long-range tasks and lost local-pattern ones, and attention cost grows as n squared; take the learning curve on your own data.
- [[Choosing Batch, Stream or Online Serving]] — Pick the serving shape by when the consumer needs the answer; batch is the default because a failure is a rerun, precompute works only when traffic concentrates on few keys, and online latency is mostly features and framework, not the model.
- [[Choosing k and Naming Clusters Honestly]] — Scale and choose features before clustering, expect BIC, silhouette and seed agreement to disagree on k, shuffle columns as a null check, and only name clusters that survive reseeding.
- [[Choosing Which Rows to Label: Active Learning and Rules]] — Rules can stand in for many hand labels but learned rule weights lose to a plain vote at low label density; uncertainty sampling beat random on generated data and lost AUC on real data, and its labelled set is biased.
- [[Classification Metrics, Confusion Matrix and Thresholds]] — Read accuracy against a dummy baseline, derive precision, recall, F1, balanced accuracy and MCC from the confusion matrix, and move the threshold on validation data.
- [[CLT on Heavy-Tailed Metrics]] — The law of large numbers and central limit theorem only earn their keep at large N on zero-inflated heavy-tailed data: a nominal 95 percent normal interval on spend covered the truth 56 percent of the time at n=30 and needed tens of thousands of users to reach 95.
- [[Clustered Standard Errors]] — When rows share a group that was randomised or shares shocks, cluster the standard errors at that level: ignoring it understated the SE by half and inflated the false-positive rate to 28 percent, and it still fails with few clusters.
- [[Clustering Algorithms: k-means, Agglomerative and DBSCAN]] — k-means needs round similar clusters, agglomerative gives a dendrogram without prediction, DBSCAN finds arbitrary shapes and noise; with no ground truth, inspect the clusters.
- [[Clustering Categorical and Mixed Data]] — One-hot plus k-means goes deaf to categories because dummy variance is small; FAMD and MCA recode before PCA, reducing first can hide groups, and the best method depends on which columns hold the groups.
- [[Clustering Control World Test]] — Before trusting behavioural clusters, run the pipeline on a control world drawn from one Gaussian: HDBSCAN's silhouette gate can pass it, UMAP-map silhouettes cannot tell structure from noise, and clusters must beat a one-line rule on a real decision.
- [[Clustering Validation Against Named Baselines]] — validate unsupervised segments against named trivial baselines, leakage analogues and a matrix-health rule that closes the weak-result escape hatch
- [[Coconut Continuous Latent Reasoning]] — Training method that feeds the model's last hidden state back as the next input embedding instead of decoding a token, using a multi-stage curriculum; reported to match or beat CoT on ProsQA with far fewer tokens but not on GSM8k (self-reported).
- [[Code Completion Poisoning]] — Schuster et al. show that adding crafted files to a code autocompleter's training corpus, or fine-tuning on them, makes it rank an insecure completion first in attacker-chosen contexts, with targeted variants that fire only in chosen repos (self-reported).
- [[CodeRL Unit-Test Actor-Critic]] — CodeRL finetunes a pretrained code model (CodeT5-770M) with an actor-critic loop that uses unit-test outcomes as rewards, then at inference refines passing programs and repairs failing ones from error messages (Critic Sampling); it set new APPS pass@k results in 2022 (self-reported).
- [[Cohort Retention Triangle]] — Cohorts hold customer age fixed so retention changes can be read as a cohort effect (who arrived) or a period effect (something hit everyone), with incomplete ages masked and channel value judged over 24 months, not at the first order.
- [[Collocations and Pointwise Mutual Information]] — PMI scores word pairs by how much more often they co-occur than chance; it overrates rare pairs, so it needs a minimum count, while raw frequency surfaces only common pairs.
- [[Compacter Adapters]] — Compacter builds adapter weights from shared Kronecker factors and per-adapter rank-one factors, so a frozen T5-base trains 0.047 to 0.073% of its parameters yet matches full fine-tuning on GLUE and SuperGLUE (self-reported).
- [[Compiling Workflows into Weights]] — Dennis et al. write a procedure as a flowchart, have a strong model generate dialogues along its paths, and fine-tune a small model on them so no orchestrator runs at inference; they report near-in-context quality at 128-462x lower cost per conversation on three scripted customer-service domains (self-reported, judged by the teacher model).
- [[Composer 2 Training Recipe]] — Cursor's coding model: continued pretraining of Kimi K2.5, then RL in the production harness with Dr. GRPO-style updates, self-summarization trained end to end and a length penalty; scored on the internal CursorBench-3.
- [[Computer Use Visual Grounding]] — How a GUI agent turns a screenshot into a click — Set-of-Mark labelling, structured DOM/accessibility indexing (prefer it whenever structure exists) and direct coordinate prediction with aspect-ratio-matched rescaling — plus the observation-interface fix for dynamic screens and why per-step efficiency is now the bottleneck.
- [[ComputerRL API-GUI Agent RL]] — ComputerRL combines an action space mixing generated APIs with GUI actions, step-level GRPO with a binary outcome reward, and Entropulse, which alternates RL with SFT on collected successes to restore entropy; its 9B agents scored 48.9% on OSWorld (self-reported).
- [[Concept-Drift Detector Families and Prequential Evaluation]] — A survey of online learning under drift sorts detectors into four families with different cost and precision, treats only real drift as needing adaptation, and scores streams prequentially because cross-validation breaks time order.
- [[Confidence Interval Coverage and Wilson]] — A 95 percent interval is a claim about the procedure's long-run coverage, and the textbook Wald interval for a proportion fails badly for rare events at modest n where the Wilson interval holds up.
- [[Conformed Dimensions, the Bus Matrix and Drilling Across]] — Joining a finer-grain table or a second fact directly onto a fact multiplies rows and inflates sums with no error; share conformed dimensions, aggregate each fact to them first, then join.
- [[Confounders, Mediators and Colliders]] — Control for a confounder, leave out a mediator if you want the total effect, and never condition on a collider; each mistake has a measured cost, and plotting the data beats trusting a single correlation coefficient.
- [[Connection Pooling Turns Failure into Waiting]] — Each Postgres connection is a server process under a fixed max_connections, so a burst beyond it fails; a pool shares a few connections and converts the failures into queueing, with PgBouncer transaction mode breaking session state.
- [[Consensus and Coordination Services]] — Consensus accepts a decision from a majority so it terminates while a majority is up; terms stop stale leaders, and most applications outsource it to a small coordination service or to a database row lock.
- [[Constitutional AI and RLAIF]] — Anthropic's two-phase replacement for human preference labels — critique-and-revise SL-CAI followed by RLAIF against a written constitution — plus what AI feedback provably matches and where it still underperforms humans.
- [[Constitutional Classifiers]] — Reference design for classifier guardrails — a natural-language constitution generates the synthetic training data, question and answer are judged together so coded requests are caught, and a near-free activation probe screens everything and escalates only suspicious traffic to a stronger classifier.
- [[Contract Change Impact Chain and the Same-Name Unit Change]] — Follow a column change from contract to catalog to metrics to named consumers using registration, lineage and the query log; a unit change under the same name passes every check.
- [[Contrast-Consistent Search]] — Contrast-Consistent Search (CCS) trains a linear probe on a frozen model's unlabeled hidden states so that a yes-no statement and its negation get opposite probabilities; Burns et al. report it beating calibrated zero-shot on average over 5 models and 10 datasets, and holding up under a misleading prefix (self-reported).
- [[Contrastive Self-Supervised Pretraining]] — Learn embeddings without labels by pulling positives together and pushing negatives apart (triplet loss, SimCLR, MoCo) or by avoiding negatives with a predictor plus moving-average target (BYOL), or by masking and reconstructing patches; collapse is the central failure and toy-scale runs do not reproduce paper gains.
- [[Convolution, Shift Sensitivity and Parameter Sharing]] — A convolution reuses one small filter at every position (sharing plus sparse connections), so it survives shifts that wreck an MLP, but pooling and the final linear layer keep it from true invariance; augmentation buys invariance only for the shifts shown.
- [[Copy-on-Write vs Merge-on-Read]] — Updating a lake table either rewrites whole files (copy-on-write) or appends delete and delta records merged later (merge-on-read); choose by update distribution, and compare Delta, Iceberg and Hudi as of their release snapshot.
- [[Cost-Based Decisions from a Posterior]] — Turn posterior draws and explicit costs into decisions: the newsvendor quantile short/(short+over) for stock, a cost-ratio probability threshold for blocking, and expected net value with regret risk across plausible costs for a ship decision.
- [[Counter-Metrics, Guardrails and Goodhart Variants]] — A guardrail is a global must-not-break; a counter-metric is paired with one target to expose hitting it badly, and it must come from a later funnel step; four Goodhart variants each need a different defence.
- [[Counterfactual Evaluation with Inverse Propensity Scoring]] — Logs only record what the old system showed; inverse propensity scoring reweights logged events by one over the logging probability to estimate an unshipped policy's value, at the price of variance, so log propensities, keep some randomisation and report effective sample size.
- [[Covariate Balance and Overlap Checks]] — Before reading any outcome, check that treated and comparison groups are comparable using standardised mean differences, variance ratios and propensity overlap; fix the design on balance alone, and report the effective sample size since 5,710 weighted controls were worth 107.
- [[Critical-State RL]] — Critical-State RL picks which single tool call in a multi-turn trace to train on, ranking candidates by how much the action itself changes the outcome, then runs occurrence-local RL on that call only; it beat the alternative turn in all four BFCL cells tested (self-reported).
- [[Cross-Validation Fold Spread and Choosing the Splitter]] — Fold spread sizes up noise but is not a clean standard error; compare models fold by fold, and pick the splitter (stratified, group, forward-in-time) that mimics how new data will arrive.
- [[Cross-Validation Variants and Nested Search]] — Pick the splitter that mirrors how the model meets new data, search hyperparameters on a log scale, and use nested CV because the best CV score is optimistic.
- [[CUPED Variance Reduction]] — CUPED subtracts theta times the centred pre-experiment value of the same metric from each outcome, cutting variance by rho squared without bias because the covariate predates assignment; it equals regression adjustment and fails for new users or weak predictors.
- [[Cut Cross-Entropy]] — Cut Cross-Entropy (CCE) computes the language-model loss in blockwise fused kernels without materialising the tokens-by-vocabulary logit matrix, cutting loss-layer memory from gigabytes to about a megabyte on Gemma 2 2B at near-equal speed.
- [[D-Adaptation (Learning-Rate-Free Optimisation)]] — D-Adaptation sets the learning rate by tracking a running lower bound on the distance from the start point to a minimiser, with no line search or extra gradient evaluations.
- [[Dagster Assets vs Airflow Tasks]] — Dagster's nodes are tables that should exist and Airflow's are work to run, so Dagster can answer which partitions are missing while Airflow reports which runs happened; both leave the checked table already written.
- [[DAPO]] — Four changes to PPO/GRPO-style RL — Clip-Higher, token-level loss, dynamic sampling and overlong reward shaping — each aimed at stopping the policy from collapsing early onto a single strategy; the reported state-of-the-art recipe for verifiable-reward math RL.
- [[Dashboard API Exposure and Payload Cost]] — A list endpoint returning the whole table is insecure and slow; return aggregates by default, filter fields with a response model, cap and keyset-paginate row-level routes, and enforce limits server-side.
- [[Dashboard Design: Paired Metrics and Context]] — A dashboard serves a recurring decision with few contextualised metrics, each growth metric paired with a health metric; Streamlit reruns the script per interaction, and BI tools suit shared editing.
- [[Dashboard Stack Layers and Choosing a Route]] — Every dashboard has analysis, application and presentation layers; chart libraries are presentation only, and the route (BI tool, Python app, full stack) is chosen by audience, data and maintainer.
- [[Data Catalog Metadata: Push, Pull and Measuring Adoption]] — A catalog needs both push and pull ingestion, separates tier from certification, and should be measured by stale descriptions and reads on certified tables, not coverage.
- [[Data Contracts Beyond Schemas]] — A data contract is a machine-checked agreement between producer and consumers covering structure, meaning, quality, freshness, ownership and grain; a units change such as dollars to cents passes every schema test and only a written semantic rule catches it.
- [[Data Defect Correlation]] — Error of a sample mean equals the correlation between being recorded and the value, times sqrt((1-f)/f), times the standard deviation, so a large self-selected sample can be worth a few hundred random rows while reporting a tiny standard error.
- [[Data Engineering Foundations]] — The six stages of data engineering as a pipeline discipline — ingest, transform, orchestrate, warehouse, monitor, feature-serve — where each stage's job is to prepare data for the next, plus the modern-stack tools (DuckDB, Polars, Iceberg, Dagster) missing from Zoomcamp-era material.
- [[Data Generating Process Simulation Check]] — Before trusting an analysis, simulate data from a known process, run the estimator, and report bias, spread and coverage; a placebo run with a true zero effect exposes a model that invents effects.
- [[Data Incident Playbook: Contain Before Diagnosing]] — A data incident runs detect, triage, contain, repair, verify and postmortem in that order, because wrong data persists and spreads after the cause is fixed; contain first, never fix raw in place, and close only when the checks that fired are quiet.
- [[Data Mesh as an Ownership Model and Federated Governance]] — A mesh decides who owns each dataset, not where it is stored; it only works if global rules run as a gate that can fail a publish and name the owner.
- [[Data Quality Dimensions and Tests as Failing-Row Queries]] — Quality is six separate questions, each needing more context than the last; a test is a query that should return no rows, with a stated tolerance, and row-level tests are blind to anything that is only visible against time.
- [[Data Science Curriculum Layers]] — The tree-shaped ML curriculum — statistical foundations, then supervised learning branching to model evaluation and independently to unsupervised/ensembles/Bayesian — plus the six-layer analytics progression that precedes it and the branching decision that ends it.
- [[Data SLOs, Error Budgets and Paging on the Symptom]] — An SLI measures, an SLO targets, and the error budget (one minus the target) is arithmetic for acceptable failure; page on symptoms that threaten the SLO, and adapt burn-rate thresholds to a batch cadence where one late run is a large share of the budget.
- [[Data Sufficiency Refusal]] — pure never-raising guard on minimum positives per fold that withholds cost metrics rather than caveating them; refuse-not-abort exits 0
- [[Data Systems Foundations Reading Map]] — Map and rules of thumb for the storage-and-compute layer under data engineering: what a system promises, encoding and layout, concurrent writers, partitioning and replication, failure, consensus, distributed compute, durability and choosing a store.
- [[Data Vault Hubs, Links and Satellites]] — Data Vault splits each source entity into insert-only hubs (keys), links (relationships) and satellites (attribute history) so new sources cost a table each; the price is several joins per attribute, so readers get marts on top.
- [[Datetime Parsing, Frequency Aliases and Time Zones]] — Parse dates with an explicit format, use the explicit pandas 3 frequency aliases, and store timestamps in UTC so ambiguous days, renamed aliases and daylight-saving hours do not corrupt results.
- [[dbt Build Test Semantics, Severity and Source Freshness]] — A dbt test is a query returning the rows that break a rule; in dbt build a failing error-severity test skips downstream models but the tested model is already rebuilt, and only freshness notices data that never arrived.
- [[dbt Incremental Models: Watermarks, Lookback and Strategies]] — An incremental model carries two code paths, and a watermark with no key duplicates the boundary row while a bare greater-than watermark loses late rows silently; use a unique key, a lookback sized from measured lateness, and a periodic full refresh.
- [[dbt Layers, Materialisations and the ref Graph]] — dbt compiles templated SELECTs into warehouse SQL and runs them in dependency order; staging, core and mart layers each read only the one before, so a wrong number traces to one line in one file.
- [[dbt Singular, Generic and Unit Tests with Reconciliation]] — Column tests cannot see a join that fans out; a reconciliation test of fact against source plus a unique test on every joined model's grain catches it, and unit tests with mocked rows guard logic that today's data does not exercise.
- [[dbt Snapshots: History as Samples]] — A snapshot is a Type 2 history built by comparing a mutable source with what it holds on each run, so it samples rather than records: changes between runs are lost and a missed run cannot be rebuilt.
- [[DECKARD LLM-Hypothesized World Model]] — DECKARD asks a few-shot LLM once for a crafting-recipe dependency graph, then verifies and corrects it through Minecraft experience during RL training, so the LLM guides exploration but not execution; it cut steps to a stone pickaxe from 32M to 2.6M.
- [[DeepConf Confidence-Filtered Voting]] — Scores each sampled reasoning trace by token-level confidence, keeps or stops the low-confidence ones, and votes over the rest; in the paper, offline voting raised AIME accuracy by about 5 points, while a separate online mode saved up to 84.7% of tokens in its best case (self-reported); needs logprobs.
- [[Delethink Markovian Thinker]] — Delethink trains a reasoning model with RL in fixed-size chunks, resetting context at each boundary to the query plus the last m tokens, so thinking length no longer sets context size; a 1.5B model trained at 24K matched or beat LongCoT-RL at the same budget on math (self-reported).
- [[Delivery Guarantees and Effectively-Once Processing]] — At-most-once, at-least-once and exactly-once differ only in whether position or output is saved first; effectively-once comes from idempotent keyed sinks, offsets stored atomically with the data, and idempotent producers, and it holds only within a boundary.
- [[Delta Lake Transaction Log]] — Delta Lake keeps a write-ahead log of table membership as files in the object store itself, giving ACID tables over Parquet data with no server holding table state.
- [[Delta Method for Ratio Metrics]] — A ratio of two per-user totals, such as revenue per session, needs a standard error that accounts for numerator and denominator co-varying; computing it as if sessions were the independent units understates it by about 40 percent.
- [[Delta-Mem Fixed-Size State Memory]] — delta-mem keeps a frozen language model and adds a small 8x8 matrix state updated by a gated delta rule, whose read-out nudges attention; at 4.87M trainable parameters it raises a 5-benchmark average on Qwen3-4B from 46.79 to 51.66 (self-reported) with text-memory baselines falling below the backbone.
- [[DetectGPT]] — DetectGPT flags text sampled from a model by comparing its log probability with that of T5-perturbed rewrites; the normalised gap is larger for model samples, giving zero-shot detection with no training data or watermark (self-reported).
- [[Dialogue Transformers — TED Policy]] — Transformer Embedding Dialogue (TED) policy (Rasa, 2020) applies self-attention at the discourse level — over dialogue turns rather than tokens — outperforming LSTM-based policies on sub-dialogue handling while being simpler and faster than REDP.
- [[DIET Architecture]] — Dual Intent and Entity Transformer (Rasa, 2020) — a multi-task NLU architecture for joint intent classification and entity recognition that outperforms fine-tuned BERT while being 6x faster to train, using plug-and-play pre-trained embeddings with sparse features.
- [[Difference-in-Differences as Two-Way Fixed Effects]] — A difference-in-differences estimate equals the treated-by-post coefficient in a two-way fixed effects regression, and rests on untestable parallel trends that pre-period event-study checks detect poorly.
- [[Diffusion Model Training-Data Extraction]] — Carlini et al. show a black-box generate-and-filter attack that pulls memorized training images out of Stable Diffusion and Imagen, and a CIFAR-10 study finding diffusion models leak more training data than GANs (self-reported).
- [[Dilated Causal Convolutions]] — Convolutions with exponentially increasing dilation factors that preserve temporal causality while growing the receptive field exponentially with depth — the key architectural innovation in WaveNet for modeling long-range audio dependencies efficiently.
- [[Dimensional Model Grain and the Four Design Steps]] — A star schema is agreements about what one row means, what can be added up and what a label meant on the day: declare the grain first, keep an Unknown row so nothing drops, and store the parts of any ratio.
- [[Distributed Compute Cost and the Single-Machine Threshold]] — Choose a cluster only when one query's input after pruning exceeds what a large single machine can hold; DuckDB and Polars spill to disk and push down filters, and scaling curves can hide large overheads.
- [[Distribution Monitors Need an Effect Size and a Fixed Reference]] — Only a distribution monitor sees a silent unit change; threshold on an effect size such as the KS statistic rather than a p-value, keep an approved fixed reference, and start with prediction drift when labels arrive late.
- [[Distribution Selection for Metrics]] — Name the distribution behind a metric and you can usually name the test: Normal for measurements, Binomial for conversions, Poisson for counts, Exponential for gaps, lognormal for money, Beta for an uncertain rate.
- [[Divergences and Non-Centred Parameterisation]] — Hierarchical models create a funnel when the group scale is small, so NUTS diverges and underestimates the scale; sampling a standard normal and scaling it (non-centred form) changes the coordinates, not the model, and removes most divergences.
- [[Doc-to-LoRA]] — Doc-to-LoRA (D2L) is a hypernetwork that turns a document into a LoRA adapter in one forward pass, so later queries run without the document in context; it is trained to imitate context distillation and reached 82.5% of in-context quality on SQuAD with a 2B model (self-reported).
- [[DoRA Weight-Decomposed Low-Rank Adaptation]] — DoRA splits a pretrained weight into a magnitude vector and a direction, trains the magnitude directly and the direction with LoRA, and merges back with no inference overhead; commonsense-8 average on LLaMA-7B 74.7 to 78.4 over LoRA (self-reported).
- [[Dr. MAMR Lazy-Agent Multi-Agent RL]] — Dr. MAMR traces the collapse of trained two-agent reasoning pairs into one working agent to a turn-count normalization in multi-turn GRPO, and fixes it with a debiased loss, a causal-influence reward and a restart reward, scoring above single-agent GRPO on seven math benchmarks (self-reported).
- [[DreamGym Synthetic Experience RL]] — DreamGym replaces the real environment with an LLM experience model that predicts next states and rewards, plus a variance-based task curriculum; agents trained only on its output reached roughly the level of RL on 80K real transitions in two of three environments (self-reported).
- [[Drift Seen and Unseen in an Automated Router]] — With a frozen router, covariate drift trips a label-free alarm while the error stays fine, whereas prior and concept drift can leave the alarm green and double the error on automatically paid cases; recalibration fixes only part.
- [[Drift Tests: Significance, Magnitude and False Alarms]] — KS, chi-squared and PSI answer different questions: p-values go to zero on any shift at scale, PSI is noisy on small windows, and testing many features daily rings daily unless corrected; a domain classifier catches changes in feature relationships.
- [[Drift Triage: Broken Feeds, Benign Shifts and Concept Drift]] — Input drift can scream while error is flat, and concept drift can double the error while inputs and predictions look identical; triage an alert by checking the pipeline first, then measuring error on labels, and retrain only for real harm.
- [[Drift Types and What Monitors Can See]] — Covariate shift is loud and harmless, prior and concept shift are silent to input monitors, and feature skew can break a model while the score monitor barely moves; bin coded columns by level.
- [[DualDistill Agentic-R1]] — Distil two teachers, one that reasons in text and one that solves with Python code, into a single student by composing trajectories where one teacher's failure is followed by the other's correction; the 7B student chooses between code and text per problem on math.
- [[DuckDB Embedded Analytical Database]] — DuckDB runs analytical SQL inside the host process using a vectorized interpreted engine, compressed column blocks and serializable MVCC, built from existing techniques rather than new ones.
- [[EASE and Item-Based Neighbourhood Recommenders]] — EASE is a linear item-item model with a zero diagonal and a closed-form solution from one matrix inverse; with item kNN it led a small-catalogue ranking table, though the cubic inverse limits it to catalogues of tens of thousands.
- [[EDA Audit Loop]] — What to do with a new extract before any test or model: fix the row unit, hunt impossible and missing values, pick summaries the shape allows, and write the findings and open questions down.
- [[Efficient Attention Approximations]] — Three 2020-2021 routes to sub-quadratic attention, random-feature kernels (Performer), global plus window plus random sparsity (BigBird) and learned cluster routing (Routing Transformer), with their reported results and limits.
- [[EK-FAC Influence Functions for LLMs]] — EK-FAC approximates the inverse-curvature term in influence functions cheaply enough to trace a language model's outputs back to pretraining sequences at up to 52B parameters.
- [[Elastic Weight Consolidation]] — Elastic weight consolidation adds a quadratic penalty weighted by the diagonal Fisher information, anchoring parameters that mattered for an old task so a fixed-capacity network learns tasks in sequence.
- [[Embedding Clustering and Anomaly Detection]] — Sentence-embedding matrices cluster and flag outliers far better than TF-IDF, with k-means, UMAP plus HDBSCAN plus c-TF-IDF, and kNN or LOF detectors, but each result needs its setup and a manual look.
- [[Encoder Tokens, Position and Attention Scaling]] — Self-attention plus mean pooling sees a set, so position must be added; a learned table and sinusoids tie, and the choice of what a token is (a single pixel, a row, a patch) matters more: patches matched or beat single pixels at a fraction of the time.
- [[Encoding Compatibility and Schema Evolution]] — Data outlives code, so a format must be both backward and forward compatible; text formats silently destroy types, and schema-carrying binary formats (Avro, Protobuf) make evolution a checked property, with silent drift across files as the remaining trap.
- [[End-to-End Memory Networks]] — A softmax-attention form of the Memory Network that stacks several read hops over embedded sentences and trains from question-answer pairs alone; on bAbI it came close to a supervised memory network and on language modeling it edged out LSTM baselines (self-reported).
- [[Entity-Disjoint Splits and Group Scores for Pair Matchers]] — Because match labels are transitive, a random pair split leaks the answer: close the labels into groups, hold out whole groups, block inside each side, and tune on group F1 when merging, since closure chains pair errors.
- [[Entropy Balancing]] — Entropy balancing finds control weights closest to uniform whose covariate moments equal the treated group's exactly, skipping the propensity model; limited overlap shows up as extreme weights, so check effective sample size alongside balance.
- [[Erasure on Immutable Storage: Vacuum, Crypto-Shredding and the Ledger]] — Deleting one person from immutable files needs a file rewrite, a vacuum in table formats, a ledger replayed on restore or crypto-shredding, and a coverage check across every store.
- [[ERNIE Knowledge-Enhanced Language Representation]] — ERNIE (Zhang et al. 2019) stacks a knowledge encoder over a BERT-style text encoder to fuse knowledge-graph entity embeddings into token representations during pre-training; it improved entity typing and relation classification over BERT and was comparable on GLUE (self-reported).
- [[Error Analysis by Confusion-Matrix Cell]] — Read a model's errors in order: name the positive class, set the table at the decision threshold, compare the four cells, read random rows before confident ones, then diagnose before touching the model.
- [[Error Analysis, Bias and Variance Gaps]] — Diagnose which gap to attack first: human level to train error (avoidable bias), train to train-dev (variance), train-dev to dev (data mismatch), dev to test (overfit to dev); count an error category before building a fix, since its share of mistakes is the ceiling on the gain.
- [[Error-Stream Detectors and Retraining Policy]] — When labels arrive, Page-Hinkley or CUSUM on the error stream trades detection delay against false alarms with no free setting; label delay decides whether this is possible, and scheduled retraining is the defensible default over triggers.
- [[Event Time, Watermarks and Late Data]] — Windows must be defined on event time, but a stream never says a window is complete; a watermark is a heuristic that fires windows, and allowed lateness trades lost late events against stale results.
- [[Expand, Migrate, Contract]] — A rename, split or type change cannot be one statement because old and new code run together during a rollout; add the new shape, dual write, backfill, verify, move readers, and drop the old shape last, since contract is the one step with no rollback.
- [[Expectation and Variance Rules]] — Expectations always add, even for dependent variables, while variances add only for uncorrelated ones; indicator variables turn counts into sums, and the law of total variance explains why knowing a group removes noise.
- [[Expectation-Maximisation for Gaussian Mixtures]] — EM fits a mixture by alternating soft cluster assignments (E-step) with weighted parameter refits (M-step); the likelihood never falls but only reaches a local peak, so restart several times and keep the best, and remember it gives a point estimate with no parameter uncertainty.
- [[Experiment Ramps and Overlapping Tests]] — A platform assigns users with a salted cryptographic hash per experiment layer so many tests overlap independently, ramps traffic from tiny shares because a catastrophe needs far less data than a planned effect, and audits the portfolio because winners' summed lifts overstate what shipped.
- [[Explain vs Predict Modelling]] — Explanation wants low bias on a coefficient and prediction wants low total error, so a wrong simple model can win held-out error at small n while the true form wins at large n; say which goal comes first.
- [[Explanatory Data Story and the Big Idea]] — Fix audience, ask and one-sentence point of view before opening a slide tool, size the decision with named assumptions and a break-even, and test the story with horizontal logic.
- [[External Validity and Transportability]] — Randomisation buys internal validity only: whether an effect carries to another population depends on how the sample was selected, and reweighting fixes selection on measured variables but never on an unmeasured one.
- [[Eyeball and Blackbox Sets and Fixes That Fit the Reading]] — Reading errors overfits the rows read: keep a read set apart from a score set, fix classes not rows, count rounds, and expect even a well-argued mechanism fix to vanish on unread data.
- [[Fast-Slow Thinking Architecture]] — Three ways to give a real-time agent deep reasoning without blocking it — fast fillers then slow answers, a fast interaction model advised by a background reasoner (the 2026 mainstream), or one model that thinks while speaking — plus the text-vs-latent channel between them and the rule that the fast side never claims 'done' before the slow side confirms.
- [[Faults, Load Parameters and Systems of Record]] — Design vocabulary for data systems: faults versus failures, why correlated software and human faults beat redundancy, choosing load parameters from the distribution not the mean, and separating systems of record from rebuildable derived data.
- [[Feature Representation for Linear Models]] — Binning, interactions, polynomials, log transforms, selection and domain features give linear models shape; trees gain little from them.
- [[Feature Stores: Parts and When You Need One]] — A feature store is a point-in-time join with a registry, an offline history store, an online latest-value store and a materialisation job around it; most teams with one model need only the join and a shared function.
- [[Fencing Tokens and Zombie Writers]] — A paused or deposed worker still believes it holds a lock; majority quorums settle truth inside a cluster, and a monotonically increasing fencing token checked at the resource stops stale writers, which is how zombie task attempts are neutralised.
- [[Flashcard Schedulers: Leitner, SM-2 and FSRS]] — Leitner boxes and SM-2 need no review history, while FSRS fits per-card memory parameters and beats half-life regression at predicting recall on its own project's benchmark; prediction is not learning, and no controlled comparison of learning outcomes was found, so start simple.
- [[Flipped-Label In-Context Learning]] — Experiments flipping or replacing in-context labels show that overriding semantic priors and learning arbitrary input-label mappings both emerge with model scale, and that instruction tuning strengthens priors.
- [[Focused Transformer Crossbatch Training]] — Focused Transformer trains memory attention with keys from other documents as negatives (crossbatch training) to counter the distraction issue; LongLLaMA 3B reached 94.5% passkey accuracy at 100K tokens (self-reported).
- [[Forecast Baselines and Rolling-Origin Backtest]] — A forecasting model earns trust only by beating free baselines (seasonal naive above all) across many rolling origins, scored with a scaled error plus bias, with the final origin held back as an untouched exam.
- [[Forecast Issue Time and Measured Leakage]] — Fix the issue time, horizon, information set and loss before fitting; every feature lag must be at least the horizon, and three common leaks were measured on one hourly series.
- [[Foundation Models as Decision Makers (MDP View)]] — A 2023 survey frames dialogue, prompting and tool use by a foundation model as sequential decision problems in a Markov decision process, with the model as policy and tools, humans or the world as environment; it reports no experiments, only framing and cited numbers.
- [[Four Levels of AI Design Complexity]] — A CHI 2020 framework says AI is hard to design for because of capability uncertainty and output complexity, and sorts systems into four levels by how many of the design challenges they bring; rule-based simulators prototype levels one and two but not three and four.
- [[FP8-LM Mixed-Precision FP8 Training]] — FP8-LM extends FP8 beyond matrix multiplies to gradients, optimizer states and distributed communication in three add-on levels; the paper reports GPT-175B training 75% faster than BF16 Megatron-LM with 39% less memory and matching loss curves.
- [[Freshness Alerts Need a Date Spine]] — An age check on max(loaded_at) clears the moment a newer partition lands, with the hole still behind it; pairing it with a list of expected dates keeps firing until the gap is repaired.
- [[FrogNano Online Task Synthesis]] — Train a 4B coding agent with RL on synthetic tasks regenerated each iteration to match the current policy's solve rate (TaskPilot); five iterations of 300 tasks took SWE-bench Verified from 39.4% to 61.5% with no distillation of trajectories.
- [[Frozen vs Fine-Tuned Sentence Encoders]] — A pre-trained encoder is not one result: a small frozen encoder lost to TF-IDF at every label count, a larger one won with few labels, and fine-tuning DistilBERT first clearly beat word counts only at 5,000 labels (0.905 vs 0.886); measure on your task and keep TF-IDF as the bar.
- [[Functional Form Misspecification]] — A linear control for a hump-shaped confounder biases the treatment effect threefold with zero coverage, flexible forms fix it, and a quadratic whose peak looks significant can be an artifact of a curve that never turns.
- [[Funnel Step Diagnosis]] — A flat end-to-end conversion can hide a broken step behind an unrelated gain; split into conditional step rates by device, use an unaffected segment as a difference-in-differences control, rule out tracking bugs, then price the loss and size the test the step's own traffic allows.
- [[Gaussian Processes and Bayesian Optimisation]] — A Gaussian process is a prior over functions that gives a prediction with an honest error bar; Bayesian optimisation uses it as a cheap surrogate and an acquisition function to pick the next expensive evaluation, but beats random search only when each fit is costly.
- [[Generalised Linear Models and Deviance]] — A GLM pairs a distribution with a link function and is fitted by maximum likelihood; deviance replaces the sum of squares for comparing models, while Pearson dispersion is the better check of the variance assumption.
- [[Generative Story Models]] — Build a Bayesian model by writing how the data could have been generated, top down, and translating each line into a distribution; a known measurement process (a changepoint, randomised response, serial numbers) goes into the model, not into a correction afterwards.
- [[Gist Tokens]] — Gist tokens compress a prompt into a few learned tokens by changing only the attention mask during instruction finetuning, so later tokens see just the gist; up to 26x compression with little quality loss on short instructions (self-reported).
- [[Git Branch Triage]] — Deciding what to do with in-flight work before switching context — the branch health check that separates merged from unmerged commits, the WIP-branch-versus-stash choice, and reading a stash diff before trusting it.
- [[Global vs Per-Segment Forecast Models]] — Start with one global model plus the naive row, add the segment as a feature before splitting the data, and split only where later folds confirm it; one eight-fold table gave the wrong answer on synthetic cash-flow data.
- [[GLTR]] — GLTR scores each token's probability, rank and predictive entropy under a detection language model and colours the text by rank bucket; in a 35-student study the overlay raised human detection of fake text from 54% to 72% (self-reported).
- [[Gower Distance, PAM and k-prototypes]] — Gower handles missing values but equal weights give categories about three times the say of numbers; PAM needs an n-by-n matrix, and k-prototypes' gamma has no value that suits every world.
- [[Gradient Boosting Mechanics and Library Choice]] — Boosting fits shallow trees to loss gradients; the main libraries tie on accuracy within seed noise, so choose by speed, categorical and missing-value handling, and tune rounds by early stopping.
- [[Gradient Checking with Finite Differences]] — A centred finite difference gives a slow but independent estimate of every gradient; the relative difference to backprop separates a correct implementation (about 9e-11) from a planted 10% error (about 0.05), and the check is switched off in training.
- [[Gradient-Boosted Forecasting Relative to a Known Level]] — A regression tree cannot predict above anything it has seen, so divide target and lags by a level known at issue time; on a short single series this beat LSTM, TCN and DLinear, and quantile models give intervals whose coverage must be checked per fold.
- [[Grammar of Graphics]] — A chart is a composition of independent decisions (data, mark, encoding, statistic, scale, coordinates, facet), so chart choice becomes "which encoding makes my comparison easiest to read".
- [[Green-List LLM Watermark]] — Kirchenbauer et al.'s watermark adds a bias to a pseudo-random green subset of tokens at each sampling step and detects it with a z-test on the green-token count, without model access; on OPT-1.3B it flagged 98.4% of generations at z=4 (self-reported).
- [[Group Fairness Criteria and the Impossibility Results]] — Demographic parity, equal opportunity, equalized odds, calibration and predictive parity ask different questions; with unequal base rates and an imperfect score they cannot all hold, so someone must choose and own the metric.
- [[Groupby Silent Group Loss and Pivot Defaults]] — Groupby drops NaN keys, hides empty categories or averages by default without a warning, so compare the sum of groups with the overall total and state every default.
- [[Groupby Split-Apply-Combine and Result Shapes]] — groupby splits a table, applies a function per group and recombines; agg, transform, filter and apply differ by the shape they return, and picking one starts from what a row of output should mean.
- [[GUI Agent RL with GUI-R1 and ARPO]] — Two GRPO recipes for GUI agents: GUI-R1 uses rule-based rewards on 3K filtered examples for action prediction, while ARPO adds a replay buffer of successful trajectories so groups of all failures still carry signal; both report gains with large caveats in their own text.
- [[Hard-Negative Mining and Measuring What It Fixes]] — Mining hard negatives did not raise recall on a tuned matching encoder but cut look-alikes in the top 10 from 0.494 to 0.363 on every seed; judge mining on what the negatives were meant to fix, keep in-batch negatives, and read a sample of what you mined for false negatives.
- [[Harmful Fine-Tuning of Aligned Models]] — Qi et al. find that fine-tuning an aligned model on 10 to 100 harmful or identity-shifting examples, or even on benign data, raises its harmfulness rate (GPT-3.5 Turbo from 1.8% to 91.8% at 100 shots), and test mixed-in safety data as a mitigation (self-reported).
- [[HDBSCAN with KMeans Fallback]] — Clustering selection strategy that tries density-based HDBSCAN first and falls back to KMeans when silhouette drops below 0.25 — plus the diagnostic discipline that treats a fallback as a feature-quality signal rather than a resolved choice.
- [[Hierarchical Story Generation]] — Generate a short premise first, then condition a convolutional seq2seq story model on it, using gated multi-scale decoder self-attention and fusion with a pretrained model; introduces the WritingPrompts dataset and top-k sampling for decoding.
- [[High-Watermark Incremental Extraction and Its Blind Spots]] — Reading rows newer than the last seen updated_at misses late-committed rows and every hard delete; an overlap window patches the first, nothing patches the second, and dlt's incremental cursor is the same mechanism.
- [[How Far Plain Postgres Stretches]] — A single relational server emulates key-value, document, search, time-series, graph and small-scale vector workloads at levels most applications never exceed; it stops being enough when writes exceed one primary, when scanning most of a huge table, for multi-region writes, search as a product, or blobs.
- [[Hyperparameter Search Budgets and Selection Optimism]] — Random, grid and TPE searches tie when the plateau is wide; the winner's score is optimistic by an amount set by how few rows chose it, so report on untouched rows or nested folds.
- [[Iceberg Metadata Tree and Hidden Partitioning]] — Iceberg stores a tree of metadata, manifest lists and manifests under an atomic catalog pointer, which enables planning-time pruning, hidden partitioning, partition evolution and schema tracking by field ID.
- [[Idempotent Backfills and Dated Corrections]] — A repair job that replaces one logical day per transaction is safe to run twice, corrections live in a dated table above untouched raw data, and a late partition needs a trigger: rebuild any mart row older than its input's newest load.
- [[Idempotent Batch Loads and Partition Overwrite]] — A task is idempotent when rerunning it for the same period leaves the same state as running it once; overwrite the period in one transaction by default, merge only where no period owns the row, and keep tasks pure and side effects keyed.
- [[Idempotent Retries and Request IDs]] — Because a timeout hides whether a write applied, retries are needed and duplicate effects follow; a request ID stored in the same transaction as the write makes a retry harmless, which is what most 'exactly-once' means.
- [[Imbalance Remedies Versus a Tuned Threshold]] — On a 3% default book no resampling remedy beat leaving the data alone and setting the threshold from the cost ratio, and every balancing method wrecked the probabilities until recalibrated.
- [[Implicit Feedback: iALS and BPR]] — With clicks and plays there is no negative signal and counts measure confidence, not strength; iALS weights every cell in closed form and BPR optimises pairwise order, and a published alpha of 40 was the worst setting on other data.
- [[Implicit Weight-Patch Theory of In-Context Learning]] — Dherin et al. show algebraically that the effect of a prompt on a transformer block equals a rank-1 patch of the first MLP weight matrix, plus a bias patch with skip connections; the equivalence is exact but shown only on toy models.
- [[Incrementality Measurement Ladder]] — Rank marketing evidence by what it can claim: attribution shares credit, a geo test measures the average return over a tested spend change, and a media mix model with adstock and saturation estimates marginal returns, which budgets move on.
- [[Independence Pitfalls]] — Pairwise independence does not give mutual independence, and two correlated events can be independent given a third; checking variables two at a time cannot prove there is no joint structure, and a shared cause creates correlation that vanishes when conditioned on.
- [[Infrastructure as Code Plans: Drift, Replacement and State]] — A plan loop of write config, plan, apply after approval exposes three traps: console edits show up as drift and get reverted, some edits force a destroy and re-create, and the state file must be stored remotely with locking.
- [[Inspection Paradox in Metrics]] — A metric sampled one way and quoted another differs: sampling by member weights each group by its size, so per-session averages exceed per-user averages and the two can move in opposite directions.
- [[Instrumental Variables and LATE]] — An instrument moves treatment but affects the outcome only through it, so the Wald ratio of the instrument's effect on outcome over its effect on treatment recovers the effect for compliers despite unmeasured confounding; weak first stages and an untestable exclusion restriction are the failure modes.
- [[Interactive Charts and When They Pay]] — Interaction moves work from author to reader; use it for exploration and monitoring, state the finding yourself for decisions, and know the shipping costs of plotly and Altair.
- [[Interference and Cluster-Randomised Experiments]] — When one user's arm changes another's outcome, as with fixed stock in a marketplace, a user-level A/B test overstates the effect (+30% measured against +19% real), so randomise by cluster, switchback or item and pay the design effect 1+(m-1)rho in variance.
- [[Intrinsic Dimension of Objective Landscapes]] — Training only a d-dimensional random projection of a network's parameters and finding the smallest d that reaches 90% of normal performance (d_int90) gave about 750 for an MNIST fully connected net of 199,210 parameters, nearly constant across model sizes (self-reported).
- [[Inverse Scaling in Test-Time Compute]] — Gema et al. find tasks where giving a reasoning model a longer budget lowers accuracy, with distractor, spurious-feature and constraint-tracking failure modes, so test-time compute is not monotone.
- [[Isolation Forest vs Gaussian Anomaly Detection]] — A per-feature Gaussian scores how likely a point is, an isolation forest how easy it is to cut off; pick by the shape of normal data, and irrelevant columns wreck the forest.
- [[JavaScript Charting Ladder]] — D3 (elements and geometry, data join), Vega-Lite (JSON grammar compiled to Vega) and Observable Plot (marks and options) trade control against code volume; default to the highest rung that works.
- [[K-Anonymity, Quasi-Identifiers and Linkage Attacks]] — Removing names does not anonymise a table: combinations of quasi-identifiers single people out, k-anonymity measures it, and l-diversity covers the inference it misses.
- [[Kafka Ingest Model, Delivery Semantics and Stream Windows]] — Topics, partitions, keys, offsets and consumer groups define what ordering and replay a stream gives you; auto-commit is at-most-once, a schema registry turns silent schema change into a failed deploy, and four window types cover most stream aggregation.
- [[Kafka Log, Partitions and Offsets]] — A log keeps every record and lets each reader remember its own offset, which gives replay, multiple independent consumers, per-partition ordering and a lag metric that tells you whether a consumer is healthy.
- [[KARL Multi-Task RL Knowledge Agents]] — KARL trains a search agent with iterative large-batch off-policy RL (OAPL) on two tasks, using agent-synthesised questions and a learned compression tool; the result matched a frontier model on a six-task suite at lower cost (self-reported) and generalised better than SFT distillation.
- [[Kernel SVM and the Kernel Trick]] — A kernel SVM is a linear model in an implicit richer feature space; C and gamma must be tuned together and features must be scaled.
- [[KnowNo Conformal Ask-for-Help]] — KnowNo has an LLM planner propose multiple-choice options, turns next-token probabilities into conformal prediction scores, and asks a human when the set is not a single option; with 400 calibration examples it gives a task-success guarantee, and cuts help by 10-24% in simulation (self-reported).
- [[Label Bias, Proxies and the Cost of Mitigation]] — When the label is a past human decision every label-based metric inherits its bias and calibration can look clean; dropping the sensitive column leaves proxies, and mitigation costs depend on which label you price them with.
- [[Label Horizon vs Sampling Window]] — label definition, label horizon, and sampling window are distinct; a fraud frame's two-day Time span is not a horizon, and applying a horizon standard to one run but not its sibling is the finding
- [[Label Quality: Agreement, Noise and Confident Learning]] — Kappa measures agreement, not correctness, and is unstable on rare classes; label noise hurts probabilities more than ranking unless it depends on the row; confident-learning flags are a review list, not a cleaning rule.
- [[Lakehouse vs Warehouse Decision]] — A lakehouse puts a transactional metadata layer over open files to avoid the two-tier lake-plus-warehouse copy; what you gain is one copy and many engines, what you give up is data independence, multi-table transactions and some native performance.
- [[LaMer Meta-RL Exploration]] — LaMer trains a language agent on trials of several sequential episodes with a reflection written after each failure, optimising a cross-episode return; pass@3 beat GiGPO on Sokoban, MineSweeper and Webshop (self-reported) while first-attempt success was mixed.
- [[Lamport Timestamps and Total Order Broadcast]] — A counter-plus-node-ID gives a total order consistent with causality but only after the fact; total order broadcast, in practice a replicated log, fixes the order at delivery and so can implement uniqueness checks and compare-and-set.
- [[Laplace Mechanism and Sensitivity-Calibrated Noise]] — Dwork, McSherry, Nissim and coauthors prove that Laplace noise with scale equal to a query's L1 sensitivity over epsilon keeps answers epsilon-indistinguishable, even for adaptive query sequences, and that non-interactive mechanisms need exponentially large databases.
- [[Latency Percentiles, Tail Amplification and Coordinated Omission]] — Response time is a distribution: report percentiles, merge histograms rather than averaging percentiles, expect the tail to compound across backend calls, and measure with an open-loop load generator.
- [[Latent Class Analysis for Mixed Data]] — Latent class models each group as a recipe of per-column distributions fit by EM; BIC can answer no groups, but correlated columns inside a class break local independence and inflate the class count.
- [[Layer-Condensed KV Cache]] — The Layer-Condensed KV Cache pairs every layer's queries with the keys and values of only the top layer, so one layer's KV is cached; it reports up to 26x throughput against a standard Transformer but costs about 3x pretraining time and was checked for quality only at 1.1B (self-reported).
- [[Learning Curves and Bias-Variance Diagnosis]] — Plot train and validation error against training size to tell high bias (more data will not help) from high variance (it will).
- [[Learning From Corrections and the Audit Sample]] — A review queue only labels what the model chose to show, so silence is not confirmation and correction rate cannot separate a good model from a tired reviewer; measure with a random audit and retrain from audited labels.
- [[Learning to Rank: Pointwise, Pairwise, Listwise and LambdaMART]] — Learning to rank trains on one item, one pair or one whole list; NDCG is flat or discontinuous so LambdaMART defines gradients (RankNet's times |delta NDCG|) instead of a loss, yet on a matching task moving to pairs bought most of the gain and the NDCG factor little.
- [[Lineage for Blast Radius and Rebuild Lists]] — Lineage is a graph of which job read and wrote which dataset; walking it downstream gives the blast radius of a bad table, upstream gives suspects, and joined to run history it gives the exact runs to redo.
- [[Linear Text Classifiers and the TF-IDF Baseline]] — TF-IDF plus a regularised linear model inside a pipeline is the text-classification baseline; Naive Bayes, trees and averaged word vectors compared, with coefficient reading as a leakage check.
- [[Linearizability, Ordering and Quorum Reads]] — Linearizability is a recency guarantee on one object, distinct from serializability; strict quorums with w + r > n do not deliver it, CAP is better read as consistent-or-available under partition, and causality gives only a partial order.
- [[LLM Prompting for Mobile UI Screens]] — A 2022 paper converts an Android view hierarchy to HTML and prompts PaLM with at most two exemplars for four screen-language tasks; one example in the prompt delivers most of the improvement, and results are rater-study and benchmark numbers with no agent loop (self-reported).
- [[LLM Reasoning Surveys and the Can-LLMs-Reason Debate]] — Four sources that map or question LLM reasoning without new benchmark runs of their own: a maths-reasoning survey, a regime-by-architecture survey, a prompting/architecture/learning survey, and Kambhampati's argument that LLMs retrieve approximately rather than plan.
- [[LM2 Large Memory Models]] — LM2 adds a gated cross-attention memory bank to every decoder block of a transformer; at about 1.7B parameters it beats a recurrent-memory baseline and a same-size vanilla model on BABILong, but the gains are concentrated in a few task types and the memory lives only within one context (self-reported).
- [[Lock Queue and lock_timeout for Live DDL]] — A one-millisecond ALTER can stall every read because it queues for an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock behind a long reader while new queries queue behind it; a per-session lock_timeout with retry turns the stall into a short delay.
- [[Logistic Regression Odds Ratios vs Marginal Effects]] — A logistic coefficient is a change in log-odds; exponentiating gives an odds ratio, but the change in probability depends on the starting rate, so report average marginal effects for business impact.
- [[Logistic Regression Separation]] — When a feature perfectly predicts the outcome, maximum likelihood diverges, giving a huge coefficient and an absurd standard error; a penalty or weakly informative prior stabilises it.
- [[LongMem Side-Network Memory]] — LongMem caches key-value pairs from a frozen backbone layer into a 65k FIFO memory bank and reads them through a trainable SideNet with gated joint attention; it reached 40.5% on ChapterBreak AO3 at an 8k prefix (self-reported).
- [[LSTM Forget-Gate Bias and Vanishing Gradients]] — Default-initialised LSTMs pass a gradient of about 10^-12 from the first step, no better than a plain RNN; setting the forget-gate bias to 1 or 2 (mind PyTorch's two bias vectors) restores long memory, and gradient clipping handles the opposite failure.
- [[MAKER Million-Step Decomposition]] — MAKER solved a 20-disk Towers of Hanoi (over a million steps) with gpt-4.1-mini by decomposing into one-step agents, voting first-to-ahead-by-k per step and discarding malformed answers, with a theoretical cost of order s ln s (self-reported).
- [[MapReduce, the Shuffle and Spark Lineage]] — MapReduce splits a job into map, a hash-partitioned sort-and-copy shuffle and reduce, tolerating failure through immutable inputs and atomic output; Spark keeps the shape but recovers by recomputing partitions from lineage.
- [[Markov Chain Customer Value]] — A monthly state-transition table gives customer lifetime value in one linear solve, but a single pooled chain averages unlike customers and undervalues them (about 19% low in simulation); check the chain on a holdout and use a mixture of chains.
- [[Match Errors Are Not Classification Errors]] — Score a matcher on whole lines, not pairs: 99.5% pair accuracy hid 27% wrong lines; a wrong link makes two errors, a one-to-one constraint hurt part payments, a subset-sum search fixes batches, and the margin over the second candidate beats raw score for abstaining.
- [[Matching and Weighting Estimators]] — Exact matching, coarsened exact matching, propensity matching, propensity weighting, entropy balancing and regression adjustment answer different questions (ATE, ATT, local ATT) and all landed 3 to 4 pounds above the truth because of an unlogged confounder; report who was kept and judge designs on balance before outcomes.
- [[Materialise or Query and Cost Tests]] — Decide whether to store a daily aggregate by comparing rebuild cost to scans saved at the real read rate, and stop cost regressions with dry-run byte budgets and per-job attribution instead of finding them on the invoice.
