- [[A Clean Contract Review Is Only as Wide as the Contract]] — A contracts review dimension that returns `[]` has only shown that no *written* rule was broken — when the contract declares no budget, scopes an invariant to two paths, or never names a rule that lives elsewhere, a clean verdict is an absent control, not a satisfied one, and a repeated clean-for-that-reason is a signal to amend the contract.
- [[HITL Gate Without a Resumer]] — interrupt() gates collapse to auto-escalate when nobody resumes; sequential gates compound; calibrate thresholds to a target escalation rate
- [[Deterministic Scrub Before LLM Review]] — regex scrub first, LLM review only on placeholder output (local/DPA-covered model), grep gate before commit
- [[Stub Backend Is Permanent Dev Infrastructure]] — tool interface is the dev/prod seam; the MCP stub is never replaced, production is a second implementation
- [[Green Lint Proves Absence, Not That the Rule Fires]] — A custom lint rule checked only by hand proves nothing once green; ESLint flat config replaces `no-restricted-syntax` across overlapping globs, so test each rule via `lintText` with a virtual file path
- [[Orchestrator Verifies, Never Relays]] — In multi-agent review/build loops the orchestrator checks agent claims against the repo (git index, merge-base, base branch, mutation of the fix) before acting on them
- [[Guard the Source, Not the Destination]] — an empty-input guard on the destination goes dead after the first run; check the source and pin both stall and idle-week cases
- [[Defined but Never Wired]] — before blaming the model or ranker, trace the prompt/extension from definition to the literal call argument
- [[Demote the Auto-Writer Before a Semantic Change]] — make the downstream writer suggest-only first so a regression lands in a reviewable file, and gate the change on a measured blast radius
- [[Signal Without a Consumer]] — A schema field, lint flag or relationship type that is defined and even emitted, but that no protocol step or tool ever acts on, is a dead letter. Before adding new machinery, inventory the signals the system already produces and give the unconsumed ones a reader.
- [[Freshness Key Blind to Content Semantics]] — A persisted index whose freshness check keys only on schema version and source mtimes never notices a code change to what gets stored, or a runtime flag that changes it. Rebuilding is skipped and queries quietly run against a degraded index. Bump the schema version whenever stored semantics change, and never let a rebuild fire while a degrading flag is set.
- [[Gated Weighted Composite Score]] — weighted points total plus one hard cap from the single non-averageable dimension, an independent mandatory-flag rule, and remediation-only scoping when two critical gaps coexist
- [[Server-Derived Autonomy Tier]] — HITL tier computed server-side in one roster-wide switch, drafts written only via a DB function that also creates the pending approval, so no agent can grant itself autonomy
- [[Type Home Decided by Importers]] — a type's home is decided by who imports it: wire-crossing shapes in shared types, single-module shapes stay put, schema-backed shapes inferred never hand-written
- [[Demote Judge Findings to Keyless Gates]] — When an LLM judge finds a defect that a regex or derivation check could catch, turn it into a keyless heuristic grader gated at 1.0 on the deterministic path; judges then measure only the model lane and prose quality, never the regression class itself.
- [[Judge Sees the Drafter's Facts]] — Give a groundedness judge the same derived facts the drafting agent was given, as reference facts the output must not contradict; otherwise the judge fails correct output for restating a given score or status as if it were invented.
- [[Computed-Correctness Lesson Loop]] — Close an agent's learning loop by recording what an upstream agent predicted next to what actually happened downstream, with correctness as a generated column (computed, never judged), and make human promotion through one RPC the only path by which a lesson becomes visible to the agent that consumes it.
- [[Single-Writer Transition Command]] — Make one SECURITY DEFINER function, called with the caller's JWT, the only writer of a lifecycle: it derives the current stage, authorises, checks guards and writes event, audit and approval rows atomically; ship it additive-first and revoke direct writes in a second PR, naming the interim hole until then.
- [[Pin Client-Executable Functions With an Allow-List]] — Postgres functions are PUBLIC-executable by default, so assert the exact set of functions anon and authenticated can execute (an allow-list via routine_privileges and results_eq), not \"nothing dangerous\"; trigger-only functions can be revoked because EXECUTE is checked only at create-trigger time.
- [[Keyless Injection Regression Suite]] — Pin prompt-injection defences with three keyless tests: adversarial twin fixtures whose output must equal the clean twin's, schema-omission tests proving model output cannot set privileged fields, and prompt-builder tests proving partner text lands inside a labelled delimiter block.
- [[Advisory Enrichment Breaks Ties Only]] — Server-side lookups against an external registry should be advisory: never fail or delay the request, keep a tri-state (undefined = not attempted, null = no match) plus a stored lookup status, act on the decision only as a tiebreak when user-stated facts are silent, and surface everything else as reviewer flags.
- [[Fix the Generator, Not the Generated Record]] — When review finds gaps in a skill-generated design document, sort findings by where the fix belongs (in the specs but not surfaced = generator bug; tracked; genuinely missing; endorsements), put content in the specs and rules in the skill's template, and validate the skill's checklist by showing it reads zero on a failed output and passes on a reference.
- [[Embeddings That Only Rerank Cannot Recall]] — BM25-only candidate pool makes embeddings rerank-only; RRF alone cannot lift recall
- [[Closed-Vocabulary Parser Silently Drops Invented Types]] — closed type regex silently dropped 481 invented link annotations
- [[Native Library Load-Order Collision]] — LightGBM/torch vendor different libomp builds; first loaded wins, segfault without traceback
- [[Cited-Minus-Read Grounding Check]] — Make a chat agent's grounding checkable: cite pages with a marker that cannot collide with page content, then compute ungrounded = cited − pages successfully read in this answer, emit it as its own event, and never let a cited id drive UI highlighting.
- [[File-Disjoint Agent Waves in One Working Tree]] — Run parallel subagents in one working tree by giving each an exclusive file list per wave, fixing shared interface contracts in a serial Wave 0, adding only optional props, and reserving integration and cross-cutting styling for later single-owner waves.
- [[Render-Scoped Component Types Remount Their Subtree]] — A React component type created inside a render (e.g. a markdown renderer's components map) is a new type every render, so any parent re-render unmounts and remounts the whole subtree, silently detaching observers; hoist the types and memoise the map.
- [[Index Pin Applies Only to Direct Dependencies]] — uv's [tool.uv.sources] index pin reaches only packages the project lists directly, so a CPU-only torch pin is ignored when torch arrives transitively and Linux/Docker builds pull multi-GB CUDA wheels; list the package explicitly in the extra that needs it.
- [[Review Cursor Advances Only Past a Clean Run]] — A scheduled review of what changed since last time keeps a per-repo cursor that moves only after a run completes cleanly, so a failed or partial run re-covers the same range next time instead of silently skipping it.
- [[Per-Column PII Verdicts: Keep, Remove, Move, Change]] — Take PII out of an app schema by classifying every column (Yes / Maybe / ID / No) and giving each one of four verdicts — keep, remove, move to the system of record, or change its shape — with emails replaced by actor ids plus a staff-only view, and free text kept under a retention rule.

## Prompting

- [[Aggregation of Reasoning (AoR)]] — Replaces majority voting over sampled chains of thought with an LLM evaluation of the chains themselves, scored locally within answer buckets and globally across buckets, with dynamic sampling.
- [[Algorithm of Thoughts Plus (AoT+) State Memoization]] — AoT+ adds periodic restatement of the current state to a single-prompt search trace; on PDDL planning benchmarks its ablation credits the restatement, not the hand-written heuristics, and it matches or beats an external-verifier loop at about a third of the tokens (self-reported).
- [[Analogical Prompting]] — One prompt asks the model to recall relevant example problems (optionally with a tutorial) and then solve the target problem, replacing hand-labelled few-shot exemplars; helps larger models, hurts small ones.
- [[ATF Analysis-to-Filtration Prompting]] — ATF has the model list the irrelevant clauses in a problem, rewrite the question without them, then reason on the filtered text; on GSMIR (GSM8K plus one irrelevant sentence) it lifted chain-of-thought accuracy on GPT-3.5-Turbo from 50.2 to 74.9 (self-reported).
- [[Branch-Solve-Merge]] — A three-prompt program: the model plans parallel sub-tasks, solves each independently, then merges; on MT-Bench writing pairs with LLaMA-2-70B-chat it lifted judge-human agreement from 0.43 to 0.55 (self-reported).
- [[Buffer of Thoughts]] — Buffer of Thoughts distils each problem into key information and retrieves a stored thought-template from a growing meta-buffer, so one query instantiates a template instead of searching; it reports gains on 10 GPT-4 tasks at about 12% of multi-query cost, measured as time (self-reported).
- [[Chain of Code]] — The model writes reasoning as code; a Python interpreter runs each line and an LM emulator fills in lines that fail to execute (semantic helpers), giving 84 on BIG-Bench Hard against 72 for CoT in the paper (self-reported, text-davinci-003).
- [[Chain of Draft]] — A prompt that asks for a terse draft of each reasoning step, about five words, instead of full chain-of-thought prose; on four easy tasks it kept most of the accuracy at a fraction of the tokens, but the limit is not enforced and zero-shot use degraded.
- [[Chain-of-Table]] — Table question answering where the LLM repeatedly picks one of five table operations, the operation is executed programmatically, and the transformed table is fed back, so the reasoning lives in the evolving table.
- [[Chain-of-Thought Prompting]] — Few-shot exemplars that include written-out intermediate reasoning steps; a prompting technique with scale-dependent gains and a contested account of why it works.
- [[Chain-of-Thought Unfaithfulness Under Biased Context]] — Turpin et al. add biasing features to prompts (always-A answers, a suggested answer, weak stereotype evidence) and find chain-of-thought explanations almost never mention the bias while accuracy falls by up to 36 points on GPT-3.5; the tests can show unfaithfulness but cannot prove faithfulness (self-reported).
- [[Chain-of-Verification]] — A four-step prompting method (draft, plan verification questions, answer them without the draft in view, write a revised answer) that raised Llama 65B's FActScore on biographies from 55.9 to 71.4 with the factor-and-revise variant (self-reported), at the cost of fewer facts.
- [[Coding-Agent Skill Distillation (CASD)]] — CASD hands an unmodified coding agent a static corpus of agent trajectories and lets it analyse the whole corpus with code, then write a skill file used as the system prompt; the paper reports it beating GEPA on three of four agentic benchmarks at lower cost (self-reported).
- [[Complexity-Based Prompting]] — Choose the chain-of-thought exemplars with the most reasoning steps, and vote only among the longest sampled chains; helps large models on math, little on small ones.
- [[Context-Faithful Prompting]] — Framing the context as a narrator's statement and asking for the narrator's opinion, plus counterfactual demonstrations, makes a model follow supplied text over memorised facts and say it does not know when the text lacks the answer.
- [[Contrastive Chain of Thought]] — Contrastive Chain of Thought adds an invalid rationale to each few-shot demonstration, built by shuffling the objects in the valid one, and reports gains over plain CoT on three reasoning sets with one model; a toy theory paper offers partial support.
- [[Controllable Text Generation Taxonomy]] — A 2024 survey sorts control of LLM output into content (hard) and attribute (soft) tasks, and into training-stage and inference-stage methods; prompting, latent steering and decoding-time edits sit on the inference side. It ran no experiments, so it ranks nothing.
- [[CoT-Decoding]] — Elicits chain-of-thought paths without any prompt by branching on the top-k first tokens, continuing greedily, and choosing the path with the largest answer-confidence margin.
- [[Ctrl-G HMM Logical Control]] — Ctrl-G distills a hidden Markov model from the LLM, compiles a logical constraint to a DFA, and reweights next-token probabilities by the chance of satisfying the constraint; it reached full constraint success on text editing, but needs logit access and a distilled model per LLM.
- [[Decomposed Prompting (DecomP)]] — A decomposer prompt emits tagged sub-questions that a controller routes to separate handlers, which can be few-shot prompts, recursive decomposers or symbolic tools such as retrieval.
- [[DERA Dialog-Enabled Resolving Agents]] — DERA has two GPT-4 prompts, a Researcher that points out problems and a Decider with final authority, converse over a draft and write accepted fixes to a shared scratchpad; on clinical summaries and care plans physicians preferred the result, while multiple-choice and open-ended medical QA showed little or no gain (self-reported).
- [[Direct-Indirect Reasoning (DIR)]] — Adds indirect reasoning prompts, contrapositive and proof by contradiction, alongside direct reasoning chains and votes over both, improving logical reasoning and mathematical proof tasks.
- [[Discriminator Bottleneck in LLM Planning]] — A study of re-ranking, iterative correction and tree search over sampled programs finds that the accuracy of the discriminator that scores candidates matters more than the planning method: advanced methods need roughly 90% discrimination accuracy to beat re-ranking, tree search is 10-20 times slower, and execution feedback lifts weak discriminators (self-reported).
- [[EmotionPrompt]] — Appending a short emotional or confidence-pushing sentence (such as a remark about career importance) to a prompt; the 2023 paper reports gains across six models, but most of the headline comes from picking the best of 11 stimuli per task on the test set.
- [[FormatSpread]] — Meaning-preserving changes to prompt format (casing, separators, spaces, enumeration) move few-shot accuracy by up to 76 points on LLaMA-2-13B; FormatSpread estimates the min-max spread across formats with a bandit under a budget, without model weights (self-reported).
- [[GATE Generative Active Task Elicitation]] — GATE has a language model interview the user, with open-ended questions, yes/no questions or generated edge cases to label, so the user's preferences are captured better than by writing a prompt.
- [[GCG Adversarial Suffix Attack]] — GCG appends an optimised token suffix to a harmful request by gradient-guided greedy search so an aligned model starts with an affirmative reply; suffixes tuned on open models transferred to some closed models in mid-2023 tests, and the paper also introduced the AdvBench harmful-behaviour set.
- [[GeM-CoT]] — Routes each question to a typed demonstration pool by embedding similarity, falls back to zero-shot CoT for unmatched questions, and grows the pool by clustering cached fallbacks into auto-built demonstrations.
- [[GEPA Reflective Prompt Evolution]] — GEPA is a prompt optimizer for multi-module LLM systems that has a reflection model read run traces and textual feedback, rewrite one module's prompt, and keep candidates on a per-instance Pareto frontier; the paper reports it beating GRPO on five of six tasks with far fewer rollouts (self-reported).
- [[Graph of Thoughts]] — Prompting framework where a user-written Graph of Operations (Generate, Aggregate, Score, KeepBest, Repeat) lets thoughts merge and loop; the paper reports lower sorting error than Tree of Thoughts at lower cost on GPT-3.5 (self-reported).
- [[HiAR-ICL Thought Cards]] — HiAR-ICL replaces worked examples in the prompt with abstract thought cards, reasoning-action chains found offline by Monte-Carlo tree search on 200 seed questions and retrieved by problem complexity; the authors report 80.6% on MATH500 with a 7B model and about 7 to 22 times less time than rStar (self-reported).
- [[Hypotheses-to-Theories (HtT)]] — Two-stage prompting that induces a library of rules from training questions, filters them by coverage and confidence, then prepends the library so the model retrieves rules rather than generating them.
- [[Iterated Decomposition]] — A human-in-the-loop loop for building select-then-generate LM pipelines: run on gold answers, inspect failing steps in a trace viewer, fix the worst one (usually paragraph selection) and repeat; reported gains on three science Q&A tasks with text-davinci-002 (self-reported).
- [[Jailbreak Rapid Response]] — Jailbreak rapid response adapts an input guard after seeing a few successful jailbreaks, multiplying each with generated variants; RapidResponseBench tests it on six attack strategies and reports large attack-success drops from fine-tuning the guard (self-reported).
- [[Least-to-Most Prompting]] — Two-stage few-shot prompting that first lists easier subproblems and then solves them in order, feeding earlier answers forward; large gains on length generalisation, small ones on GSM8K.
- [[LLM Action Grounding for Embodied Planning]] — Two frozen-LLM planners for household tasks, one translating each generated step to the nearest admissible action and one retrieving examples and re-planning on failure; both report executability or low-data gains, with a correctness cost in the first (self-reported).
- [[LM-BFF and PET Prompt-Based Fine-Tuning]] — Two 2021 recipes that recast classification as a cloze task for a small masked language model and fine-tune on few labels (LM-BFF 16 per class, PET 32 in total): LM-BFF (RoBERTa-large, searched templates, sampled demonstrations) and PET (ALBERT, ensembled patterns distilled into a classifier); each reports its own gains (self-reported).
- [[LM-Guided Chain-of-Thought]] — A small fine-tuned LM (FLAN-T5 80M) writes the rationale and a frozen larger LM (FLAN-T5 XXL 11B) answers; distilled rationales beat plain CoT on 2WikiMultiHopQA by about 10% EM/F1, while the RL stage adds only tenths of a point (self-reported).
- [[Logic-of-Thought]] — A zero-shot augmentation that extracts propositions from the context, expands them with logic laws in Python, translates the result to English and appends it to the original context; improved the baseline in 37 of 40 comparisons in the paper (self-reported).
- [[Logits Masking and Grammar-Constrained Generation]] — Two decode-time patterns from a design-patterns book: logits masking zeroes rule-violating continuations at sampling, grammar restricts output to a context-free syntax; try-and-try-again is only cheap when the failure rate is under about 10 percent.
- [[LongICLBench and Many-Shot ICL Scaling]] — Two 2024 studies of in-context learning with hundreds to thousands of demonstrations. Many-shot ICL keeps improving on classification up to about 1,000 examples, while LongICLBench shows most models collapse when the label space is large (self-reported).
- [[Medprompt]] — Medprompt combines nearest-neighbour few-shot exemplars, self-generated chain-of-thought and a choice-shuffle ensemble to push GPT-4 past specialist medical models on multiple-choice benchmarks.
- [[MetaCrit Adaptive Critique]] — A four-call pipeline (generator, read-only monitor, controller, synthesizer) that scales critique to the answer instead of always critiquing; in its paper, appending a plain critique instruction to every baseline made accuracy collapse, while the graded pipeline reached 94.12 on TruthfulQA with GPT-3.5-Turbo (self-reported).
- [[Mixtures of In-Context Learners]] — MoICL splits demonstrations into subsets, runs the model on each as an expert, and mixes their next-token log-probabilities with learned weights, including negative ones; it beat concatenated ICL on 5 of 7 classification sets and resisted noisy or imbalanced demonstrations (self-reported).
- [[Multi-Expert Prompting]] — One prompt chain simulates several experts, answers as each, then merges the answers with seven subtasks borrowed from the Nominal Group Technique; the paper reports 89.35 True% on TruthfulQA with ChatGPT at about 22.7 times the tokens of zero-shot CoT, and no gain on reasoning multiple choice (self-reported).
- [[Multimodal Chain-of-Thought]] — A two-stage recipe for sub-1B T5 models: stage one writes a rationale from text plus ViT image features, stage two reads the rationale and answers; the paper reports 90.45% on ScienceQA with 738M parameters and traces text-only failures to hallucinated rationales (self-reported).
- [[Outlines Guided Generation]] — Outlines casts constrained generation as a finite-state machine over the vocabulary, precomputing an index from state to allowed tokens so each step applies a logit mask at roughly constant cost; it guarantees format, not correctness, and the paper's evidence is thin.
- [[PAIR Black-Box Jailbreak Refinement]] — PAIR has an attacker LLM rewrite a jailbreak prompt over a few rounds using the target's reply and a judge's score; the paper reports tens of queries per success on some targets, weak results on heavily safety-tuned ones, and better transfer than GCG (self-reported).
- [[PEZ Gradient-Based Hard Prompt Discovery]] — PEZ finds discrete text prompts by gradient descent on a continuous iterate that is projected to the nearest token embeddings at each forward pass; it needs white-box gradients, yields often unreadable prompts, and was tested only on small models and narrow classification.
- [[Plan-and-Solve Prompting]] — Zero-shot prompt (PS and PS+) that asks the model to devise a plan, extract variables and compute intermediate results before answering; reported on text-davinci-003 to beat Zero-shot-CoT on math sets with triggers tuned per dataset (self-reported).
- [[Principled Instructions (26 Prompt Principles)]] — A list of 26 hand-written prompting rules in five categories, tested on a 20-questions-per-principle human-rated benchmark; the paper's gains are self-reported win rates without statistics, and the politeness, tipping and threat rules were never tested individually.
- [[Prompt Engineering]] — The pillar hub — prompt ⊂ context ⊂ harness, the "colleague with minimal context" golden rule, and the nine techniques with their authority hierarchy.
- [[Few-Shot Prompting]] — Zero-shot versus few-shot, the three properties good examples need (relevant, diverse, structured) with the failure mode for each, and few-shot CoT.
- [[Prompt Order Sensitivity and Entropy Probing]] — The order of the same few-shot examples can swing GPT-2 and GPT-3 classification accuracy from about 85% to about 50%; Lu et al. rank orderings without labels by the entropy of predictions on a model-generated probing set (GlobalE, LocalE), reporting gains (self-reported).
- [[Prompt-Induced Waste in Coding Agents]] — A preregistered coding-agent benchmark reports that wording such as 'consider several approaches' or 'be absolutely certain' multiplies reasoning or verification cost at flat success, that harness choice swings cost more than wording, and that cost per successful task is the metric to watch (self-reported).
- [[RAP Reasoning via Planning]] — A framework that prompts one frozen LLM as both reasoning agent and world model and searches reasoning traces with Monte Carlo Tree Search; the paper reports large gains on Blocksworld planning and small ones on GSM8K (self-reported).
- [[ReAct Synergizing Reasoning and Acting]] — The ReAct paper's few-shot results on PaLM-540B, interleaving thought, action and observation steps; it trails CoT on HotpotQA alone (27.4 against 29.4 EM) but a ReAct/CoT-SC hybrid reaches 35.1, and it beats action-only prompting on ALFWorld and WebShop (self-reported).
- [[REFINER Critic Feedback on Reasoning Steps]] — A generator-critic framework in which a trained critic gives structured, typed feedback on a model's intermediate reasoning, reported to beat scalar PPO reward and LLM-written self-feedback on small math, logic and norm tasks (self-reported).
- [[Rephrase and Respond]] — Ask the model to rewrite and expand the question and then answer it, in one prompt (RaR) or two calls (Two-step RaR); in the paper's GPT-4 setup the gains were largest on tasks where the original question was ambiguous.
- [[Retrieval Augmented Thoughts (RAT)]] — Zero-shot procedure that drafts a chain of thought, then revises each step in order using retrieval queried from the task, the already revised steps and the current draft step.
- [[Scratchpads and Self-Notes]] — Let a model write intermediate computation as text before the answer (scratchpads, 2021) or inline while reading the context (Self-Notes, 2023); Nye et al. report results on 137B and 8B models, while Self-Notes is mostly small GPT-2 work on synthetic tasks (self-reported).
- [[Selection-Inference]] — Split multi-step logical reasoning into alternating Selection calls (pick facts) and Inference calls (derive one new fact from only those facts), so each step depends on the last; the paper reports a 7B model beating larger baselines on 10 tasks (self-reported).
- [[Self-Ask Prompting]] — A few-shot prompt in which the model writes each follow-up question and its intermediate answer before the final answer, optionally answering the follow-ups with a search engine; on the 125-question Bamboogle set with davinci-002 it scored 57.6% against 46.4% for chain of thought, and 60.0% with search (self-reported).
- [[Self-Discover]] — Self-Discover has the model select, adapt and implement reasoning modules into a JSON plan once per task, then fill the plan per instance; it reports gains over zero-shot chain of thought with PaLM 2-L and GPT-4, mostly on world-knowledge tasks, and small MATH gains because failures are arithmetic (self-reported).
- [[Self-Evaluation Defense Against Adversarial Suffixes]] — A prompted LLM judges the user input, the generator's output, or both as safe or unsafe before a reply is returned; the paper reports it driving GCG-suffix attack success to near zero on a small test and beating Llama-Guard2 and moderation APIs, at the cost of over-refusal on suffixed safe inputs (self-reported).
- [[Self-Evaluation Guided Beam Search]] — Decoding method that scores each reasoning step by the model's own probability that the step is correct, combines it with generation probability and samples beams by that score; the paper reports gains on GSM8K and long chains at about three times the cost of self-consistency (self-reported).
- [[Self-Refine]] — A training-free loop in which one LLM drafts an output, writes feedback on it and rewrites it; the paper reports about 20 points average gain over one-shot generation (self-reported) but almost none on math without an external correctness signal.
- [[SelfCheck Step Verification]] — Checks each reasoning step with four independent LLM calls (extract target, collect context, regenerate, compare) and uses the confidence for weighted voting; on GSM8K with GPT-3.5 and 2 solutions it lifted accuracy 71.7 to 74.3 (self-reported).
- [[Skeleton-of-Thought]] — A prompt-only way to cut answer latency: the model first drafts a short numbered skeleton, then each point is expanded in a parallel request; the paper reports up to 2.39x speed-up for its best model and up to 2.33x for its best question category, on Vicuna-80 (self-reported), with poor fit for step-by-step math.
- [[SkillOpt Agent Skill Optimization]] — SkillOpt optimizes an agent's skill document as external state borrowing training controls: capped edits, a gate on held-out data, a store of rejected edits and a slow epoch-end update; the paper reports the best or equal-best score on every cell, with several reporting gaps.
- [[Strategic Chain-of-Thought (SCoT)]] — One structured prompt makes the model list candidate methods and choose the most efficient as strategic knowledge before solving; a few-shot variant retrieves demonstrations by the chosen strategy.
- [[Symbolic Chain-of-Thought (SymbCoT)]] — Four LLM-run modules (translator, planner, solver, verifier) translate premises to first-order logic and reason over them with named inference rules, with no external solver.
- [[System 2 Attention]] — System 2 Attention has the model first regenerate the prompt keeping only relevant text, then answer from the cleaned version; on modified TriviaQA with LLaMA-2-70B-chat accuracy rose 62.8 to 80.3 (self-reported).
- [[ThoughtSculpt]] — Inference-time Monte Carlo Tree Search over LLM thought nodes whose action space includes revising earlier output; the paper reports gains over Tree of Thoughts and Self-Refine on story outlines, crosswords and constrained generation (self-reported).
- [[Visualization-of-Thought (VoT)]] — A zero-shot prompt asking the model to draw the state after each reasoning step, which helps GPT-4 on text-grid spatial tasks but gives mixed or no gain on small models.
- [[XML Prompt Structuring]] — Tagging inputs so the model can tell instructions from data — the technique that addresses misattribution, which is the mechanism behind injection.
- [[Structured Output]] — A structured-output prompt is a request, not a guarantee; always pair with harness-side schema validation and prefer constrained decoding where available.
- [[Prompt Templates and Variables]] — The fixed-skeleton/variable split makes prompts versionable, evaluable, and — most importantly — makes the injection boundary explicit.
- [[Long-Context Prompting]] — Longform data at the top (up to 30% quality improvement when the query appears last), XML tags for multi-document inputs, and grounding responses in extracted quotes.
- [[Prompt Chaining]] — Why decomposition beats one large prompt (split attention, contamination), what it costs, and where chains cross into harness territory.
- [[Prompt Injection]] — Instructions and data share one channel with no structural separation — the attack surface, Best-of-N power-law scaling, and defences ordered with least privilege first.
- [[Evidence-Labelled Generation with Deterministic QC]] — model output cites evidence labels E1..En that code verifies against the inputs, so grounding is checked without a judge
- [[Graded-Uncertainty Enrichment Contract]] — three levels of not-knowing (flags, partial, needs_review) so sparse input lowers certainty not quality; `unknown` for enums, empty arrays for multi-selects, partial_but_usable as the anti-backlog route
- [[Capped Tiered Tag Assignment]] — Category defines a primary/secondary/situational tag range; items get 1–3 (default 2) with at most one stretch tag and concrete evidence, because tag inflation kills filtering
- [[Length Limits Are a Fit Layer, Not a Prompt]] — enforce maxLength in characters and truncate in code; cap validate-retry at 2–3 then repair deterministically
- [[Free Text With Deterministic Repair Over SDK-Enforced Schema]] — decision: keep text generation plus a sanitize/validate chain and amend the spec; unmeasured criteria default to the status quo, and enforcement wording must name each path's real mechanism
- [[Prompts as Document Completion]] — An LLM is a document-completion engine, so a prompt is the opening of a document whose most plausible continuation is the answer — five properties (tokens, autoregression, left-to-right attention, truth bias, chat-as-transcript) explain nearly every prompting rule.
- [[Four Prompt Criteria]] — Every prompt must at once resemble training data (the Little Red Riding Hood principle), carry all and only the relevant information (Chekhov's gun), lead to a solution, and have a natural stopping point — chat tuning gives you three for free, never the second.
- [[Prompt Assembly as Snippet Packing]] — The feedforward pass of an LLM app (retrieve → snippetize → score → assemble) ends in a packing problem — pick snippets by priority under a token budget, honouring elastic versions and mutual exclusion, then emit the winners in document order.
- [[SOP-Style Agent System Prompts]] — Write a long agent system prompt as an ordered procedure with per-stage exception branches, not an unordered rule pile — in a Tau-Bench ablation, flattening the same rules into a list cut task success by more than 30% while tone changes barely mattered.
- [[Prompting Technique Selection]] — Which named prompting technique to reach for by problem shape — zero-shot first, few-shot for non-obvious format, CoT and self-consistency for reasoning, ToT for search, PAL for exact computation, ReAct for fresh information, Reflexion when a checker exists, chaining for separable stages.
- [[Self-Consistency]] — Sample several chain-of-thought completions at non-zero temperature and take the majority answer — reasoning paths that converge are more trustworthy than any single greedy path, at N× cost and only when there is a single extractable answer to vote on.
- [[Tree of Thoughts]] — Generalizes chain-of-thought into search — a propose prompt generates candidate intermediate steps, a value prompt rates each (sure/maybe/impossible), and a code controller runs BFS/DFS with backtracking; GPT-4 went from 4% (CoT) to 74% on Game of 24.
- [[Program-Aided Language Models (PAL)]] — The model writes a program and an interpreter computes the answer — few-shot examples are code with the reasoning in comments, so exact computation (dates, arithmetic) moves out of the token predictor; only safe with a sandbox, and today usually a code-execution tool.
- [[Reflexion]] — Verbal reinforcement learning — an actor attempts, an evaluator scores, a self-reflection step turns the score into natural-language feedback kept in memory for the retry; it works only when you get a do-over and an objective evaluator, and reinforces confident mistakes with a weak one.
- [[Sampling Parameters]] — Temperature, top_p, max_tokens, stop and frequency/presence penalties — tune temperature or top_p and one penalty, never both; temperature 0 is not deterministic; best-of-n wants temperature ≈ sqrt(n)/10; many reasoning endpoints fix or reject these knobs.
- [[Stop Sequences and Completion Anatomy]] — A completion runs preamble → solution → recognizable end → fluffy postscript; push structural boilerplate into the prompt, let reasoning preamble run, kill fluff, and decide up front which recognizable end a server-side stop sequence will cut on.
- [[Logprobs as Confidence Signal]] — Where the API exposes token log-probabilities they are the best confidence instrument — gate on average token probability, read classification probabilities off a first token, give every label a distinct first token to avoid prefix collision, and calibrate per-label offsets on labelled data.
- [[Fine-Tuning as Prompt Compression]] — Fine-tuning is prompt engineering by other means — full fine-tuning teaches facts, LoRA teaches which existing behaviours and priors to use, soft prompting learns a vector-valued prompt; reach for it when the static prompt is long, stable and still not followed, then make prompts look like the fine-tuning documents.
- [[Tool Definitions as Prompts]] — Function calling is still document completion — schemas are rendered into the system message (TypeScript-style in one vendor's format, with some JSON-schema constraints silently dropped), so every name, description and enum is prompt text steering a chain of hierarchical classifiers; validate constraints and gate side effects in code.
- [[Prompting Reasoning Models]] — Reasoning models do chain-of-thought natively, so prompts shift from how to think to what to achieve — state goals and constraints, drop manual CoT (it can degrade instruction-following), escalate effort gradually against an eval, and never treat the visible thinking as a faithful audit trail.
- [[Prompt Optimization]] — Treat the prompt as a versioned artifact searched against a fixed eval set and metric — by hand (cheapest layer first, one change at a time), by an LLM proposer (APE, OPRO, meta-prompting) or by a framework (DSPy, TextGrad); an optimizer without a metric is just a rewriter.
- [[DSPy]] — DSPy replaces hand-written prompts with signatures and modules that an optimizer compiles against a metric and training set — bootstrapping and ordering few-shot demonstrations, searching instruction wordings, or distilling into weights; worth it only with a multi-step pipeline, a scorable metric and labelled data.
- [[Evaluating Prompts]] — Prompt-level evaluation — build the eval before the prompt; match test style to what changes (model, single prompt, composition); climb from a git-diffed example suite to an automated harness by solving sample sourcing and scoring independently; then A/B online with success and guardrail metrics fixed up front.
- [[SOMA Judge Rubric]] — Write LLM-judge rubrics as Specific questions on an Ordinal, level-described scale across Multiple Aspects — never tell the judge it is grading its own work, split Goldilocks questions into separate axes, put the rubric before the content, and treat scores as relative.
- [[Diversity Seeding for Synthetic Data]] — Temperature alone does not make synthetic data diverse — inject the randomness into the prompt by sampling combinations of dimension values (TinyStories), generating hierarchically, varying target audience (phi-1), or few-shot query generation for retrievers (Promptagator); a seeded label is a free label.
- [[Code Generation Prompting]] — Prompt shapes for code — instruction→code, comments→code, signature completion with stop sequences, schema→SQL, explain-code, parseable unit-test prompts and fill-in-the-middle — plus the over-refusal trap of generic safety system prompts; context and verification remain the biggest levers.
- [[Stateful Objects of Discourse]] — Collaboration talks about persistent objects, but plain chat duplicates them into the transcript and makes "it" ambiguous — separate the object (own pane, short name, updated in place) from the conversation, prefer diffs and edit tools over regeneration, support several objects, and feed user edits back as diffs.
- [[Prompt Leaking]] — Extracting a system prompt, its examples or other confidential context — models refuse direct requests but yield to an escalation ladder (tagged echo, odd tool names, a logging tool, encodings, baiting with partial leaks), so the only safe stance is to assume the prompt will leak and keep secrets out of it.
- [[Zero-Shot Chain-of-Thought]] — A trigger sentence appended to the question, plus a second call to extract the answer, elicits step-by-step reasoning with no exemplars; strong on arithmetic and symbolic tasks, flat on commonsense.

## Context

- [[ACE Agentic Context Engineering Playbooks]] — ACE keeps an agent's context as an itemized playbook updated by small merged deltas instead of full rewrites, to avoid brevity bias and context collapse; with DeepSeek-V3.1 it beat prompt optimizers on AppWorld and finance tasks, but degrades without a reliable feedback signal (self-reported).
- [[AdaCoM Learned Context Manager]] — AdaCoM trains a small RL manager to edit a frozen agent's context through JSON edits; on BrowseComp-Plus it lifted four agents by 39.0% on average over ReAct (self-reported), and the learned edit style differs per agent.
- [[AgentFold Context Folding]] — AgentFold lets a web agent fold its own history at two scales each turn, condensing one step or consolidating a range into one block, keeping context near 7k tokens over 100 turns on BrowseComp (self-reported).
- [[Agentic Context Management (ACM)]] — ACM gives the agent two tools, one to summarise everything since the last call while saving the raw messages to disk and one to query them by id, so the agent chooses when to compress; post-training a 9B model on it lifted BrowseComp-Plus Pass@1 from 0.635 to 0.727 (self-reported).
- [[Catastrophic Remembering in Context Files]] — Agent instruction files such as CLAUDE.md grow monotonically in the wild (+226% over a file's life, 1,867 repos), and a simulated study finds that annotating edits with notes to the next maintainer cuts the excess growth sharply; the fix was never tested on a real file.
- [[Coding Agents as Long-Context Processors]] — Cao et al. (2026) treat a huge text corpus as a folder of files and let an off-the-shelf coding agent (Codex with GPT-5) read it with shell search and Python; it beat the best published result on four of five long-context benchmarks, while adding a retriever did not reliably help.
- [[Context Engineering]] — The pillar hub — the minimization objective, the four levers (Write → Select → Compress → Isolate) applied in order, and the four context types.
- [[DeepSeek-OCR Optical Context Compression]] — DeepSeek-OCR renders text as an image and encodes it into far fewer vision tokens; on a 100-page English test it decoded at 97% precision under 10x compression and about 60% near 20x (self-reported), evaluated as OCR only.
- [[Focus Active Context Compression]] — Focus gives a coding agent two tools, start_focus and complete_focus, so it can collapse a finished exploration into a short knowledge block and drop the raw messages; a five-instance SWE-bench Lite test cut tokens 22.7% at equal pass count (self-reported).
- [[IN2 Training and VAL Probes]] — IN2 is synthetic long-context fine-tuning that places the answer at random positions, and VAL Probing is the document, code and database test set that measures how much accuracy depends on where the evidence sits; the paper argues needle-in-a-haystack scores overstate long-context ability.
- [[Knowledge Conflicts Taxonomy]] — A 2024 survey sorts the ways an LLM's inputs and weights can disagree into context-memory, inter-context and intra-memory conflicts, with causes, observed behaviour and fixes for each; the survey compiles other papers' numbers and says they are not comparable.
- [[LC-Boost Short-Context Processing of Long Inputs]] — LC-Boost has a short-context LLM walk a long input chunk by chunk, choosing actions such as retrieve, append and merge to accumulate evidence before answering; with a 4K working context it led on multi-document QA but not on code completion, and cost more tokens on aggregation tasks (self-reported).
- [[Learned Proactive Compaction]] — A coding agent can be trained to call compact() itself, at a seam rather than at a length limit; AutoCompact reports 39.6% vs 30.4% on SWE-bench Verified (self-reported), but the like-for-like 16K gain is 38.0% vs 28.8%, and most of the lift survives even when the compaction calls are skipped.
- [[LightThinker Gist-Token Compression]] — LightThinker trains a model to compress each finished thought into a few gist tokens and drop the text; on Qwen it cost 1% accuracy for 26% less time and 70% lower peak tokens, and LightThinker++ adds commit, expand and fold primitives (self-reported).
- [[LLMLingua Coarse-to-Fine Prompt Compression]] — LLMLingua uses a small LM's perplexity to drop low-information tokens from a long prompt before it goes to a black-box LLM; on GSM8K it kept accuracy within about 1.5 EM at 20x compression in the authors' setup, with larger losses on BBH.
- [[LongHarness Evidence State]] — LongHarness keeps a source-linked evidence bank and a conclusion state with explicit open gaps outside the interaction history, and scored well above direct baselines and shell-based harnesses on 100M-token corpus QA, though its ablations show most of the gain comes from the shared scaffold (self-reported).
- [[Lost in Conversation (Sharded Multi-Turn Simulation)]] — A 2025 simulation splits single-turn tasks into one-fact shards revealed turn by turn; across 15 LLMs and six tasks average performance fell 39%, mostly through unreliability rather than lost aptitude, and recap or lower temperature did not close the gap (self-reported).
- [[Lost in the Middle]] — Models given a long prompt use information at its start and end better than information in the middle; GPT-3.5-Turbo with 20 documents scored 75.8% with the answer first but 53.8% at index 9, below its closed-book 56.1% (self-reported).
- [[MemAgent and InfMem Bounded Memory Reading]] — MemAgent reads a long document chunk by chunk and overwrites a fixed 1024-token memory, trained with Multi-Conv DAPO; InfMem adds a pre-think, retrieve, write loop with early stopping and reports gains over it (both self-reported).
- [[ParSer Parallel Chunk Reading]] — ParSer keeps the whole document away from the lead agent and lets it broadcast questions to frozen chunk-bound subagents that return evidence or "Unknown"; it beat sequential memory agents on multi-hop QA up to 896K tokens (self-reported).
- [[PRO-LONG Programmatic Memory]] — PRO-LONG keeps a lossless append-only log of everything an agent observes and does, and lets a coding agent read it back with grep, Python and file tools; it raised pass@1 on ARC-AGI-3 and NetHack over the same agent without a log (self-reported).
- [[Progressive Disclosure and Skill Packs at Library Scale]] — He et al. (2026) turn long books into skill packs an agent harness reads on demand; a flat pack (one SKILL.md with a chunk index) usually beat raw text, a hierarchical pack and hybrid RAG, but gains depend on task, language and harness (self-reported).
- [[ReContext Attention Evidence Replay]] — ReContext, a training-free harness, scores context tokens by attention from the last eight prompt tokens, copies the top sentences into a pool and replays them before the question; mean accuracy rose 0.24 to 0.30 across eight datasets (self-reported).
- [[Scroll Event Log and Eviction Index]] — Scroll keeps an agent's whole history in an append-only log outside the prompt, evicts stale spans from the view only, and leaves model-written headlines pointing at the verbatim record; it scored 73.1 on BEAM 10M against 68.0 published (self-reported, not a controlled comparison).
- [[SheetCompressor Spreadsheet Encoding]] — SheetCompressor serializes a spreadsheet for an LLM by keeping rows and columns near structural anchors, inverting the index to value-to-address and aggregating by number format, reaching 24.79x average token compression; Chain of Spreadsheet then detects the table before answering (self-reported).
- [[SKILL.state]] — SKILL.state replaces the growing transcript with a fixed spec, a JSON state the model updates by validated patch, and the latest observation; on the authors' synthetic Warehouse benchmark it held 0.94 accuracy at 100 steps on 65,408 tokens against 1,062,387 for a stateful baseline (self-reported).
- [[Trace as State]] — Trace as State re-runs a long-context task and places the first-pass reasoning traces before the context, not after it; it beat appending them in 26 of 27 cells, with GraphWalks Parents EM 29.2 to 81.8 for one model (self-reported).
- [[Why Context Is Finite]] — Attention divides a fixed budget across n² token pairs, so added tokens thin attention rather than expanding capacity; the marginal return curve turns negative, not merely flat.
- [[Context Anatomy]] — What goes in the window and in what order — system-prompt altitude, the five layers organized by stability, and stable-before-dynamic ordering for cache prefix matching.
- [[Context Retrieval Strategies]] — The shift from pre-computed retrieval to just-in-time context, the hybrid default with the ≥80%-of-turns test, and pre-retrieval pipeline ordering.
- [[Context Compaction]] — Transforming interaction history into a continuation state — retention priority, why tool-result clearing is the highest-value move, and why state extraction is what makes compaction safe.
- [[Memory as Context]] — Memory is the mechanism by which context outlives a window: episodic→semantic distillation, structured note-taking, index-plus-detail, and hygiene rules.
- [[Multi-Agent Context]] — Sub-agent isolation is the highest-cost lever — the discarded window is the feature, orchestrator-holds-plan is the default, and isolation doubles as a security boundary.
- [[Context Failure Modes]] — Five distinct failures (rot, poisoning, distraction, clash, injection) with separate mechanisms and fixes, plus the diagnostic flow.
- [[Compression Strips the Epistemic Frame]] — summarisation keeps headline facts and drops how-sure/how-known/how-changed; keep prose canonical, mark compression boundaries, audit for hedging drift
- [[Measuring Fixed Context Overhead]] — first-turn cache_creation_input_tokens on cold sessions as the fixed-prefix cost; declared files were only ~half of it
- [[Agent Status Bar]] — A short, code-maintained, key-value block the harness appends at the end of the context every turn (counters, time, TODO, environment, errors) — it works because in-context learning is closer to lookup than aggregation, so distilled state beats raw history; it is lossy and trusted almost unconditionally, so its accuracy must be monitored.
- [[Context Window Budgeting]] — Deciding the token split across instructions, evidence and working space before the window fills — reserve output first, cap every growing component, value each chunk by its marginal information, and set the budget by ablation against the effective (not advertised) window.
- [[Cache-Stable Context Layout]] — Once prompt caching carries real money, byte-stability of the prefix becomes an architectural constraint — the usual cache breakers (timestamps, per-request config, reordered tools, sliding windows, per-request few-shot) and the three design moves that keep the key stable (cache-boundary ordering, byte-aligned sub-agents, frozen tool-result replacements).
- [[Reasoning State Pass-Back]] — The server renders API messages into one token stream through a model-specific chat template, so mislabelled roles change behaviour (a tool result sent as a user turn can reset reasoning mid-task), and current reasoning models expect their prior reasoning returned verbatim inside the tool loop — stripping it makes agents repeat ruled-out hypotheses.
- [[Context-Aware Compression]] — Compressing tool results with the query and the agent's known state in the compressor prompt — in a course experiment it finished in 7 iterations and 40k tokens where per-result and combined summaries took 10–12 iterations and 93k–277k, while keeping identifiers verbatim; compression is understanding, so the compressor needs near-main-model capability.
- [[Structured Context and Schemas]] — Structure compresses meaning rather than tokens — tags for boundaries, markdown for prose, JSON for anything code reads back; schemas belong in three places (input, state carried across turns, output), and state should be small, structured, versioned, validated and overwritten rather than appended.
- [[Cognitive Tools]] — Reasoning steps packaged as callable prompts (understand, recall, examine, backtrack), each run in its own isolated context — prompting alone lifted a non-reasoning model on a maths benchmark from 26.7% to 43.3%, best when the model chooses which tool to call; gains shrink on reasoning models, and a deterministic check beats a model verifier wherever one exists.
- [[Protocol Shells]] — Pseudo-code prompt structure (name, intent, input, process, output) from a no-code context-engineering track — the explicit four parts measurably improve consistency, but the syntax itself has no special power; a markdown prompt with the same sections performs about the same.
- [[Evaluating Context]] — Context evals ask whether the right material reached the window and was used — instrument the failure chain (exists → retrieved → survived → used), measure each lever with its own metric, sweep length and position to find the effective window, and set the budget by component ablation; tokens per successful task is a quality metric.
- [[Context System Maturity Ladder]] — Five rungs of context machinery — single instruction, plus examples, plus carried state, plus coordinated specialist contexts, plus reasoning tools and verification — with the rule to climb only when the rung below fails measurably, seven recurring patterns, four reference architectures, and the order of design questions.
- [[Context Field Theory (Speculative)]] — Provisional — the neural-field / attractor / resonance / quantum-semantics vocabulary from part of a context-engineering course is metaphor, not method; only three ideas survive translation (early framing is sticky, salience comes from structure and repetition, ambiguity is resolved by context), and one underlying result (a symbolic abstraction→induction→retrieval circuit) is real.
- [[Index-Time Distillation]] — Naive chunk-and-retrieve fails on aggregation and rule-induction questions however good the retriever, because attention is soft retrieval, not a summariser — spend compute at index time to turn cases into statistics, rules and hierarchy (RAPTOR, GraphRAG, L0/L1/L2 filesystem tiers, factor discovery).

## Harness

- [[ADAS Meta Agent Search]] — ADAS defines an agent as code and has a meta agent program new agents from an archive of past discoveries; the discovered agents beat hand-designed ones, with small margins on knowledge-limited tasks.
- [[Adversarial Pop-up Attack on Computer-Use Agents]] — An injected pop-up built from an attention hook, an instruction, an info banner and an ALT descriptor drew clicks from every tested vision-language agent in the paper's OSWorld and VisualWebArena setups, and prompt-only defences helped little (self-reported).
- [[Aegean Stability-Horizon Consensus]] — Aegean is a leader-based, Raft-like protocol that finalizes a multi-agent answer only when a quorum agrees and the answer has held for a set number of rounds, replacing fixed round limits and majority voting; its speedups of 1.2 to 20 times at accuracy within 2.5% are self-reported on math and multiple-choice tasks.
- [[Agent Laboratory and mle-solver]] — Agent Laboratory runs literature review, experiments and report writing with role-prompted agents and optional human feedback, at $2.33 per paper with a gpt-4o backend (self-reported); its mle-solver edits code against an LLM reward, and automated reviewer scores far exceed human ones.
- [[Agent Primitives Latent Composition]] — Agent Primitives reduces multi-agent systems to three reusable blocks (review, voting and selection, planning and execution) that pass KV-cache state instead of text and are composed per query by an LLM organizer; reported gains are 12.0 to 16.5 points over one agent on Qwen3 models (self-reported, ICML 2026).
- [[Agent S Hierarchical GUI Agent]] — Agent S drives a desktop through a Manager that plans and a Worker that executes, with web knowledge plus two retrieved experience stores and a bounded action interface; with GPT-4o it roughly doubled the best prior OSWorld result (self-reported).
- [[Agentic Harness Engineering]] — A closed loop where an evolve agent edits file-level components of a coding agent's harness using layered trace reports and a change manifest that predicts which tasks each edit fixes or risks.
- [[Agentless Localize-Repair-Validate]] — Agentless solves repository issues with a fixed three-phase pipeline of hierarchical localization, sampled patch repair and test-based selection, with no agent loop, and reports 32.00% on SWE-bench Lite at $0.70 per issue.
- [[AgentRxiv Shared Preprint Server]] — AgentRxiv gives research-agent labs a shared preprint server to upload to and retrieve from; one lab with access to prior reports kept improving on MATH-500 while a lab without plateaued, and parallel labs got there sooner at about triple the cost.
- [[AGENTS.md Context File Evaluation]] — A study of repository context files (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md) on SWE-bench Lite and a new benchmark, CtxBench; the files did not significantly raise success and raised cost by about 20%.
- [[AI Assistance Skill Formation RCT]] — A randomized trial with 52 Python developers learning an unfamiliar library found that those given a GPT-4o chat assistant scored 4.15 of 27 points lower on a follow-up quiz (about 17%) with no significant speed-up (self-reported).
- [[AI Co-Mathematician]] — The AI co-mathematician is a multi-agent research workbench over Gemini 3.1 Pro with no custom training; it reports 23 of 48 (48%) on FrontierMath Tier 4 against 19% for the base model (self-reported), and names false consensus and non-terminating review loops as failure modes.
- [[AI Scientist Automated Research Pipeline]] — The AI Scientist runs idea generation, coding-assistant experiments, LaTeX write-up and an LLM reviewer end to end on three small ML templates for roughly $10-15 per paper (self-reported); its own reviewer rejected all ten highlighted papers, and it edited its own timeout when limited.
- [[AIOS]] — AIOS puts an operating-system kernel beneath existing agent frameworks: agent requests become syscalls on LLM, memory, storage and tool queues, served by a scheduler that can pause and resume generation; the reported gain is up to 2.1x throughput on one GPU (self-reported).
- [[AIRA Draft-Debug-Improve Search]] — AIRA-Compose and AIRA-Design run a greedy tree search of draft, debug, improve and analyze operators to design neural architectures; one-shot agents produced no valid submissions and only a third of open-ended autoresearch steps scored.
- [[AiScientist File-as-Bus]] — AiScientist coordinates a long-horizon ML research agent through durable, schema-governed files rather than conversation, keeping orchestration thin; removing the file bus costs far more on later-round refinement than on producing a first runnable result.
- [[AlphaCodium Flow Engineering]] — A multi-stage code-generation flow for competitive programming that iterates on public tests, then on model-written tests guarded by test anchors; raised GPT-4 pass@5 on CodeContests validation from 19% to 44%.
- [[AnyTool]] — AnyTool searches a pool of 16K+ APIs with a hierarchy of GPT-4 agents (meta-agent, category agents, tool agents) and a self-reflection loop, scoring 58.2% average pass rate on filtered ToolBench against 22.9% for the original ToolLLM pipeline (self-reported).
- [[AppAgent Exploration Documents]] — AppAgent lets a GPT-4 agent operate smartphone apps without training by giving it a per-element document written either through its own trial-and-error exploration or by watching a human; the paper reports success rising from 2.2% to 95.6% across configurations (self-reported).
- [[AsyncLM Asynchronous Function Calling]] — Let the model keep decoding while function calls run in background workers, injecting results as interrupt blocks in a small markup protocol (CML); the preliminary paper reports up to 5.4x lower task latency than synchronous calling on BFCL workloads, with accuracy neutral at best (self-reported).
- [[AsyncThink Fork-Join Organizer]] — AsyncThink trains one small model with reinforcement learning to act as an organizer that forks sub-queries to workers sharing its weights and joins their summaries; on math and countdown it matches parallel thinking with 28% lower critical-path latency (self-reported), but baselines are not token-matched.
- [[AutoHarness Synthesized Code Harness]] — An LLM writes and refines a code harness (a legality verifier or a whole policy) from environment feedback, using tree search; tested on text games, where the verifier removed illegal moves and the policy variant ran with no model call at test time.
- [[AutoScientists Self-Organizing Teams]] — AutoScientists runs a decentralised team of research agents over a shared state with a noise-aware promotion gate; its gain over a single agent is domain-dependent and every removed mechanism hurts on at least one task.
- [[Budget Tracker and BATS]] — Showing a tool-using agent its remaining tool-call budget each turn (Budget Tracker), and the fuller BATS loop that adds budget-aware planning and answer verification, changed how a ReAct agent spent search calls on BrowseComp (all self-reported, mostly Gemini-2.5-Pro).
- [[CAID Centralized Asynchronous Isolated Delegation]] — A manager agent builds a dependency graph and delegates ready units to engineer agents, each in its own git worktree, merging through tests; beat a single agent on two coding benchmarks at higher cost and no clear wall-clock saving.
- [[Chain-of-Tools Frozen-Model Tool Retrieval]] — Chain-of-Tools picks tools for a frozen LLM by comparing its hidden state with tool-description embeddings through small trained modules, which keeps prompt length flat however large the tool pool gets; it handles 837 unseen tools at 10.4% top-1 where its baseline scores zero (self-reported, 7B models).
- [[ChatDev Chat Chain Software Company]] — ChatDev runs role-specialised LLM agents (CEO, CTO, programmer, reviewer, tester) through a chat chain of five subtasks in design, coding and testing phases, with a step where the assistant asks for specifics before answering; the authors report it beating two baselines on their own dataset and metrics, at several times the tokens and time of a single agent (self-reported).
- [[ChatTester Intention Prompt and Compile Repair]] — An LLM unit-test generator that first asks for the method's intention and then repairs compile errors in a bounded loop, built on an empirical study of ChatGPT-generated Java tests.
- [[ChemCrow Chemistry Tool Agent]] — ChemCrow wraps GPT-4 in a ReAct loop with 18 chemistry, search, safety and reaction tools; the authors report four robot-executed syntheses and expert preference over tool-less GPT-4 on 14 tasks, while a GPT-4 judge ranked the two the other way round (self-reported).
- [[CoAct-1 Coding Actions for Computer Use]] — A computer-use multi-agent system whose Orchestrator delegates each subtask to a GUI Operator or a Programmer that writes Python and Bash, so code becomes an action beside clicks.
- [[Code, Workflow, RAG or Agent Decision Guide]] — A qualitative four-way decision guide for choosing plain code, a deterministic workflow, a chatbot or retrieval system, or an autonomous agent, with design principles; no cost or latency numbers back it.
- [[Code-Based Self-Verification and Weighted Voting]] — Prompting a code-interpreter model to verify its answer with code, then weighting sampled answers by verification state, lifted MATH accuracy where natural-language verification did not.
- [[CodeScientist Semi-Automated Discovery]] — CodeScientist generates ideas from papers and code blocks, runs experiments, and relies on external reviewers plus a vetoing expert; the denominators matter, because 6 surviving discoveries came from 250 runs on 50 human-picked ideas.
- [[Codified Context Three-Tier Infrastructure]] — An experience report proposing an always-loaded constitution, domain-specialist agent specs and an on-demand knowledge base served over MCP, built for a roughly 100,000-line codebase.
- [[Conductor Learned Orchestration]] — The Conductor is a 7B model trained with GRPO on correctness alone to write a whole multi-agent workflow in natural language (a subtask, a worker id and an access list per step); it scored 77.27 average against GPT-5's 74.78 on its authors' table, with gains of a few points on unseen tasks (self-reported).
- [[Confucius Code Agent]] — A coding-agent scaffold combining file-based working memory, threshold-triggered structured summarisation, a cross-session note-taking agent and a meta-agent that tunes tool prompts.
- [[CORAL LLM-Native Recommender Harness]] — A fixed-order loop in which an LLM agent tunes a live recommender's resource allocation each cycle, under a constrained optimizer and an outcome-attributed memory, improving across A/B-tested rounds without retraining; gains were small.
- [[Deep Research Agent Systems]] — How search and deep-research agents are built, from a survey's three-layer taxonomy to concrete systems (Open Deep Search, MindSearch, Baidu's multi-agent search); what each reports and how little of it is comparable across papers.
- [[DeepCode Information-Flow Management]] — Four operations for generating a whole repository from a long paper without overflowing context: a blueprint, per-file memory entries with context clearing, conditional retrieval of reference code, and a verification loop.
- [[DeepDive Knowledge-Graph Search Agent Training]] — Synthesise hard multi-hop search questions from knowledge-graph random walks with blurred attributes, then train a search agent with multi-turn RL whose reward subtracts a keyword-overlap penalty for redundant queries.
- [[Dependency Injection for LLM Chains]] — Pass each step of an LLM chain in as a function argument whose default is the real call, so one step can be tested against a mock while the others run and so a model swap touches one function; Lakshmanan and Hapke present it as the alternative to vibe-checking a nondeterministic pipeline.
- [[Effective Feedback Compute]] — A proposed predictor of agent-harness success that counts feedback events by informativeness, validity, non-redundancy and retention rather than raw tokens or cost; evidence is largely synthetic.
- [[Evaluator Exploits in Discovery Loops]] — When an LLM search loop is scored by code, it finds the gaps in the scorer: an irregular function that fools numerical integration, an invalid construction that outscores the optimum, an oracle that can be tricked; an exact programmatic evaluator closes the gap.
- [[GAP Graph-Based Parallel Tool Planning]] — GAP trains a 3B model to emit a dependency graph of tool calls and run the independent ones in parallel; reported gains are fewer turns and less time at a small accuracy edge, on one model and one tool, with baseline mismatches in its own tables (self-reported).
- [[GenAI Maturity Model for Agentic Systems]] — A pattern-language book orders enterprise GenAI adoption into maturity levels and later collapses them into three roadmap stages; the numbering is inconsistent across its own chapters, and no outcome is measured.
- [[Gorilla and APIBench]] — A LLaMA-7B fine-tuned on instruction-API pairs, optionally with a retrieved API document in the prompt, and the APIBench set of 1,645 ML-hub APIs scored by AST sub-tree matching; reported to hallucinate less than GPT-4 zero-shot (self-reported).
- [[Harness Continual Learning]] — Keep the model frozen and evolve the harness (interface, memory, capability map, router), committing a candidate only if it gains on the current task and loses little on held-out anchors from earlier tasks.
- [[Harness Effect on Token Economics]] — A vendor study that swapped the orchestration layer (cache-stable prefix, compaction, offload, delegation) on 22 enterprise tasks and 6 models and reported lower cost per task at about the same completion; no ablation.
- [[Harness Engineering]] — The pillar hub — Agent = Model + Harness, the four parts (acceptance baseline, execution boundary, feedback signals, rollback), why reliability compounds negatively, and the ratchet.
- [[Harness Anatomy]] — The nine harness components in three groups, why the filesystem is the foundational primitive, the layered mental model, and the causal build order.
- [[Harness Handbook Behavior-Organized Map]] — A generated, source-linked three-level map of a harness codebase plus a shared-state register view, used to find every code site a behaviour change touches before planning an edit.
- [[Harness Updating vs Harness Benefit]] — A study that separates how well a model evolves a harness (updating) from how much a harness helps a given agent (benefit); updating was flat across evolvers while benefit depended on the agent's skill loading and adherence.
- [[Harness-1 State-Externalizing Search Agent]] — A 20B search agent trained with RL inside a harness that holds the candidate pool, curated set, evidence graph and verification records outside the model; ablating harness parts at inference cuts recall without retraining.
- [[Harness-of-Harness Cross-Loop Development]] — HoH wraps an unchanged coding-agent harness in repeated Planner, Developer and QA Tester loops that carry both the code and the QA evidence from loop to loop.
- [[Harness-R1 Learned Harness Editing]] — A small trained model writes Python hook patches around a frozen agent from that agent's failure trajectories, with reward equal to the measured success change when the patch is installed.
- [[HeavySkill Heavy Thinking Skill File]] — A two-stage parallel-then-deliberate reasoning pipeline packaged as a plain-text skill file, with benchmark results that compare it against single trajectories and majority vote rather than against a harness.
- [[HERMES Dev-Primitives]] — Wrap each repository file in its own resident LLM (a Dev-Primitive), activate primitives along dependency edges, message only the affected ones, and route test failures back to the owning component; reported gains over matched harnesses at higher cost.
- [[HuggingGPT Controller LLM]] — Four-stage pipeline in which ChatGPT plans a JSON task graph, selects Hugging Face models, runs them and writes the response; the paper reports 63.08 end-to-end success on 130 requests with a GPT-3.5 controller (self-reported).
- [[Human-Agent Interaction Patterns]] — Five patterns for who calls whom between people and agents, with escalation below a confidence threshold, plan confirmation and a credential-holding proxy; the chapter's numbers are invented illustrations.
- [[InfCode Adversarial Test-Patch Refinement]] — An issue-resolution pipeline where a test generator and a code generator iterate against each other, then a selector agent filters candidate patches by running them; reports 79.4% on SWE-bench Verified, with the test generator's drift conceded.
- [[Instruction Fidelity Auditing]] — An auditor agent compares a handoff's output with the original instruction and returns pass or fail, with persistent anchors and a fractal reflection template as companions; a design from a pattern book with no measurements.
- [[JAZ Invoke Loop]] — A minimal agent loop in which the model writes code, subagents are recursive calls, and all inputs and history are REPL variables, so long recall is code-searching the raw history instead of using a memory tool.
- [[JIT-Agent Just-in-Time Harness Evolution]] — A trained 27B generator writes a task-specific harness (memory, planning, capability and action modules) for each instance at inference time, with training rewarding quality, latency and cost together.
- [[KnowAgent Action Knowledge Base]] — Planning method that writes a hand-built action set and allowed-transition graph into the agent prompt and fine-tunes on rule-filtered self-generated trajectories; reported to cut invalid actions and beat ReAct prompting on HotpotQA and ALFWorld (self-reported).
- [[LIFE-Harness Runtime Interface Adaptation]] — Four runtime layers (environment contract, procedural skills, action realisation, trajectory regulation) are evolved by a stronger LLM around a frozen small solver, so the interface is adapted instead of the model.
- [[Llama Guard]] — Llama Guard is a Llama2-7b model instruction-tuned to label user prompts or model responses safe or unsafe against a taxonomy supplied in the prompt, so the taxonomy can be changed by prompting or light fine-tuning (self-reported).
- [[Meta-Harness End-to-End Optimization]] — An outer-loop search where a coding agent reads the source, scores and raw traces of every earlier harness candidate from a filesystem and proposes the next single-file harness.
- [[MetaGPT SOP Role Agents]] — MetaGPT encodes a software team's standard operating procedures as five role agents that exchange structured documents through a shared message pool, with executable feedback for code; it reports HumanEval 85.9 and MBPP 87.7 Pass@1 and wins over ChatDev on its own benchmark (self-reported).
- [[Model or Harness Failure Taxonomy]] — Label an agent failure by the interaction edge where it starts and the fault side that needs repair (model, harness, environment), taking the earliest unrecovered failure; 41 modes, validated on 40 worked examples with LLM judges.
- [[Natural-Language Agent Harnesses]] — Write harness policy (stages, state, acceptance) as a natural-language document that a runtime executes, while code keeps exact mechanisms; compared with code and plain-prompt harnesses on three benchmarks, with module ablations.
- [[NIST Adversarial ML Taxonomy]] — A NIST report that classifies attacks on predictive and generative AI by what the attacker wants, controls and knows, and lists mitigations for each class with their weaknesses.
- [[OpenDev Terminal Coding Agent]] — A single-author engineering report on a terminal coding agent: scaffolding versus harness, staged context compaction, tool-output offloading, lazy MCP discovery, system reminders, doom-loop detection and schema-level safety; design constants with no benchmark.
- [[OpenHands Generalist Agent Platform]] — An open platform with an event stream, Docker runtime, skills library and agent delegation, plus a follow-up that gave the base agent a few general tools and raised GAIA results.
- [[OS-Copilot FRIDAY Tool Repository]] — A generalist computer agent with four control methods behind one runtime, a tool repository the agent grows by writing Python tools gated by a critic score, and a DAG planner.
- [[Paper-Replication Completion Gate]] — A coding-agent skill that makes "done" a state of a persistent workspace, checked by validators outside the agent, instead of the agent's final message; shown on 12 runs over 4 papers with no baseline.
- [[Play2Prompt]] — Play2Prompt has an LLM play with each tool zero-shot, then uses what it observed to write demonstrations and rewrite the tool's documentation; it raised BFCL Executable accuracy for all six task models tested, by 3 to 7 points (self-reported).
- [[PreAct]] — PreAct compiles a computer-using agent's first successful run into a state-machine program that later runs replay without per-step LLM calls, storing a program only if a re-run from a clean state passes the benchmark evaluator; warm replays ran 8.5 to 13 times faster on WebArena (self-reported).
- [[Prime Agent State Hierarchy]] — A harness that splits agent state into four levels (weights, active context, a persistent REPL with recursive subagents, disk-backed history and skills) with standardised persistence, recovery and budgeted autonomous loops.
- [[Procedural Graphs]] — A procedural graph stores an agent's learned workflow as a small editable graph whose local neighbourhood is turned into guidance for a ReAct solver, and a gated loop evolves it from success and failure traces.
- [[Robustness and Fault-Tolerance Pattern Ladder]] — A five-level ladder of 17 resilience patterns for agent systems, from hardcoded behaviour to mesh defence, with a retry-restart-fallback-escalation chain; the code contradicts the prose in several places.
- [[Routine Structured Plan for Tool Calls]] — Routine gives an agent an explicit step list that names the tool for each step, which lifts step-level tool-call accuracy in one HR scenario by fixing tool selection (Qwen2.5-7B 14.9 to 49.7, best results up to 99.3, self-reported); the result tests plan quality, not planning against ReAct (self-reported, preprint).
- [[Runtime-Independent Persistent Agents (RIPA)]] — RIPA defines a persistent agent as an identity, memory and executable-body triple that outlives the model, harness and server running it, and moves between them through a six-phase fenced migration; evidence is a handful of case studies on one agent lineage (self-reported).
- [[SALE Strategy Auction]] — SALE has each agent in a pool of different-sized models bid with a short plan, scores bids by plan cost against a peer-jury value, and refines losing bids from memory; on two domains it beat the largest single agent by 2.7 to 3.5 pass@1 points at 25% to 42% lower cost per million tokens (self-reported, one model family).
- [[SAT Self-Organizing Agent Teams]] — SAT learns a bank of team strategies offline from a few dozen problems, then runs a frozen bank of up to ten multi-phase strategies across a three-model team and a judge; on math it scored 66.7% against 48.8% for the best member (self-reported), with compute only approximately matched and the cost never reported.
- [[SeeAct Grounding Strategies]] — SeeAct splits a web task step into planning in text and then grounding the plan to a page element; three grounding strategies are compared, with textual choice lists beating image annotation and attribute matching, and a gap to oracle grounding that is self-reported at 13.3 points online.
- [[Self-Improvement Flywheel]] — A four-stage loop (generate, evaluate, learn, deploy) with the R5 operational model, planner-plus-scorer, synthetic data and coevolution, from a pattern book whose cost figures are uncited.
- [[Skill Verification Levels and the Biconditional Check]] — Metere's design treats a skill as untrusted code until verified, gates human approval by a fixed four-level verification enum, and checks that the world matches the approved-and-executed audit log (the biconditional); the OSS runtime reached 76.3% recall at 0.0% false positives on LLM-generated payloads in the author's own harness (self-reported).
- [[SkillWeaver Compositional Skill Routing]] — SkillWeaver routes a multi-skill query by decomposing it, retrieving skills per sub-task and composing them; its Skill-Aware Decomposition (SAD) step re-decomposes with retrieved skill names as hints, lifting exact step-count accuracy from 51.0% to 67.7% on the authors' CompSkillBench (self-reported).
- [[SoL-Pi Auto-Research Harness]] — SoL-Pi uses an auto-research loop over 535 environments to keep four token-saving harness mechanisms, with search and held-out evaluation kept apart; the stack cuts tokens and cost at a modest score loss.
- [[Stagewise Recoverability Harness Granularity]] — A theory that a harness has a workflow part and a guidance part, that more structure is not monotonically better, and that scaffolding should stop once the agent can recover on its own, with thin real-benchmark support.
- [[Stochastic-Deterministic Boundary Runtime Patterns]] — A position paper that names the contract between an LLM proposer and a deterministic verifier, commit step and typed reject signal as the core of a production agent runtime, with six composable patterns.
- [[SWE-agent Agent-Computer Interface]] — SWE-agent gives a language model a purpose-built command interface (windowed file viewer, linted edit, capped search, collapsed history) over a shell; ablations show interface design moves SWE-bench scores without changing weights.
- [[SwiftSage Fast-Slow Agent]] — SwiftSage pairs a small behavior-cloned module that acts by default with a prompted large model that plans only when a hand-written heuristic says the small one is stuck, and reports higher ScienceWorld scores at far fewer tokens per action than ReAct, Reflexion and SayCan (self-reported).
