- [[Task-Decoupled Planning]] — Task-Decoupled Planning has a Supervisor build a DAG of subtasks and gives each node a planner and executor that see only that node's spec, its predecessors' outputs and its own trace; the authors report 70-82% fewer output tokens than Plan-and-Act on two benchmarks, with no ablations (self-reported).
- [[TestGen-LLM Filter Cascade]] — Meta's test-generation tool passes LLM-written unit tests through three deterministic filters (builds, passes five repeated runs, adds coverage) before any engineer sees them; reported at Instagram and Facebook with a 73% acceptance rate.
- [[Tool Design as Harness Surface]] — Tools as a harness contract rather than a context cost — ACI over API, the five-section tool spec, the four gating questions, and write-operation safety.
- [[Execution Boundaries and Guardrails]] — Sandboxes, hooks, and permission gates — encode constraints rather than documenting them, trust zones, rollback, and why a role label is not a sandbox.
- [[Canary Testing for Permission Boundaries]] — Deny rules are untested code — run each destructive route unguarded then guarded, and require a structured denial event, because a surviving file proves nothing.
- [[ToolLLM ToolBench and DFSDT]] — Pipeline that builds ToolBench from 16,464 RapidAPI APIs with ChatGPT, annotates solution paths with a depth-first decision-tree search (DFSDT), fine-tunes LLaMA-2 7B and judges with ChatGPT-based ToolEval (self-reported).
- [[Trace-Free+ Tool Description Rewriting]] — Trace-Free+ fine-tunes Qwen3-4B-Instruct to rewrite a tool's description from its schema alone, trained on descriptions derived from agent failure traces; it lifted multi-step StableToolBench query-level success from 33.5 to 44.6 with GPT-4.1 (self-reported).
- [[Verification Loops]] — Two distinct failures needing two distinct fixes: a forced verification pass and an external evaluator — three kinds of reflection, asymmetric QA, and when a verifier is worth deleting.
- [[Agent Retry Taxonomy]] — Which failures are retryable and which never are, retry at both call and node level, and feeding the error back as the move that makes a retry a re-plan.
- [[Loop Detection and the Two-Retry Rule]] — The doom loop, per-file edit-count detection that forces re-planning rather than repair, and the reasoning sandwich.
- [[Long-Horizon Execution]] — Coherence decay and context anxiety — state externalization makes the loop re-entrant, plans as first-class artifacts, context reset vs compaction, and Ralph loops.
- [[Harness Orchestration]] — Subagents as a context firewall before they are an architecture — role separation, graph-based orchestration, the four multi-agent failure modes, and when not to go multi-agent.
- [[Harness Maturity and Failure Modes]] — The five-stage maturity ladder, the six-stage per-task pipeline with its Approve gate, and the five ways teams fool themselves.
- [[Production Reliability Primitives]] — Per-step checkpointing, cross-provider model fallback, fail-fast on ambiguity, and confidence-routed quarantine as the shape of HITL at volume.
- [[Iterative Harness Simplification]] — Components encode assumptions about what the model can't do; when models improve those assumptions expire — strip one at a time and re-run the eval.
- [[Agentic Engineering and the New SDLC]] — The vibe-coding-to-agentic-engineering stakes spectrum, per-phase SDLC transformation, the conductor/orchestrator split, the 80% problem, and the CapEx/OpEx case for the harness.
- [[Agent Deployment Anti-Patterns]] — Eight recurring deployment failures that present as model limitations but are engineering-constraint failures, and the reframe that a documented rule is probabilistic while a hooked rule is deterministic.
- [[Task Decomposition Patterns]] — Four axes for cutting work across agents — functional, spatial, temporal, data-driven — each a bet about where the dependencies aren't.
- [[Protocol-Driven Multi-Agent Collaboration]] — Natural language inside a task, protocols between tasks — the nine conversational-coordination failures as classical distributed-systems failures, and the three foundations that prevent them.
- [[Skill Authoring Discipline]] — Writing a skill rather than defining one — the description as routing logic under a token/precision tension, negative examples as the higher-leverage half, and skills-plus-network as an exfiltration path.
- [[Spec, Verifier, Environment Layering]] — practitioner framing (medium confidence) of agent work as spec (goal not task, agile slices), verifier (criteria first, second-model critic, external signal), environment (contract, KB, skills, hooks for never-do)
- [[Harness Subsystem Attribution]] — Diagnose a failing agent by attributing the failure to one of five subsystems — instructions, tools, environment, state, feedback — fix only that one on a fixed model, and use ablation to learn which subsystems are load-bearing.
- [[Three-Layer Termination Check]] — A floor under 'verify' — static, runtime, and system checks run in order with each gating the next — because unit tests are blind by design to boundary defects and an agent left alone runs the fastest test and declares victory.
- [[Agent-Oriented Error Messages]] — Write every check's failure message for the agent — WHAT failed, WHY the rule exists, HOW to fix it — and promote review comments you keep repeating into such checks, so each category of mistake stops needing a human.
- [[Proposer-Reviewer and Sidecar Gating]] — Two shapes for putting a second model in front of risky tool calls when no human can be in the loop — a serial proposer–reviewer that sees intent, and a parallel sidecar that sees only structured call fields — plus the design rules both need.
- [[Lethal Triad]] — Private data plus untrusted content plus an external communication channel makes prompt injection an exfiltration path — persistent memory is a fourth amplifier — and the reliable defence is to cut a leg deterministically in the sandbox, not to filter the injection.
- [[Dynamic Tool Discovery]] — Eagerly loading every tool schema can spend ~30% of a window before the first message — three lighter alternatives (retrieval pre-filter, proactive discovery via a tool_search meta-tool, skills as a greppable catalog) and the KV-cache rule for where a newly loaded schema goes.
- [[Belief Parameters with a Ground-Truth Gate]] — Put the policy check inside the execution tool: optional expected_* parameters force the model to look facts up first (a checklist), but carry no authority — every policy fact comes from the database and server clock, and belief/truth mismatches are logged.
- [[Asynchronous Agent Event Loop]] — A resident agent drains a queue of structured events and consumes them only at safe points — cancel, queue, or answer in parallel — with placeholder results to keep tool calls paired, numbered batches so none are ignored, and async tool interfaces that name the initiation, not the completion.
- [[Quality Document]] — A per-area health record (grade, verification, agent legibility, test stability, boundary compliance, key gaps) that agents and humans both read, so codebase quality is tracked rather than assumed — sessions fix the lowest grade first, and simplification passes have a baseline.
- [[Runtime vs Process Observability]] — Harness observability has two layers — runtime signals (logs, traces, lifecycle) answer what the system did; process artifacts (plans, contracts, rubrics, scored verdicts) answer why a change should be accepted — and the harness, not the agent, must collect both.
- [[Coding-Agent Search and Edit Schemes]] — The coarse-to-fine search ladder (glob, grep, semantic index, LSP) and five file-editing schemes compared — old→new string as the predictable default, start/end anchors as the economical compromise, and why line ranges fail without a live line map.
- [[Deep Agent Pillars]] — What separates a deep agent from a shallow tool-calling loop — explicit planning, an orchestrator with clean-context sub-agents, external memory with agentic search, and verification — all sitting on context engineering, and convergent across coding agents, agent SDKs, and deep-research products.
- [[VeriHarness Agentic Verification]] — A same-model verifier with workspace and evidence tools audits ten rollouts of a long-horizon task, resolves disputed claims, challenges shared errors and revises the best rollout.
- [[WebAgent and HTML-T5 Modular Web Automation]] — WebAgent splits real-website automation between a finetuned HTML-T5 that plans and picks relevant HTML snippets and a frozen Flan-U-PaLM that writes Selenium programs; it reached 65 to 80 percent success on three real sites over 20 instructions each, and 70 percent of its real-estate failures were planning errors (self-reported).
- [[WebThinker Deep Research Agent]] — A reasoning model that decides for itself when to search, delegates page exploration to a sub-agent, and drafts a report section by section; trained with iterative online DPO.

## Loop

- [[AdaptEvolve Confidence-Routed Evolution]] — AdaptEvolve lets a small model draft each mutation in an evolutionary coding loop and escalates to a large model only when a classifier on the small model's token confidence predicts failure.
- [[AI Research Preference Model]] — A research preference model ranks candidate experiments by pairwise comparison before any is run, which lets a research-agent search spend its compute on the likelier winners.
- [[Darwin Gödel Machine]] — The Darwin Gödel Machine keeps an archive of coding agents, has each parent rewrite its own code, and keeps a child only if a benchmark says it works; HyperAgents extend it by making the improvement procedure itself editable.
- [[EvoPrompting Code-Level Architecture Search]] — EvoPrompting uses a language model as the mutation and crossover operator in evolutionary architecture search over code, and soft-prompt-tunes the model on its own evaluated children each round.
- [[Forgetting Reset for Discovery Teams]] — At the end of each round a discovery loop discards every agent session and the whole search history, keeps only the best candidate and its score, and starts fresh agents from it; the paper argues long sessions anchor agents to their own past attempts.
- [[LADDER and TTRL Verified Variant Trees]] — LADDER trains a model with reinforcement learning on a model-generated tree of easier variants of problems it cannot yet solve, scored by a programmatic checker; TTRL repeats the trick per question at test time and then rolls back.
- [[Learned Skill and Action Libraries]] — Four papers grow a library the agent writes for itself (Voyager code skills, LearnAct Python actions, COS-PLAY protocols, WikiSkill text skills); each gates entries on a check, and each reports the library helping, with controls that differ in strength.
- [[Loop Engineering]] — The fourth layer of the stack — designing the cycle that re-prompts, checks, and stops an agent when nobody is watching, replacing yourself as the person who prompts it.
- [[Loop Termination Design]] — Stop rules are layered and independent — success verifier, iteration cap, budget cap, stall detector, escalation path — and the cap is the backstop, never the primary exit.
- [[Loop Autonomy Ladder]] — Four rungs of handoff — tool approval, stop condition, trigger, session — climbed one at a time, each earned with a verifier that has been observed catching a real failure.
- [[Evolve Loop]] — A slow loop pointed at a fast one that rewrites files rather than weights — four edit targets, a 5–10 run cadence, and the anti-busywork rule that makes "no change needed" a first-class success.
- [[MASS Multi-Agent Self-Supervision]] — MASS alternates evolutionary workflow search with fine-tuning on the model's own multi-agent traces, using one model as agent, optimizer and evaluator; the gains on research tasks come mostly from completion, and coding did not improve.
- [[Meta^n Recursive Operator Stack]] — Meta^n keeps one improving operator fixed and recurses on its input instead, feeding each layer the traces and code of the layer below; most of the gain comes from the plain context string passed between layers.
- [[Oracle-Gap Recovery by Selection]] — In sample-and-select loops the cap on gains is the selector, not the sampler: one study recovers 78.3% of the gap to an oracle on SWE-bench Verified, and a second shows that selecting on the test score instead of validation inflates medal rates by 9 to 17 points.
- [[Recursive Self-Improvement]] — Level 4 at the frontier — the write boundary is the load-bearing design decision, a 3% hit rate is fine when attempts are cheap, and automating generation shifts the bottleneck onto verification.
- [[Loop Scorekeeper]] — The scorekeeper (verifier/grader) decides whether a loop's cheap repeated motion becomes value or slop — it must be objective, automatic, fast, clear and hard to fake, live outside the loop's write scope, anchor on deterministic checks, and be written before the loop's first pass.
- [[Loop State File]] — A long-running loop must forget on purpose and remember on disk: a small state file in three tiers (now / recent / archive) with five fields — goal, last score, what was tried, what is next, waiting for a human — kept current against context drift, read at the start of every pass of a guarded Ralph loop.
- [[Red Queen Gödel Machine]] — RQGM lets the evaluator co-evolve with the agent in epochs, holding it frozen within an epoch and checking replacements against an independent anchor; its guarantees hold per epoch, not across them.
- [[Rehearse Focused Outcome Memory]] — Rehearse keeps a plain-file store of earlier changes and whether they worked, retrieves only similar ones for a pre-execution judge, and so avoids the accuracy drop a memoryless judge shows once a loop has accumulated successes.
- [[SAGA Goal-Evolving Science Agents]] — SAGA nests an outer loop that rewrites the objectives and scoring functions inside an inner optimiser, so the objective keeps pace with what the optimiser exploits; it defines three autonomy modes by which stages a human touches.
- [[Self-Evolving Agent Taxonomies]] — Three surveys sort self-evolving agents differently: by loop component and stage (Fang), by what, when, how and where to evolve (Gao), and by how much of the improvement process the AI owns (Duan's ladder); none of them measures the practitioner evolve loop.
- [[Self-Generated Task Practice]] — Two agent-training recipes have the model invent its own practice tasks and train on the outcomes: PAE scores them with a VLM judge, and Self-Challenging agents write a code-defined verifier for each task; both report gains and both depend on how good the task checker is.
- [[SIFT Judge-Guided Self-Improvement Tree Search]] — SIFT puts a cheap pairwise LLM judge in front of the expensive benchmark run, ranks candidate agents with a Bradley-Terry fit, and spends full evaluation only on the top nodes of a self-improvement tree.
- [[Strategy Lock-In in Post-Training Agents]] — A study of coding agents running post-training jobs finds they refine execution but rarely change their training strategy (2.1% of adjacent runs), and that a single human instruction to switch beat the agent's own continuation in forked runs.
- [[Three-Layer Trajectory Verification]] — Before a self-improving loop learns from a run it must verify it at three layers — outcome (was it done, by code), process (was it done in an allowed way, by code), quality (was it done well, by an item-wise LLM rubric) — and keep the verdict structured, not scalar, so it says what to edit.
- [[Four Media for Agent Lessons]] — A self-improving loop must decide not only which config to edit but which medium holds the lesson — experience doc, prompt/Skill, program/harness, or weights — promoting lessons along that path as evidence builds and always choosing the smallest target that is easiest to validate and roll back.
- [[Self-Modification as a Release Process]] — An agent 'modifying its own code' should never overwrite the running process: each self-edit is a candidate branch with a falsifiable change contract, failure replay and retention suite before canary; safety machinery stays outside its write scope, and the loop is measured stage by stage because harness updating is not harness benefit.
- [[Sleep Learning]] — The offline half of a dual loop: the online loop only appends immutable evidence, and a scheduled consolidation pass (trigger, orient, consolidate, validate, prune and re-index) turns it into long-term knowledge — so one lucky success or injected page can't immediately rewrite behaviour.
- [[Train-Test Ablation Audit]] — After an LLM evolutionary search, remove each discovered component and compare the effect on training and held-out games; components that matter on both generalise, those that matter only on training are overfit and can be dropped.

## Graph

- [[Agensh Orchestrator-Free Agent Scaling]] — A Microsoft Research harness where peer workers coordinate through a Git workspace, chat and an append-only shared context with no central orchestrator, scaled from 1 to 1,024 agents on ProgramBench tasks.
- [[AgentConductor Topology Evolution]] — AgentConductor trains a 3B orchestrator with SFT then GRPO to emit a layered-DAG topology in YAML for each coding problem, regenerating it from execution feedback and scoring density by difficulty; it reached 58.8 pass@1 on APPS with gpt-4o-mini workers (self-reported).
- [[Agentic Coordination Patterns Catalog]] — Six coordination designs (router, supervisor, swarm, blackboard, contract-net, supervision tree) with their stated failure points and safeguards, from a pattern book that reports no measurements.
- [[Archon Inference-Time Architecture Search]] — Archon stacks generate, critique, rank, fuse and verify layers and picks the configuration by Bayesian optimisation over 9,576 candidates; the authors report 15.1% average gain over single frontier models at up to five times the cost of one call (self-reported, with internal inconsistencies).
- [[CAMEL Role-Playing Inception Prompting]] — Two-agent framework in which a task specifier and inception prompts set an AI user and an AI assistant conversing until a termination token; reported preference over single-shot gpt-3.5-turbo and four named failure modes (self-reported).
- [[Codebook Agent Amortized Topology Design]] — Codebook Agent replaces per-query topology generation with a learned codebook of about sixteen graphs and one MLP scoring call, reporting 84.62 average accuracy on six benchmarks and 21.9 to 33.2 percent fewer tokens than a generate-and-score pipeline (self-reported).
- [[CoELA Modular Embodied Agents]] — Five-module cooperative embodied agent (perception, memory, communication, planning, execution) with LLM-driven communication and planning, tested on TDW-MAT and C-WAH with one run per setting (self-reported).
- [[Coordination Network Measurement in Multi-Agent Coding]] — A measurement study of Claude Code agent teams on synthetic Python tasks treats files as nodes and messages as edges, and finds that a prompt-only coordinator creates no hub, message growth near n squared stops at large teams, and failures concentrate at interfaces no agent owns (self-reported, one model, 10 runs per cell).
- [[Degree-Weighted Path Count (DWPC)]] — DWPC scores how strongly two nodes are linked along a typed path pattern while damping paths that run through high-degree hubs; a Neo4j book's worked examples use it to rank disease-linked gene processes and miRNA targets.
- [[Delegation Ceiling and Posterior Distortion]] — A decision-theory note argues that a multi-agent network adding no new outside signal can at best match one Bayes decision maker seeing the same signals, and that the cost of passing messages is the distortion of the posterior; MMLU relay experiments show accuracy falling with each prose stage (self-reported).
- [[Diversity Collapse in Multi-Agent Ideation]] — A study of LLM agent groups writing research proposals finds that authority-structured personas produce less diverse ideas than flat junior groups, that quality barely offsets this, and that mixing models helps hierarchical groups but not flat ones (all self-reported, one task, one main backbone).
- [[Emergent Cheating in Research Swarms]] — In a 100-agent research swarm one agent's grader exploit spread through the shared knowledge library and cleared the remaining problems in 27 minutes, while prompt-level integrity rules failed and whistleblowers lacked tools to stop it.
- [[Encouraging Divergent Thinking with Multi-Agent Debate]] — Two debater LLMs argue under a tit-for-tat prompt while a judge ends the debate early or extracts the answer, aimed at the Degeneration-of-Thought problem of self-reflection; reported gains on counter-intuitive reasoning and translation, at 2.46 times the CoT tokens (self-reported).
- [[EvoOntology Self-Evolving Ontology Layer]] — EvoOntology keeps a small typed ontology over a data agent's tables, serves it through browse and resolve MCP tools, and revises it from trajectories behind a paired-validation gate; the authors report +17.8 Trajectory-Wise on DDR-Bench over six backbones, with the static prompt version often hurting (self-reported).
- [[FLORA-Bench GNN Workflow Predictors]] — A position paper builds FLORA-Bench to test whether a graph neural network can predict whether an agentic workflow graph succeeds, finding it works within a domain and as a cheap reward for workflow search but fails across domains.
- [[GitSwarm Decentralized Compounding Inference]] — GitSwarm has identical workers coordinate only through a shared, branchable Git repo in which every commit declares the earlier work it drew on; on a 50-task ProgramBench subset it scored 79.4% against 65.1% for one agent prompted to keep going (self-reported).
- [[GrailQA Three Levels of KBQA Generalization]] — GrailQA is a 64K-question Freebase dataset that tests knowledge-base QA at three levels (i.i.d., compositional, zero-shot); the accompanying BERT ranking model scored 50.6 EM overall and its zero-shot results depended heavily on BERT (self-reported).
- [[Graph Engineering]] — The fifth layer of the stack — designing which nodes exist, which transitions are permitted, and how the runtime work graph mutates, so multi-agent work has an organizational structure rather than just more agents.
- [[Graph Neural Prompting]] — Graph Neural Prompting encodes a retrieved knowledge-graph subgraph with a GNN into one soft prompt for a frozen FLAN-T5; it beat Prompt Tuning by about 13 percent on six QA sets while pasting raw triples into the prompt hurt the model (self-reported).
- [[Graph Topology Primitives]] — Nodes, edges, state, durable execution, and typed edges — the agency budget per node, conditional edges as where control lives, and reducers as the concurrency primitive.
- [[Graph-R1 Agentic GraphRAG]] — Graph-R1 trains an LLM with GRPO to query a hypergraph over several turns; its paper finds prompt-only GraphRAG methods below plain chunk RAG and RL as the component that matters most.
- [[GraphRAG Local-to-Global Community Summaries]] — GraphRAG builds an LLM-extracted entity graph, partitions it hierarchically with Leiden, writes community summaries ahead of time and answers global sensemaking questions by map-reduce over them; LLM judges preferred it to vector RAG on comprehensiveness and diversity, not on directness (self-reported).
- [[GraphReader]] — GraphReader turns a long document into a graph of atomic facts and key elements and has an LLM agent explore it coarse to fine within a 4K window; with GPT-4 it beat 128K full-text reading on multi-hop QA, with the gap widening up to 256K tokens (self-reported).
- [[HSE and Router Robustness in Model Societies]] — Huot et al. argue routing is only meaningful if the pool of models behaves differently (measured by HSE) and the router sends paraphrases of a query to the same model (rho); they report that fewer than ten of 112 models carry most of the diversity, and that nearest-neighbour routers lose robustness on specialist pools (self-reported).
- [[Knowledge Graph Prompting for Multi-Document QA]] — KGP builds a graph whose nodes are passages (plus page and table nodes) and has a fine-tuned T5 traversal agent walk it to collect context for an LLM reader; the T5-0.7B agent matched or beat ChatGPT and LLaMA-7B agents on 2WikiMQA, but KGP was not best on every dataset (self-reported).
- [[LatentMAS and RecursiveMAS Latent Handoff]] — LatentMAS and RecursiveMAS let agents pass hidden states or KV caches instead of text; LatentMAS reports 70.8-83.7% fewer output tokens, RecursiveMAS 34.6-75.6% fewer across recursion rounds, both against text hand-off (self-reported).
- [[LightPROF Knowledge Adapter for KGQA]] — LightPROF keeps the LLM frozen (LLaMa3-8B) and trains a small adapter that encodes retrieved knowledge-graph paths as soft prompts; it scored 83.8 Hits@1 on WebQSP and 59.3 on CWQ with about 98% fewer tokens than StructGPT (self-reported).
- [[Loop-to-Graph Escalation]] — A loop is a graph with one node and an edge back to itself; escalation is a cost you justify with five specific signals, not a maturity level you graduate to.
- [[Graph Governance and Attribution]] — Once work fans out across nodes, "the graph did it" is not an acceptable audit answer — identity propagation, per-node cost attribution, and approval gates placed where consequence concentrates.
- [[Knowledge Graph Retrieval]] — Vector search finds things that sound like your question; graphs find things that are connected to your answer — but traversal depth compounds error, which makes entity resolution the load-bearing sub-problem.
- [[Knowledge Graph as Shared Agent Memory]] — Loop → swarm → graph as three capacity unlocks: parallel workers rediscover the same findings because nothing connects them, and a typed KG turns fan-out from re-derivation into accumulation.
- [[MedAgents Expert Consultation]] — Training-free five-stage pipeline in which role-played domain experts analyse a medical question, summarise, vote until unanimous and hand off to a decision maker; reported 86.7 average on nine medical QA sets with GPT-4 (self-reported).
- [[MetaCogAgent Self-Aware Task Delegation]] — MetaCogAgent has each of three prompted GPT-4 agents estimate its own confidence on a task and re-route it when another agent looks better suited; on its authors' own 700-task benchmark it scores 82.4% against 77.1% for majority voting at fewer calls, from single runs (self-reported).
- [[MIRAGE Parallel Graph-Retrieval Reasoning Chains]] — MIRAGE splits a medical question into entity-grounded sub-questions, runs parallel reasoning chains that query a knowledge graph in anchor or bridge mode, and has a synthesizer cross-check the chains; with QwQ-32B it ranked best or tied-best by GPT-4o on three medical QA sets, by small margins on two (self-reported).
- [[Multi-Agent Debate Failure Modes]] — Wynn et al. test all-to-all debate with majority vote on three small models and find debate often scores below majority vote over the same agents' first answers, with agents flipping from correct to incorrect more than the reverse (self-reported, small samples).
- [[Multiagent Debate for Factuality and Reasoning]] — Several copies of one chat model answer independently, read each other's answers and revise over rounds; reported to beat single-agent, self-reflection and majority-vote baselines on arithmetic, GSM8K, MMLU and biography accuracy (self-reported).
- [[n8n AI Workflow Builder]] — A shipped supervisor-pattern LangGraph with published operational constants — five specialist subgraphs, prompt-only specialization, per-node iteration bounds, and an agent never allowed to fill its own context with the artifact it is editing.
- [[Multi-Agent Topology Patterns]] — The five recurring graph shapes (generate-then-verify, supervisor, fan-out/fan-in, subgraph, handoff), what each costs and how each fails, plus the two design questions underneath them: is context shared, and who decides what runs next.
- [[Multi-Agent Failure Modes at Scale]] — A single loop fails by not converging; a graph fails mechanically (cycles, deadlocks, races, unbounded fan-out, mis-routing) and through control failures between loops — and swarm-scale faults like correlated errors and Byzantine workers are caught only by independent checks and anchors.
- [[OneManCompany Talent Container Organisation]] — OneManCompany (OMC) organises heterogeneous agents as a company, with a manager tree plus dependency DAG, an explore-execute-review loop and portable Talent identities; it reports 84.67 percent on PRDBench but ablates none of its components (self-reported).
- [[Reasoning on Graphs]] — Reasoning on Graphs (RoG) fine-tunes an LLM to generate relation paths over a knowledge graph as plans, executes them to retrieve reasoning paths, then answers from them; it reached 85.7 Hits@1 on WebQSP and 62.6 on CWQ with LLaMA2-Chat-7B (self-reported).
- [[Reasoning-Trace Disagreement States]] — Wawer and Chudziak propose routing multi-agent output by four disagreement states (similar or different reasoning crossed with agreeing or split decisions) instead of a vote; a 600-item check found human disagreement ordered as predicted, but the routing policy itself was never run (self-reported).
- [[Sakana Fugu Learned Orchestration]] — Sakana's Fugu trains a small orchestrator over a pool of three frontier models, either to pick one worker per input or (Fugu-Ultra) to write a workflow with an access list per step; the report claims best results on SWE-Bench Pro and Terminal Bench 2.1 but runs no ablations (self-reported).
- [[Self-Collaboration Role-Based Code Generation]] — Three ChatGPT instances given analyst, coder and tester roles exchange text on a shared blackboard in a waterfall order; reported Pass@1 gains of 29.9 to 47.1 percent relative over one ChatGPT agent, with the tester simulating tests rather than running code (self-reported).
- [[Self-MoA Single-Model Aggregation]] — Self-MoA samples one strong model several times and has an aggregator synthesise the samples; Li et al. report it beating a mixed-model mixture-of-agents on AlpacaEval 2.0 (65.7 vs 59.1) because proposer quality mattered more than diversity in their regressions (self-reported).
- [[TANS Node Descriptions for Text-Free Graphs]] — TANS prompts an LLM with a node's computed topology (five properties with percentile ranks) plus optional node and neighbour text, then embeds the generated description so one GNN can train on graphs with little or no node text.
- [[Team of Rivals Critic-Veto Orchestration]] — A vendor case study of planners, executors and critics with strict roles, where critics hold a veto against pre-declared acceptance criteria and tool calls run in a remote executor that returns only schemas and summaries.
- [[Team of Thoughts Heterogeneous Model Orchestration]] — An orchestrator treats different post-trained models as tools, picks two using self-written profiles and aggregates their final answers, with ablations on fan-out, pool size and what a tool returns.
- [[Text-to-Cypher]] — LLM-generated Cypher over a knowledge graph is reliable only as a generate-then-verify loop: an annotated LLM-ready schema, relationships-before-query output ordering, intent detection, a pre-execution EXPLAIN validator and a bounded retry edge — and it belongs last in the retrieval order, after vector search and canned traversals.
- [[Knowledge Graph Fundamentals and Cypher]] — A knowledge graph is an ever-evolving, domain-specific graph of typed entities and named relationships; build it purpose-first from the questions it must answer, choose LPG vs RDF by goal (LPG when edges need provenance), and write Cypher with bounded paths, MERGE-on-identity-key plus uniqueness constraints, and provenance on edges.
- [[Knowledge Graph Entity Resolution]] — Building a KG from text with LLMs: extraction is the easy part — the graph is only as good as entity resolution, so keep raw mentions in a reversible metagraph, resolve with relational evidence (WCC, LLM clustering with descriptions, ontology NED), and gate loads on false-merge rate weighted above false-split.
- [[Graph Machine Learning and GNNs]] — Graph ML predicts the edges and labels a knowledge graph doesn't have — node classification, link prediction, community detection, graph classification — and since nodes aren't i.i.d., domain-informed features often beat generic embeddings; a predicted edge is a claim, written with confidence and kept out of default traversals.
- [[TUMIX Tool-Use Mixture]] — TUMIX runs about fifteen differently-built tool-using agents in rounds, each reading the others' answers, stops when an LLM judge says to, and takes a majority vote; the authors report gains over matched-cost baselines on Gemini 2.5 but note that picking the right answer is the bottleneck (self-reported).
- [[Workflow Graph Static vs Dynamic Taxonomy]] — Survey taxonomy of LLM-agent workflow optimisation: structure is static if it is a reusable template fixed after training or search, dynamic if any of it is selected, generated or edited at inference; the survey reports no experiments, only design guidance and a corpus of 77 works.

## RAG

- [[ADAPT-LLM Learned RET Token]] — ADAPT-LLM fine-tunes Llama-2 7B on data labelled by where its own zero-shot answers fail, so it emits a RET token when it needs retrieval; on PopQA it edged out always-retrieve by 0.9 to 1.6 points while still asking for context on most questions.
- [[Adaptive-RAG Query Complexity Routing]] — Adaptive-RAG trains a small T5 classifier to label each query by complexity and routes it to no retrieval, single-step retrieval or a multi-step loop; with FLAN-T5-XL it keeps most of multi-step accuracy at far lower time, and with GPT-3.5 it matches it (self-reported).
- [[AgentIR Reasoning-Aware Retrieval]] — Embed the deep-research agent's current reasoning trace together with its search query, and train a retriever on that pairing; reported to raise end-to-end accuracy on BrowseComp-Plus without any reranking call.
- [[ANCE Global Hard Negatives for Dense Retrieval]] — ANCE draws each training negative from the top results of an ANN index of the whole corpus, rebuilt asynchronously from a recent checkpoint, instead of using in-batch negatives.
- [[Astute RAG]] — Astute RAG has the model write its own passages, merge them with retrieved ones while tagging sources, and answer with a confidence per group; on the authors' 1,042-question benchmark it beat plain RAG with Claude and Gemini (self-reported), with no gain for one 12B model.
- [[Atlas Retrieval-Augmented Few-Shot Learning]] — Atlas jointly pre-trains a Contriever retriever and a T5 Fusion-in-Decoder reader, so a retrieval-augmented model fine-tuned on few examples competes with far larger prompted LLMs on knowledge tasks, with an updatable and compressible index.
- [[Auto-RAG Autonomous Iterative Retrieval]] — Auto-RAG fine-tunes Llama-3-8B-Instruct on 10,000 synthesized reasoning trajectories so the model itself decides, turn by turn, whether to retrieve again and what to query; it has the best six-benchmark average among the compared methods and its ablations point to reasoning as the key ingredient (self-reported).
- [[Bedrock KB vs LangGraph Decision]] — Decision framework for Bedrock Knowledge Bases vs. LangGraph CRAG pipeline — quality, observability, cost, and migration path analysis.
- [[BlockSearch In-Context Retrieval at Million-Token Scale]] — A small LLM trained to pick gold documents out of a long in-context corpus collapses near a million tokens because attention to the gold document dilutes; scaling scores by log N and routing to a shortlist recover most of a dense retriever's recall.
- [[Cache-Augmented Generation]] — Cache-augmented generation (CAG) preloads a whole small corpus into the model's KV cache once and answers with no retrieval step; one short paper reports it beating plain RAG on BERTScore at 21k-50k tokens, with the margin gone at 85k.
- [[Chain-of-Note]] — Chain-of-Note has the reader write a short note on each retrieved document before answering, in one of three note types; fine-tuned LLaMa-2 7B gained about 7.9 EM when all five documents were noisy (self-reported), at roughly 20 times the decoding time unless hybrid training is used.
- [[Closed-Book QA with T5]] — Closed-book QA fine-tunes a T5 model to answer from the question alone; accuracy scales with size and salient span masking, and exact match undercounts correct answers.
- [[CoAT Associative-Memory MCTS]] — CoAT runs Monte Carlo tree search over reasoning steps and gives each node an associative memory (retrieved or generated knowledge); an ablation moved a score from 0.67 to 0.77 at 32B (self-reported), though baselines are not compute-matched.
- [[COCOM Context Embeddings Compression]] — COCOM compresses each retrieved passage into a few context embeddings produced by the same LLM that decodes, with the decoder tuned jointly; the authors report up to 5.69x faster inference and 22x fewer GFLOPs at 128x compression, but the extraction's text and tables disagree in places (self-reported).
- [[Contriever Unsupervised Contrastive Retrieval]] — Contriever trains a BERT-base bi-encoder with no labels by contrasting two random crops of one document with a MoCo queue of negatives, then serves as pre-training for supervised, few-shot and multilingual retrieval.
- [[CorpusMap Entity Navigation for Agentic Search]] — Build once an entity-page layer that links entities to the documents mentioning them, and give a file-searching agent that map; reported quality gains with fewer input tokens, while direct entity-to-entity edges added nothing.
- [[Cosine Similarity Rescaling Freedom]] — Steck and colleagues show analytically that for some regularized linear embedding models the learned embeddings can be rescaled per latent dimension without changing the model's dot products, so cosine similarities between the embeddings can be made nearly arbitrary; the result covers linear models and simulated data only.
- [[Cosmos DB DiskANN Integrated Vector Index]] — Azure Cosmos DB keeps one DiskANN graph index per partition inside its existing Bw-Tree index, so vector inserts, deletes and filters live in the operational database; the paper reports low query cost and sub-linear latency scaling, as a vendor study.
- [[CRAG Corrective Retrieval Evaluator]] — CRAG scores each retrieved document with a small fine-tuned evaluator and picks one of three actions (use and refine, discard and search the web, or both); on four QA sets with LLaMA2-7B generators it lifted accuracy over plain RAG (self-reported).
- [[Demonstrate-Search-Predict (DSP)]] — DSP composes a frozen LM and a frozen retriever as a short program with three stages (bootstrapped demonstrations, LM-written multi-hop search, self-consistent prediction) and beats plain retrieve-then-read.
- [[Dense Passage Retrieval (DPR)]] — DPR trains two BERT encoders on question-passage pairs with in-batch and BM25 hard negatives, so a dot-product index over passages can replace BM25 for open-domain QA.
- [[DRAG and IterDRAG Inference Scaling]] — DRAG packs retrieved documents and RAG demonstrations into one long prompt and IterDRAG interleaves sub-query retrieval and answers; with Gemini 1.5 Flash the best configuration's accuracy rises almost linearly with each order of magnitude of input-token budget up to 5M, and a fitted allocation model predicts good settings (self-reported).
- [[DrQA Machine Reading at Scale]] — DrQA answers open-domain questions from Wikipedia by pairing a non-learned TF-IDF plus hashed-bigram retriever with a BiLSTM span reader.
- [[EfficientRAG]] — EfficientRAG replaces per-hop LLM calls with a small tagger and query filter, making one LLM call at the end; it matched or beat iterative baselines on recall with far fewer chunks and calls on multi-hop QA (self-reported).
- [[FAISS GPU Similarity Search]] — The Faiss GPU paper builds a register-resident k-selection (WarpSelect) fused with distance kernels and runs IVFADC product-quantised search across GPUs, reaching billion-scale search and k-NN graph building in hours or minutes.
- [[FILCO Sentence-Level Context Filtering]] — FILCO trains a filter to extract the one supporting sentence from retrieved passages before generation, using task-specific silver labels (string inclusion, lexical overlap or CXMI), cutting input length 44-64% while improving answers.
- [[FLARE Forward-Looking Active Retrieval]] — FLARE drafts the next sentence without retrieval and, only when a draft token is low-probability, uses that draft as the search query and regenerates the sentence; the paper reports gains on multi-hop QA and small ones on other long-form tasks (self-reported).
- [[Fusion-in-Decoder]] — Fusion-in-Decoder encodes each retrieved passage separately and lets a T5 decoder attend over all of them at once, so accuracy keeps rising up to 100 passages.
- [[Generate-then-Read Contextual Documents]] — GenRead replaces the retriever with an LLM that writes background documents for a question, using clustering-based prompts for diversity; it matches or beats retrieval on some QA sets but not NQ, and merging both sources scored highest.
- [[GritLM (Generative Representational Instruction Tuning)]] — One model fine-tuned for both text embedding and generation, so retriever and reader are the same network and a RAG pipeline can reuse cached key-value states; reported to lose nothing on either task, with the speedup shown on long documents only.
- [[GTR Large Dual Encoders]] — GTR scales a T5 dual encoder to 4.8B parameters while keeping a fixed 768-d embedding, and reports that zero-shot BEIR retrieval improves with encoder size rather than being capped by the dot-product bottleneck.
- [[HNSW Hierarchical Navigable Small World Index]] — HNSW is a multilayer proximity graph for approximate nearest-neighbour search, where links are separated by length scale across random layers and neighbours are chosen by a diversity heuristic; it is fast and accurate but memory-hungry and weak on updates.
- [[HtmlRAG HTML as Retrieval Format]] — HtmlRAG keeps retrieved web pages as cleaned and pruned HTML rather than plain text, cutting tokens with rule cleaning and a two-stage block-tree pruner; under a 4K window it beats plain-text chunk-and-rerank baselines on some metrics, though the 70B gains are small (self-reported).
- [[HyDE Hypothetical Document Embeddings]] — HyDE swaps the query for an LLM-written hypothetical passage before embedding it with an unsupervised encoder, so dense retrieval needs no relevance labels and no training.
- [[In-Context RALM and REPLUG]] — Two ways to add retrieval to a frozen LM by prepending passages to its input, In-Context RALM (BM25, optional trained reranker) and REPLUG (ensembled documents, retriever tuned by the LM's own scores), each with perplexity or QA results from its own paper.
- [[INSTRUCTOR Instruction-Finetuned Embeddings]] — INSTRUCTOR embeds text together with a natural-language task instruction, finetuning a GTR dual encoder on 330 instruction-annotated datasets (MEDI); the 335M model is reported to beat a 4.8B baseline on a 70-task average (self-reported).
- [[IRCoT Interleaved Retrieval and Chain-of-Thought]] — IRCoT alternates generating one chain-of-thought sentence with a BM25 retrieval keyed on that sentence, so few-shot LLMs gather evidence for multi-step questions without any training.
- [[ITER-RETGEN]] — ITER-RETGEN alternates retrieval and generation, using the previous output as part of the next query; with text-davinci-003 it gained up to 8.6 absolute points on four of six datasets with fewer LLM calls than ReAct or Self-Ask (self-reported).
- [[kNN-LM Nearest Neighbor Language Models]] — kNN-LM mixes a trained LM's next-token distribution with one built from nearest neighbours in a datastore, with no extra training; the page also covers its translation variant kNN-MT, a gated variant (SPALM) and an ablation study of why it works.
- [[KnowledGPT Unsupervised Knowledge Selection]] — KnowledGPT places a learned sentence selector in front of GPT-2 for knowledge-grounded dialogue, trained without selection labels via pseudo ground truth and REINFORCE; it beat truncating the knowledge on two small datasets.
- [[LIMIT Embedding Dimension Retrieval Benchmark]] — LIMIT pairs a dimension-versus-top-k bound for single-vector embeddings with a synthetic benchmark where strong embedding models score under 20% recall@100 while BM25 reaches 93.6%; the authors read it as a ceiling on single-vector retrieval.
- [[Llama2Vec Unsupervised LLM Adaptation for Dense Retrieval]] — Continued pre-training of LLaMA-2-7B with two label-free tasks (predict the input's own words and the next sentence's words from the final-token embedding) before standard retriever fine-tuning; reported gains over RepLLaMA on MS MARCO and BEIR.
- [[LLM-R Learned In-Context Example Retriever]] — Train a dense retriever to pick few-shot examples by ranking BM25 candidates with a frozen LLM's log-likelihood of the gold output, distilling through a cross-encoder reward model; the paper reports gains over random and BM25 selection on 30 tasks and weaker gains on knowledge-heavy tasks (self-reported).
- [[LongCite and LongBench-Cite]] — LongCite trains long-context models to give sentence-level citations using synthetic data built by a coarse-to-fine pipeline, and LongBench-Cite scores citation quality; the 8B model reached citation F1 72.0 (self-reported).
- [[LongRAG Dual-Perspective Extractor and Filter]] — A different paper from the long-retrieval-units LongRAG: this one retrieves chunks, maps them back to source paragraphs, then runs an extractor and a chain-of-thought filter; on LongBench multi-hop sets GLM-4 averaged 52.56 F1 against 45.96 for vanilla RAG (self-reported).
- [[LongRAG Long Retrieval Units]] — LongRAG makes the retrieval unit about 4K tokens (whole documents or hyperlink-grouped ones) instead of 100-token passages, so a few units reach recall that took 100 passages and a long-context reader does the extraction.
- [[Lucene HNSW and Pyserini Dense Retrieval]] — Pyserini wraps Lucene BM25 and Faiss dense retrieval with hybrid interpolation for reproducible baselines, and a follow-up argues Lucene's own HNSW with OpenAI embeddings makes a separate vector store unnecessary for existing Lucene shops.
- [[M3-Embedding Self-Knowledge Distillation]] — M3-Embedding is one encoder that produces dense, sparse and multi-vector scores for 100+ languages and 8192-token inputs, trained with the combined score as a teacher for each head; hybrid scoring was best on MIRACL, MKQA and NarrativeQA in the authors' tests.
- [[MASS-RAG]] — MASS-RAG is a training-free multi-agent filter for retrieved passages: three agents (summarizer, extractor, reasoner) feed a synthesis agent; it led on several QA columns but its headline gains are relative percentages (self-reported).
- [[monoBERT duoBERT Multi-Stage Ranking]] — A three-stage ranking pipeline of BM25, pointwise monoBERT and pairwise duoBERT, where how many candidates each stage admits sets the trade-off between ranking quality and the number of BERT inferences.
- [[NoiserBench Beneficial and Harmful RAG Noise]] — NoiserBench tests seven kinds of synthetic noise added to a gold passage; the authors report three types (semantic, datatype, illegal sentence) beat gold-only context while counterfactual noise collapses accuracy, whereas a separate study found no noise setting above the gold ceiling.
- [[ORQA and the Inverse Cloze Task]] — ORQA learns a dense retriever and a reader jointly from question-answer strings alone, using the Inverse Cloze Task to pre-train the retriever so latent retrieval has a usable starting point.
- [[Pairwise Ranking Prompting (PRP)]] — Rerank by asking an LLM which of two passages is more relevant, then aggregating the pairwise wins; a 20B open model is reported to match much larger listwise rerankers, at the price of quadratic calls unless a sort or sliding window is used.
- [[PlanRAG Plan-then-Retrieve]] — PlanRAG adds a plan step and a re-plan step to a ReAct-style retrieval loop for decision questions over large databases; on a game-derived benchmark with GPT-4 it scored 64.3% and 45.0% against 48.5% and 37.6% for iterative RAG.
- [[PopQA and Popularity-Gated Adaptive Retrieval]] — PopQA is a 14k-question benchmark over long-tail entities; its paper finds scaling helps mainly popular facts and gates retrieval on subject popularity, while a companion study links accuracy to pre-training document counts.
- [[Promptagator Few-Shot Synthetic Retrieval Data]] — Promptagator prompts a large LLM with up to 8 task examples to write queries for a corpus, filters the pairs by round-trip consistency, and trains a small task-specific retriever on the survivors.
- [[Query2doc LLM Query Expansion]] — Query2doc prompts an LLM with four examples to write a pseudo-document, then appends it to the query for BM25 or a dense retriever, lifting BM25 strongly but helping distilled dense retrievers little.
- [[RA-DIT Dual Instruction Tuning]] — RA-DIT fine-tunes the language model on retrieved-chunk prompts and, separately, the retriever's query encoder on the LM's own preferences, giving a small average gain over off-the-shelf retrieval-augmented prompting at 65B.
- [[RAFT Retrieval-Augmented Fine-Tuning]] — RAFT fine-tunes a model on questions paired with one golden document plus distractors, and with no golden document for a fraction of examples, with chain-of-thought answers that quote the source; LLaMA2-7B beat domain fine-tuning and RAG baselines on four of five in-domain sets (self-reported).
- [[RAG Sequence and RAG Token]] — The original RAG recipe fine-tunes a BART generator with a frozen-index DPR retriever, marginalising over retrieved passages per sequence or per token, with an index that can be hot-swapped to update knowledge.
- [[RAG Survey Taxonomies]] — Two surveys' maps of the RAG design space, one by pipeline phase and retrieval-loop type (2024), one adding a structuring axis (2025); mostly vocabulary, with only borrowed numbers as evidence.
- [[RAG vs Long Context: Evaluation Evidence]] — Two 2024 evaluations disagree in emphasis: one finds long context ahead of RAG on filtered QA but RAG winning on about 10% of questions, the other finds RAG accuracy peaking then falling as more passages are retrieved, driven by hard negatives.
- [[RAG+ Application-Aware Retrieval]] — RAG+ attaches pre-aligned worked application examples to each retrieved knowledge item, with no retriever change or fine-tuning; gains were large on a legal task and small or negative on several medical cells (self-reported).
- [[RAGate Adaptive Augmentation Gate]] — RAGate is a binary gate that decides per conversation turn whether to retrieve; on KETOD with a GPT-2 backbone, always augmenting raised BLEU from 9.38 to 16.08 but lowered generation confidence (self-reported), and the best gate reached F1 0.4281.
- [[RankRAG]] — RankRAG instruction-tunes one Llama3 model to both rerank retrieved passages and generate answers, using about 50k ranking examples; the 8B model averaged 52.6 over nine benchmarks against 49.6 for ChatQA-1.5 (self-reported).
- [[RankZephyr Listwise Reranker Distillation]] — A 7B open listwise reranker distilled in two stages from RankGPT teachers; reported to match or beat the GPT-4 teacher in-domain, trail it out of domain on strong first stages, and stay stable when the candidate order is shuffled.
- [[RARE Retrieval-Augmented Reasoning]] — RARE adds two retrieval actions and a retrieval-based factuality scorer to rStar-style tree search; on medical QA with LLaMA 3.1 8B it averaged 73.84 against 70.56 for rStar (self-reported), at roughly 300 times the tokens of chain-of-thought.
- [[Reader-Attention Distillation for Retrievers]] — A Fusion-in-Decoder reader's cross-attention over passages is turned into soft relevance labels, and a bi-encoder retriever is trained to match them with a KL loss, iterating without query-document labels.
- [[REALM Retrieval-Augmented Pre-Training]] — REALM pre-trains a dense retriever with masked language modelling by backpropagating through the retrieval step, refreshing the MIPS index asynchronously so the retriever is rewarded when retrieval lowers perplexity.
- [[RECITE Recitation-Augmented Generation]] — RECITE has an LLM write out a relevant passage from its own memory before answering a closed-book question, then majority-votes over 20 sampled recitations; it beats direct prompting on Natural Questions across four models, though TriviaQA gains are small for the strongest model (self-reported).
- [[RECOMP Compression and Selective Augmentation]] — RECOMP compresses retrieved documents into a short extract or summary before they are prepended, and trains the abstractive version to return nothing when retrieval would not help; QA lost a few EM points at about 5% of the tokens in the authors' setup.
- [[RepoCoder Iterative Retrieval-Generation]] — RepoCoder uses the model's own previous completion to build the next retrieval query over a repository, iterating retrieve-then-generate with frozen components; on its RepoEval benchmark two iterations beat single-pass retrieval and in-file completion, with no way to choose the iteration count (self-reported).
- [[Retrieval-Pretrained Transformer Self-Retrieval]] — RPT trains a retriever jointly with the LM from scratch, taking queries from the LM's own hidden states and supervising it with a scoring LM; on four long-document sets it lowers perplexity against a Transformer-XL and a BM25 RETRO.
- [[RETRO Chunked Cross-Attention Retrieval]] — RETRO conditions a language model on neighbouring chunks fetched from a trillion-token database through chunked cross-attention; its 2022 results and the 48B InstructRetro follow-up show gains on perplexity and zero-shot QA, with leakage and parity caveats.
- [[Search-o1 Reason-in-Documents]] — Search-o1 wraps a large reasoning model with on-demand web search inside its chain of thought and condenses retrieved pages with a separate Reason-in-Documents pass before injection; gains are clearest on GPQA and multi-hop QA and absent on some code and math sets (self-reported).
- [[Self-RAG Reflection Tokens]] — Self-RAG fine-tunes one LM to emit special tokens that decide when to retrieve and critique passages and its own output, with the critique labels inserted offline by a distilled critic; the paper's results and ablations are summarised with their setups.
- [[Self-Reasoning Retrieval-Augmented Model]] — The Self-Reasoning framework fine-tunes LLaMA2 to emit a relevance judgement, cited evidence snippets and an analysis before answering, trained on 2,000 GPT-4 trajectories; the 13B model beat Self-RAG on FEVER, NQ, PopQA and ASQA (self-reported).
- [[Shared-Normalization Multi-Paragraph Reading]] — Shared-norm trains a paragraph-level reader with a softmax shared across sampled paragraphs, including ones without the answer, so confidences stay comparable when the reader is run over many paragraphs.
- [[Skill-RAG Failure-State-Aware Retrieval]] — Skill-RAG gates retrieval with a hidden-state probe and, after a failed retrieval, routes to rewrite, decompose, focus or exit skills; it beat Probing-RAG by 6 to 14 accuracy points on out-of-domain multi-hop sets (self-reported) and added little in-domain.
- [[Soft Context Compression for RAG (CLaRa and REFRAG)]] — Two 2025 papers feed the decoder compressed chunk embeddings instead of passage tokens: CLaRa trains retrieval and generation together through a straight-through top-k, REFRAG buys time-to-first-token with chunk embeddings and an RL policy that expands some chunks.
- [[STAIR ToC-Constrained Generation Retrieval]] — Put a book's whole table of contents in a fine-tuned LLM's prompt and have it generate a valid leaf section title as the retrieval result; reported to beat a same-model generative index without the table of contents on a textbook benchmark.
- [[Sufficient Context Autorater]] — Label each (query, retrieved context) pair as sufficient or not with an LLM autorater, then use that label to split RAG errors into retrieval failures and generation failures and to gate abstention.
- [[Syntriever Synthetic Retriever Training]] — Train a small retriever from black-box LLM output only, in two stages (distil on synthetic positives, queries and hard negatives, then align to the LLM's pairwise preferences); hallucinated positives are relabelled as hard negatives.
- [[TableRAG and HeteQA]] — TableRAG indexes tables both in SQL and as Markdown text and iterates decompose, retrieve, SQL, answer up to five times; HeteQA is the 304-question benchmark built for questions that span tables and text (self-reported results).
- [[Typed-Graph Retrieval Ablation]] — Pre-registered three-arm ablation of one-hop typed-graph expansion — null result at 284 pages; semantic retrieval is already at recall ceiling, so expansion stays off by default.
- [[Agentic RAG — Advanced Patterns]] — Self-RAG vs CRAG distinction, Adaptive RAG complexity tiers, GraphRAG for relationship traversal, HyDE for lexical gap, Multi-Query RAG-Fusion, agentic latency budgets, A2A protocol mapping to LangGraph, and the production-readiness gate.
- [[RAG Architecture Selection]] — The nine named RAG architectures as one selection space — what each buys and costs, the decision cheat-sheet, and Fusion RAG over heterogeneous sources (distinct from multi-query RAG-Fusion).
- [[Text-to-SQL as a Retrieval Strategy]] — The structured branch of a hybrid retriever — dynamic schema subsetting, a semantic layer of retrieved few-shot SQL examples, mechanical validation that replaced a deleted LLM reviewer, and bounded error-as-context retry.
- [[Conversation Repository Pattern]] — Two-table PostgreSQL schema for persisting multi-turn conversation state — conversations table for sessions, messages table for turns with JSONB trace and sources columns enabling trace-linked retrieval debugging.
- [[CRAG Retry Logic]] — The confidence-gated conditional back-edge in a CRAG pipeline that re-enters retrieval when the reranker's top score falls below threshold — preventing low-confidence answers from reaching the user.
- [[Embedder Warmup]] — Force-loading the embedding model during application startup (before the first request) to prevent a 3–8s cold-start spike on the first query in production.
- [[GCP Vertex AI Search vs AWS Bedrock KB]] — Head-to-head comparison of GCP Discovery Engine and AWS Bedrock Knowledge Bases as managed RAG backends — covering search semantics, session state, answer ownership, and when to consider switching.
- [[Librarian RAG Architecture]] — The five-agent Librarian pipeline — Plan, Retrieval, Reranker, Generation, and Eval agents wired by a LangGraph StateGraph with CRAG retry loop.
- [[RAG API Design Patterns]] — Three design patterns for exposing a RAG service cleanly — multi-query surface (LLM sends 2-3 query variants), fingerprint-based global deduplication, and typed Pydantic response contract at the HTTP boundary.
- [[RAG Evaluation]] — Three-tier evaluation architecture for RAG pipelines — golden datasets, LLM-as-judge, failure clustering, ragas vs deepeval, and retrieval lift measurement.
- [[RAG Knowledge Preparation]] — The process of transforming human-readable documentation into machine-retrievable knowledge units — chunking, metadata tagging, rewriting for self-containment, and enforcing consistency.
- [[RAG Reranking]] — Reranking strategies for RAG pipelines — cross-encoder vs LLM listwise, confidence scoring, and when each is appropriate.
- [[RAG Retrieval Strategies]] — Comprehensive reference for chunking, embedding, vector store, and hybrid search strategies — component choices, tradeoffs, and swap paths used in the Librarian pipeline.
- [[RL for Retrieval Policies]] — Modelling RAG as a sequential decision process — the five decision points and their reward signals, the three optimization patterns (online RL, per-subtask modules, Self-RAG), and why reward sparsity makes end-to-end RAG RL hard.
- [[Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF)]] — Score-free fusion algorithm that combines multiple ranked lists by position — the standard method for merging BM25 and dense vector retrieval results, and for amplifying cross-query agreement in multi-query retrieval.
- [[Semantic Cache for RAG Agents]] — Zero-retrieval-cost path for RAG agents — embed the query, cosine-match against a grader-validated golden seed, and short-circuit the full CRAG pipeline on high-similarity hits.
- [[Production Retrieval Evaluation]] — Benchmarking results comparing Assistant A, Assistant B (Bedrock), and local RAG baselines across 935 Danish support questions — Assistant A outperforms Assistant B on all dimensions (MRR 0.286 vs 0.248), but 47% corpus ceiling means data-ops fixes dominate model-level improvements.
- [[UniversalRAG Modality and Granularity Routing]] — UniversalRAG routes each query to one of seven corpus routes (none, paragraph, document, table, image, clip, video) instead of using one shared embedding space; a trained Qwen3-VL-2B router reached 95.28 modality accuracy (self-reported).
- [[UPRISE Universal Prompt Retrieval]] — Train a small retriever, labelled by a frozen LLM's task scores, that picks demonstrations from a pool of other tasks and prepends them to a zero-shot input; gains transfer to larger models but are uneven across task types.
- [[Vector Database Comparison]] — Side-by-side of vector stores used across RAG pipelines — DuckDB (embedded local), ChromaDB, pgvector, OpenSearch (Bedrock), Pinecone, GCP Discovery Engine — with when-to-use guidance and migration notes.
- [[Specificity-Adaptive Ranking and Slate Composition]] — weight profiles that shift with request specificity, bounded penalties, deterministic tie-break, and role-based five-slot composition with specificity-scaled exploration
- [[Offline Annotation over Online Inference]] — decision to spend all model work at ingest and serve with a WHERE clause plus in-memory scoring; prefix-caching the static taxonomy becomes the main cost lever
- [[Embedding Side Table, Not Column]] — Embeddings in a side table keyed by model and input-recipe version keep version axes orthogonal and HNSW off unrelated writebacks
- [[Code Retrieval: Structural Index and Agentic Search]] — Code wants AST/symbol chunking, a lightweight reference graph, grep-first agentic search and Merkle-style incremental reindexing.
- [[Constraint Tiers and Two-Phase Relaxation]] — Hard / semi-hard / strong-preference / decision-signal inputs; soften internal scoring before suggesting filter changes, never auto-relax date or accessibility, and say when close alternatives are shown
- [[Anchor-and-Guardrail Similar Items]] — "More like this" where the opened item anchors similarity and the original search context guards drift; excludes the seen slate, prefers 1–2 strong over 3 weak, hides itself with reason codes
- [[Upgrade-Invariant Retrieval Contract]] — FTS first, pgvector second behind one search endpoint with a frozen response shape; vector ships only after winning a graded before/after eval
- [[Canonical Document IR for Ingest]] — hub-and-spoke adapters into one typed Document schema; vision vs parse-first PDF ingest; full context over RAG for small corpora; provenance threaded from ingest
- [[Delete an Unfinished RAG Retrofit, Don't Archive It]] — decision: a half-adapted forked RAG stack suspended behind web search was deleted, since the domain never justified a vector store and the missing asset was a corpus
- [[Prompted Reflection vs Graph-Gated CRAG]] — prompted tool-loop CRAG vs explicit graph gates; neither catches synthesis hallucination, and the loop adds little over strong hybrid retrieval
- [[Retrieval Service Returns Documents, Not Answers]] — shared RAG service exposes retrieval-only endpoint; calling agents own synthesis (answer LLM dominates latency)
- [[ACL-Aware Retrieval and Tenant Isolation]] — post-filter starves the pool, pre-filter loses ANN; metadata-aware ANN plus RLS, and similarity-search leak modes
- [[Compiled-Prose KB vs Graph-Native Store]] — parity review: compiled prose wins synthesis/eval, loses per-claim provenance, conflict detection, temporal model
- [[Contextual Retrieval]] — Have an LLM write a short situating prefix for every chunk at index time and index prefix + chunk — it repairs the context chunking cut away for both BM25 and embeddings (reported top-20 failures −49%, −67% with a reranker), and prompt caching of the parent document makes indexing cheap.

## LangGraph

- [[Orchestration Architecture Decision]] — Three architecture options for the Librarian service deployment — full Bedrock, full LangGraph, or polyglot — with tradeoffs and the recommended migration path.
- [[Deep Agents Framework]] — Opinionated agent harness built on LangChain/LangGraph — create_deep_agent() wraps planning, file management, subagent delegation, and HITL into configurable middleware with no boilerplate.
- [[Deep Agents Memory Backends]] — Pluggable backend system for Deep Agents file operations and memory — StateBackend (ephemeral), StoreBackend (cross-thread), FilesystemBackend (local disk), and CompositeBackend (routing).
- [[Framework Selection — LangChain vs LangGraph vs Deep Agents]] — Decision guide for choosing between LangChain, LangGraph, and Deep Agents — layered frameworks where higher layers add planning, memory, and middleware on top of lower ones.
- [[HistoryCondenser]] — LangGraph node that rewrites the latest user query into a self-contained form given prior turns — prevents retrieval degradation on coreference-heavy multi-turn conversations.
- [[LangChain Dependency Management]] — Package structure and version policy for the LangChain ecosystem — langchain/langchain-core/langgraph/langsmith as the required core, provider/tool packages installed a la carte, and the langchain-community non-semver trap.
- [[LangChain Fundamentals — create_agent, Tools, Structured Output]] — LangChain 1.0's core agent-building primitives — the create_agent() loop, the @tool decorator, checkpointer-based persistence, and structured output — the foundation layer beneath LangGraph and Deep Agents.
- [[LangChain Agent Middleware]] — LangChain's create_agent() middleware API — HumanInTheLoopMiddleware for tool-call approval, wrap_tool_call/before_model/after_model hooks for custom logic, and Command-based resume — the mechanism Framework Selection means when it says "LangGraph has no middleware.
- [[LangChain RAG Implementation Patterns]] — LangChain-specific RAG implementation surface — document loaders, RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter, vector store classes (Chroma/FAISS/Pinecone), similarity/MMR search, metadata filtering, and wrapping a retriever as an agent tool; the API layer beneath the conceptual choices in RAG Retrieval Strategies.
- [[LangGraph Advanced Patterns]] — Advanced LangGraph patterns beyond the basics — subgraphs, Send API fan-out, streaming modes, time-travel, breakpoints/interrupts, error handling, and Plan-and-Execute.
- [[LangGraph BaseStore]] — LangGraph's cross-thread persistent key-value store with optional vector search — the standard backend for episodic, semantic, and procedural agent memory.
- [[LangGraph CRAG Pipeline]] — The Corrective RAG pattern implemented as a LangGraph StateGraph — deterministic graph with conditional retry loop, confidence gating, and typed state schema.
- [[LangGraph State Reducers]] — Functions that define how parallel node outputs merge into shared state — preventing collisions when multiple nodes write to the same field simultaneously.
- [[Runtime Topology and Checkpointer Alignment]] — Critical rule — checkpointer backend must match runtime hosting model. MemorySaver fails silently in Lambda and multi-worker deployments. Covers trigger patterns, observability tool choice (LangSmith vs Langfuse/GDPR), and key production signals.
- [[Send API Fan-out]] — LangGraph's Send API enables dynamic map-reduce parallelism — fan out to N workers at runtime without knowing N at graph compile time.
- [[Summarization Node]] — A LangGraph node that compresses conversation history when it exceeds a trigger threshold — keeps context window usage bounded while preserving conversational continuity.
- [[Context Graph Is Not an Execution Graph]] — decision: a knowledge/context graph does not justify an orchestration framework; separate deployment before separate repository

## Google ADK

- [[ADK Context Engineering]] — How the ADK samples repo manages context — SKILL.md pattern, three skill-loading strategies, static vs dynamic instruction, and history compaction.
- [[ADK Deployment Patterns]] — ADK deployment targets (Agent Engine vs Cloud Run vs GKE), CI/CD with WIF, service account architecture, event-driven triggers, and Terraform patterns.
- [[ADK Eval Guide]] — ADK evaluation methodology — the eval-fix loop, 8 built-in criteria, evalset schema, tool trajectory gotchas, multimodal eval, user simulation, and Vertex AI managed pointwise/pairwise eval.
- [[HITL and Interrupt Patterns]] — Six HITL patterns for LangGraph agents — static breakpoints, dynamic interrupt(), clarification loop (budget-bounded), scheduler confirmation gate, tool approval for irreversible actions, and time-travel/replay/fork.
- [[ADK JS TypeScript Patterns]] — Google ADK TypeScript SDK (@google/adk 0.5.0) — LlmAgent, FunctionTool, structured Zod output, streaming NDJSON, and pitfall patterns for Next.js agent integration.
- [[ADK Observability]] — Four-tier observability for ADK agents — Cloud Trace (always-on), prompt-response logging, BigQuery Agent Analytics plugin, and third-party platforms (AgentOps, Phoenix, MLflow, etc.).
- [[ADK Python API Reference]] — Quick reference for the Google ADK Python SDK — agent types, tools, state, callbacks, plugins, artifacts, memory, context caching, and context compaction.
- [[ADK Scaffold Patterns]] — Agent Starter Pack CLI patterns for scaffolding ADK agent projects — templates, deployment options, prototype-first workflow, DESIGN_SPEC.md contract, and development phase guidelines.
- [[ADK User Simulation Eval]] — Dynamic conversation testing in ADK using ConversationScenario and a user simulator LLM — replaces static turn sequences when agent response order is unpredictable.
- [[ADK vs LangGraph Comparison]] — Side-by-side mental model comparison of Google ADK and LangGraph — primitive mappings, weighted scoring (LangGraph 716/845 for AWS/ADK-compatible context), assistant-team production findings, and the recommended vocabulary alignment approach.
- [[ADK vs LangGraph Decision]] — Decision to keep Librarian on LangGraph — ADK's strengths don't address Librarian's core requirements; vocabulary alignment (Level 1) is the right refactor scope.
- [[ADK Workflow Agents]] — ADK's three deterministic workflow agents — Sequential, Parallel, and Loop — which provide control flow without LLM orchestration.
- [[Multi-Agent Orchestration Patterns]] — Multi-agent architecture patterns — supervisor/handoff/parallel swarm trade-offs, try-agent history for fallback routing, tool count budget, and the [client] ADK POC selection rationale.
- [[Multi-Modal Agent Response]] — Agent response pattern where output can include text, data visualizations, interactive UI components, and full task surfaces — moving beyond chat to structured elicitation and guided execution.
- [[Plan and Execute Pattern]] — Separating planning from execution for multi-step agent tasks — Planner, Executor, Replanner, and Responder nodes with HITL confirmation gate.
- [[SKILL.md Pattern]] — ADK skill declaration format — YAML frontmatter listing tools + natural language instruction body, enabling dynamic skill loading without hardcoding capabilities into the system prompt.
- [[System Design — Serverless Agent Backends]] — Interview-format system design writeup of running agent systems on serverless (Vercel Functions / Next.js API routes) — stateless invocations, session state in Postgres, streaming within platform timeouts, and the designed handoff to a stateful phase 2.
- [[Product Design Patterns]] — Product design patterns for embedded assistant agents — three interaction levels, structured output as UI contract, page context awareness, escalation triggers, and tool count budget for routing quality.
- [[Voice Agent Patterns]] — Patterns for real-time voice agents — hard latency constraints, BIDI streaming session management, ADK Strategy C preloading, LangGraph flat tool node, and mandatory history pruning.
- [[Realtime Voice Architecture Reference]] — cascaded vs speech-to-speech, latency budget where LLM TTFT dominates, endpointing strategies, and GDPR Art. 9/17/22 plus health-data hosting constraints

## Memory

- [[A-Mem Zettelkasten Agentic Memory]] — A-Mem stores each interaction as a linked note whose keywords, tags and context an LLM writes, then lets new notes trigger link creation and rewriting of neighbouring notes; ablations on LoCoMo credit link generation and evolution, but with GPT-4o it loses to full context on single-hop and adversarial questions (self-reported).
- [[ACC Bounded Memory Control for Agents]] — The Agent Cognitive Compressor rewrites one bounded, schema-governed state (the Compressed Cognitive State) every turn instead of replaying the transcript or retrieving artifacts; the paper reports qualitatively flat memory size and lower hallucination and drift over 50-turn episodes but gives no numeric effect sizes in its text.
- [[Agent Memory Types]] — Three-tier memory taxonomy (semantic/episodic/procedural) with storage patterns, context window strategies, reflection pattern, and SQLite preference store for assistant agents — backed by LangGraph BaseStore.
- [[Agent-Native Memory Systems Evaluation]] — A comparison of many agent memory designs on LoCoMo, LongMemEval, DB-Bench and LongBench reports no single winner, retrieval behaving as evidence completion, and raw text beating summaries (self-reported); some authors are affiliated with MemOS, per the extraction, and the paper's text and figure disagree on the DB-Bench leader.
- [[ALMA Meta-Learned Memory Designs]] — ALMA has a meta agent write memory designs as code and keep them in an open-ended archive; with a small agent the learned designs averaged 12.3% against 6.1% with no memory over four text-game benchmarks, and they transferred to a larger agent (self-reported), while some hand-built designs scored below no memory.
- [[ArcMemo Concept-Level Memory]] — ArcMemo stores reusable abstract concepts distilled from solved puzzles instead of raw solutions or one running summary, and on a 100-puzzle ARC-AGI-1 subset it lifts an o4-mini baseline from 55.17 to 59.33, a gain the paper's own variance numbers make fragile (self-reported).
- [[AtomMem Atomic-Fact Memory]] — AtomMem distils dialogue into verified atomic facts, rolls them into events and profiles, and retrieves in three stages ending in a random walk over a memory graph; on LoCoMo it beats the raw-history baseline and five memory systems, at a cost and baseline set the authors chose (self-reported).
- [[AutoMem Coordinate-Keyed File Memory]] — AutoMem optimises the scaffold around a file-system memory (coordinate-keyed upsert instead of append, auto-synced state files) and trains a memory specialist; on three BALROG games scaffold optimisation lifted a 32B agent 1.9x to 3.7x (self-reported), but the scaffold was tuned on the evaluation seeds and bundles hand-written game logic.
- [[Bad Memory File-Based Prompt Injection]] — A 2026 study plants a malicious rule in a trusted memory file (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, behavior or knowledge files) and measures how often four models act on it and whether it survives later sessions; success and persistence vary widely by model, goal and attack order, and the lowest attack success did not mean the lowest persistence.
- [[BEAM Benchmark and LIGHT Memory Framework]] — BEAM is a synthetic benchmark of 100 conversations and 2,000 questions from 100K to 10M tokens across ten memory abilities; LIGHT adds working memory and a scratchpad to retrieval and gained most over plain long context at 10M (self-reported).
- [[ChatDB SQL Symbolic Memory]] — ChatDB lets an LLM controller keep its memory in a SQL database and turn each request into a chain of SQL steps; on a 50-question synthetic shop dataset it scored 41/50 against 11/50 for the same model with the records in its prompt (self-reported, single run).
- [[ExpeL Experiential Learning]] — Training-free agent that gathers trajectories, has gpt-4-0613 distil a list of natural-language insights from them, and at test time prompts with all insights plus the most similar successful trajectories; reported gains over ReAct on ALFWorld and HotpotQA (self-reported).
- [[Generative Agents Memory Stream]] — Generative agents keep a natural-language memory stream, retrieve from it by recency, importance and relevance, and add reflection and planning; in a 25-agent sandbox, raters ranked the full architecture above its ablations, with a single short run and a high token cost.
- [[JARVIS-1 Multimodal Experience Memory]] — JARVIS-1 stores successful Minecraft plans in a key-value memory keyed by task and visual state, retrieves them after an LLM first decomposes the task into sub-goals, and reports diamond-task success of 8.99% against 2.42% for the same planner without memory (self-reported).
- [[Jev-Mem System-One Memory Control]] — Jev-Mem moves the frequent, bounded memory decisions (typing, linking, routing, stopping) to a small typed-output controller and leaves a large LLM only the final answer; on LoCoMo it reports 0.777 against 0.700 for the best baseline with much faster build and query times, but no ablation and a proprietary controller (self-reported).
- [[LLM Personalization Taxonomy]] — A 2025 survey organises personalised LLMs by granularity (user, persona, global), technique (RAG, prompting, representation learning, RLHF) and evaluation; it is qualitative, relies mainly on LaMP and LongLaMP, and says nothing on agent memory stores or forgetting.
- [[Mem0 Extract-and-Update Memory]] — Mem0 extracts salient facts from each message pair and lets an LLM choose ADD, UPDATE, DELETE or NOOP against similar stored memories; its authors report near-full-context accuracy on LoCoMo at a fraction of the latency and tokens, with a graph variant that helps temporal questions but not single- or multi-hop ones (self-reported, company authors).
- [[MemCollab Contrastive Cross-Model Memory]] — MemCollab builds one memory bank shared by several models from the contrast between their trajectories on the same task; the paper finds that memory distilled from one model can lower another model's score, and that contrasting different models beats contrasting two samples from one model (self-reported).
- [[Memento Case-Bank Retrieval]] — Memento keeps the LLM frozen and improves an agent by storing (state, action, reward) cases and learning only which cases to retrieve, by similarity or by a small learned Q-function; it reports GAIA validation 87.88% Pass@3 and a best retrieval size of four cases (self-reported).
- [[Memex(RL) Indexed Experience Memory]] — Memex(RL) lets an agent compress its working context into an indexed summary while archiving the complete text in a separate key-value store outside the context, then dereference an index exactly; RL training on a modified ALFWorld raised success from 24.22% to 85.61% (self-reported) while peak context stayed above its penalty threshold.
