- [[Eval Falsifiability]] — An eval that cannot fail measures nothing and fails green — test both directions (the negative case is load-bearing), make scripted providers derive output from their actual inputs, run eval at production config, validate fixture tables at load, and keep eval fakes conformant to the port.
- [[Specialist Verdict Panel]] — Agent-output eval as single-dimension specialist judges (relevance, hallucination, completeness, escalation) returning structured verdicts
- [[Generation-Under-Contract Eval Harness]] — A third eval kind that drives a producer through generate → validate → repair → fallback and measures process facts; needs a producer contract returning those facts and a scripted dry-run provider with non-degenerate spread.
- [[Answer Criteria with Named Wrong Answers]] — per-topic fail-if rubrics; wrong answers cluster into five classes worth seeding into judges and golden sets
- [[Ranker Weight Sensitivity Sweep]] — one-at-a-time ±10% weight perturbation with renormalisation, scored by rank agreement over a pool of at least 20
- [[Positive-Only Judge Fixtures Measure Recall]] — All-good judge fixtures measure recall not precision; one prompt-only calibration pass; null-on-error hid a strict-schema rejection
- [[Binary Gate Sample Power]] — Before a small model-comparison run, compute what it can detect: with per-event pass/fail gates, the chance of seeing at least one failure in N events is 1-(1-p)^N. That makes N=10-20 a gross-failure detector (it catches a 15-30% failure rate) and not evidence of parity, so the results must state that scope.
- [[Stratified Judgment Pool for a Ranker Benchmark]] — offline snapshot, data-derived strata, deliberate pool mix (top 5, ranks 6-20, near-threshold by score, per-exclusion, random), and what the first real-data run broke
- [[Offline Prompt Experiment Runner]] — frozen stratified sample, same-shaped variant registry with production as control, Proxy-proven read-only, self-healing JSONL resume, prompt identity stamps
- [[Blind Labels Judge No Prompt]] — schema-enforce the blind vs output-judgment instrument split: blind rows carry only an evidence hash, so every prompt version can be re-scored against them
- [[Prove Discovery Before Evaluating Enrichment]] — prove discovery precision, negative controls, identity, recurrence and provenance before grading enrichment; one labelled sample per route with a Wilson CI
- [[Mask Permitted Spans Before Measuring Copying]] — Mask the names and spans a generated text may legitimately copy before running a verbatim-run grader, so the threshold measures real copying
- [[Single-Call LLM Gate Weakness Audit]] — seven defect classes in a one-call LLM publish gate and the baseline → deterministic fixes → measured experiments order
- [[Small-N Reporting Floor]] — every rate carries N; <30 raw counts only, <100 INSUFFICIENT-N banner; deltas only across the same population
- [[Human Verdicts as Judge Fixtures]] — export human right/wrong labels into judge fixtures with the decision in metadata, score agreement on a hash holdout beside the human ceiling, sample disagreements back
- [[Paired Input-Perturbation Eval]] — perturb only the field a skip gate wants to ignore on real rows, diff model outputs, report material vs immaterial change within and across output buckets
- [[Own-Oracle Fixtures and the Blind Relabel Path]] — escape fixtures authored by running the function: export real rows with expected=null and observed separate, human-label from the rule, then gate at the observed rate
- [[Error Analysis (Open and Axial Coding)]] — Find failure modes by reading ~100 traces before choosing any metric — free-text note on the first thing that went wrong (open coding), group notes into 5–15 categories (axial coding), rank by frequency × cost, and build graders only for failures you have actually seen.
- [[Eval Failure Taxonomy]] — A fixed set of stable failure labels, ordered by pipeline stage, one label per fix, including the eval's own failures (context_missing) — the shared vocabulary for graders, dashboards, gate reports and bug tickets.
- [[Judge Calibration (TPR, TNR and Corrected Pass Rate)]] — Calibrate an LLM judge against 100–200 human labels split train/dev/test, report TPR and TNR separately (TNR usually matters most), benchmark against human–human agreement, and correct the observed pass rate with (p̂ + TNR − 1) / (TPR + TNR − 1).
- [[LLM Judge Biases]] — Position, verbosity, self-preference, leniency, format/authority and capability limits — the documented ways LLM judges go wrong, each with a mitigation (both-order pairwise, different model family, binary criteria with tricky-FAIL examples), plus the pairwise-vs-pointwise and Elo/Bradley–Terry choices.
- [[Grader States (Heuristic, Calibrated, Experimental)]] — Give every grader an explicit state — heuristic (deterministic code), calibrated (judge with human-agreement evidence), experimental (new or uncalibrated) — and let only the first two gate a release; behind it sits one grader interface with a requires field and a grader_version.
- [[Veto Rubric Items]] — A must-pass rubric item that zeroes the total when it fires — hallucination is the classic one — inside a hybrid scoring scheme of must-pass gates plus a weighted remainder, so fluency cannot buy off a fabricated fact and keyword stuffing cannot pad past it.
- [[Eval Error Bars and Sample Size]] — Every pass rate is an estimate — SE = √(p(1−p)/n), so on 50 tasks 80% is ±11 points; size the suite from the smallest difference you must detect, average trials within a task before computing SE, cluster SEs for grouped tasks, and correct for multiple comparisons.
- [[Paired Eval Comparison]] — Compare two versions on identical tasks via per-task differences (McNemar for binary outcomes) — only discordant tasks carry information, so pairing shrinks the error bar for free; gate on the CI of the difference, read the flipped transcripts, and treat each fix as a tiered, cost-weighted hypothesis.
- [[Eval Metric Interpretation Pitfalls]] — Ways a correct-looking eval number misleads — flat headlines hiding cancelling slice moves (Simpson's paradox), dropped errored rows shrinking the denominator, ratio metrics whose denominator moves, novelty effects — and the rule: count errors and refusals as failures or report them separately.
- [[Run-Then-Assert Eval CI]] — Split an eval job into a slow, costly RUN phase that writes results.jsonl and a fast deterministic ASSERT phase that computes CIs per slice against a stored baseline — tiered by trigger (smoke per PR, regression on prompt/tool paths, full nightly), with every gate emitting metrics, labels, worst slices and a next action.
- [[Simulated User Evaluation]] — Evaluate multi-turn agents with an LLM playing the user toward a goal — realistic only with progressive information disclosure (known facts plus rules for when to reveal them), optionally dual-control where the user acts on shared state — graded per turn and per conversation, versus canned conversations that cannot see compounding errors.
- [[Eval Contamination Defenses]] — If eval rows reach a model's training data, prompts or index, the score measures memory — public benchmarks fight it with unique multi-source answers, parameterized task templates refilled every run, temporal freshness and canary GUIDs, and each tactic transfers to a private suite.
- [[Ablation Switches and Feature Debt]] — A master switch that turns off an agent product's major features (thinking, compaction, memory, background tasks) gives a bare-model baseline, and per-feature switches answer whether each still helps — run before every release to find feature debt that newer models no longer need; companions are two-layer feature flags, deterministic prompt-rendering snapshots and privacy-typed analytics.
- [[Parameterised Benchmark Instances]] — The answer to benchmark leakage for agent evals — publish the generator, keep instances private and fresh per run, grade on final environment state, and when training on a benchmark reuse environments but hold out whole templates or domains.
- [[VLM Benchmark Shortcuts]] — Vision-language benchmarks can be passed without seeing: a model of all-zero weights scores 100% on caption-pair tests when ties go to the first, correct, caption; the original VQA answers 'yes' 98% of the time to yes/no questions; exact-match VQA accuracy under-scores free-form answers; and CHAIR misleads on long outputs.
- [[Web Agent Benchmarks (WebShop and Mind2Web)]] — Two early web-agent datasets: WebShop (a simulated Amazon-style shop, best trained agent 28.7% success against 59.6% for expert humans) and Mind2Web (real websites, offline scoring, best step success 52.0% but whole-task success at most 7.1%); all numbers self-reported.
- [[WebArena]] — Benchmark of 812 natural-language web tasks (241 templates) on Docker-hosted sites; the best GPT-4 baseline reaches 14.41% end-to-end success against 78.24% for humans (self-reported).
- [[WikiQA]] — A 3,047-question answer-sentence-selection set from Bing query logs where about two-thirds of questions have no correct sentence, introducing the answer triggering task; the best system's question-level F1 stays only slightly above 30% (self-reported).
- [[Wizard of Wikipedia]] — A crowd-sourced dialogue dataset where a knowledgeable wizard grounds each reply in a retrieved Wikipedia sentence, used to test knowledge selection and knowledge-grounded generation; knowledge helped, but selection was weak and fluent wrong facts remained (self-reported).
- [[XSTest]] — XSTest pairs 250 hand-written safe prompts across ten types with 200 unsafe contrasts to measure exaggerated safety; Llama2-70B-chat with its original system prompt fully refused 38% of the safe ones (self-reported).
- [[XYEval]] — A benchmark-mutation recipe that appends one confident but wrong user suggestion to each task of an existing agent benchmark and measures the score drop as sycophancy to a bad solution; all five models lost score across six benchmarks, up to 46.7% relative (Gemini 3.1 Pro on Terminal-Bench, 67.4 to 36.0, self-reported), and a generic warning prompt recovered only part of it.

## Multimodal
- [[ALBEF Align Before Fuse]] — ALBEF aligns image and text features with a contrastive loss before a cross-attention fusion encoder, and uses momentum-distilled pseudo-targets to cope with noisy web captions, without a detector.
- [[ALIGN Noisy Alt-Text Dual Encoder]] — ALIGN trains an image encoder and a text encoder with a contrastive loss on 1.8B alt-text pairs filtered only lightly, testing whether corpus scale can make up for noise; it reports retrieval and zero-shot results on par with or above CLIP.
- [[AttnGAN Attentional Text-to-Image GAN]] — AttnGAN generates images in three stages that attend to individual words per image sub-region, and adds a word-level image-text matching loss (DAMSM) to the GAN loss.
- [[Audiobox Unified Audio Generation]] — Audiobox pre-trains a 330M flow-matching Transformer by infilling unlabeled speech, music and sound, then fine-tunes it for speech, sound and a unified model controlled by text descriptions and voice prompts.
- [[BEiT Masked Image Modeling with Visual Tokens]] — BEiT pretrains a vision transformer by masking about 40% of patches blockwise and predicting each masked patch's discrete dVAE token, which the ablations show beats raw-pixel regression.
- [[BigGAN Large-Scale GAN Training]] — BigGAN scales class-conditional GANs to batch 2048 and wider networks, adds orthogonal regularisation so a truncation trick can trade sample variety for fidelity, and documents why large GANs collapse.
- [[BLIP Bootstrapped Captioning and Filtering]] — BLIP pretrains one mixture of encoder-decoder (MED) for understanding and generation, then uses CapFilt, a captioner plus a filter, to clean noisy web image-text pairs before pretraining a new model.
- [[BLIP-2 Q-Former Bridging]] — BLIP-2 bridges a frozen image encoder and a frozen LLM with a 188M-parameter Q-Former of 32 learned queries, pretrained in a representation stage and then a generative stage (all numbers self-reported).
- [[BYOL Bootstrap Your Own Latent]] — BYOL learns image representations with no negative pairs: an online network with a predictor regresses the projection of a slow moving-average target network on another augmented view.
- [[Cascaded Diffusion Models]] — A pipeline of separately trained diffusion models at rising resolution reaches strong class-conditional ImageNet FID with no classifier guidance, provided each super-resolution stage is trained on augmented low-resolution inputs.
- [[Chameleon Mixed-Modal Early Fusion]] — Chameleon trains one Llama-2-style decoder from scratch on text, code and VQ-quantised image tokens in a shared vocabulary, with QK-norm and norm reordering needed for stable training (all numbers self-reported).
- [[ChartQA and VisionTaPas]] — ChartQA offers 9,608 human-written and 23,111 machine-generated questions on real charts; a pipeline that extracts a data table and feeds it to VisionTaPas or T5-style models reaches at best 51.84% overall, and human-written questions are far harder (self-reported).
- [[Classifier Guidance and the ADM Architecture]] — Dhariwal and Nichol re-engineer the diffusion U-Net (ADM) and steer sampling with gradients from a classifier trained on noisy images, trading diversity for fidelity and reaching lower FID than BigGAN-deep on ImageNet.
- [[Classifier-Free Diffusion Guidance]] — One diffusion network is trained with the condition randomly dropped and, at sampling, the conditional and unconditional predictions are extrapolated, giving a fidelity/diversity trade-off without a separate classifier.
- [[CLIP Contrastive Language-Image Pretraining]] — CLIP trains an image encoder and a text encoder together with a symmetric contrastive loss on web image-text pairs, so class names can be turned into a zero-shot classifier; a later open study (OpenCLIP) fits power-law scaling curves and finds scaling differs by training dataset.
- [[CogVLM Visual Expert]] — CogVLM keeps a frozen Vicuna-7B and adds, in every layer, a trainable visual expert (separate QKV and FFN for image tokens), arguing deep fusion avoids both shallow-alignment weakness and forgetting from training the LLM directly (all numbers self-reported).
- [[Common Voice Multilingual Speech Corpus]] — Common Voice is a crowdsourced, CC0 multilingual read-speech corpus with vote-based validation and speaker-disjoint splits, evaluated with DeepSpeech transfer learning from English.
- [[Conformer Convolution-Augmented Transformer for ASR]] — The Conformer encoder block sandwiches self-attention and a convolution module between two half-step feed-forward modules to model global and local dependencies in speech recognition.
- [[Contrastive and Masked Vision-Language Pretraining]] — Family comparison of how vision and vision-language encoders are pretrained: contrastive dual towers, fusion encoders, masked image or video modeling, self-distillation, and LLM-bridged instruction tuning, with a page per method.
- [[ControlNet Spatial Conditioning]] — ControlNet adds spatial conditions such as edges, depth, pose or segmentation to a pretrained Stable Diffusion model by training a copy of its encoder and joining it to the locked model through zero-initialized convolutions.
- [[DALL-E and Parti Autoregressive Text-to-Image]] — DALL-E and Parti generate images by predicting discrete image tokens with a transformer: DALL-E uses a 12B decoder over dVAE tokens, Parti an encoder-decoder up to 20B over ViT-VQGAN tokens.
- [[Deep Visual-Semantic Alignments]] — Karpathy and Fei-Fei embed image regions and sentence fragments in one space, infer word-to-region alignments, and use them to train a multimodal RNN captioner that also describes regions (self-reported).
- [[Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Models]] — A diffusion model learns to undo gradual noising of an image; DDPM trains a U-Net to predict the added noise, and three follow-ups (score-based NCSN, Improved DDPM, DDIM) change the objective, the variances or the sampler.
- [[Depth Anything Monocular Depth from Unlabeled Data]] — Depth Anything scales monocular depth estimation by pseudo-labeling 62M unlabeled images with a teacher, training a student under strong perturbation and aligning its features to a frozen DINOv2 encoder.
- [[Diffusion and GAN Image Generation]] — Map of the image-generation methods compiled from the multimodal engineering papers: diffusion foundations, guidance, text-to-image systems, editing and personalisation, GANs and their metrics, and token-based generators.
- [[Diffusion Transformers (DiT)]] — DiT replaces the convolutional U-Net of a latent diffusion model with a ViT-style transformer over latent patches, conditioned through adaLN-Zero blocks, and finds FID falls as transformer Gflops rise.
- [[DINOv2 Curated-Data Self-Supervised Features]] — DINOv2 trains a ViT-g/14 on the 142M-image LVD-142M set curated by retrieval, with DINO plus iBOT losses, centering and KoLeo, to give frozen features that match or beat text-supervised OpenCLIP.
- [[DINOv3 Gram Anchoring Self-Supervised Backbone]] — DINOv3 scales DINOv2-style self-supervised training to a 6.7B ViT on 1,689 million curated images and adds Gram anchoring, a loss on patch-similarity structure that repairs dense-feature degradation over long training (all numbers self-reported).
- [[DocVQA]] — DocVQA pairs 12,767 document page images with 50,000 extractive questions and scores answers with ANLS; scene-text VQA models and text-only BERT readers fall well short of humans, especially on layout, figures and handwriting (self-reported).
- [[Donut OCR-Free Document Understanding]] — Donut is an encoder-decoder Transformer that reads a document image and generates structured JSON directly, with no OCR engine, after pretraining to read text.
- [[DreamLLM Interleaved Comprehension and Creation]] — DreamLLM trains a Vicuna-7B to read and generate images and interleaved documents end to end, using learnable dream queries and score distillation through a frozen Stable Diffusion decoder.
- [[Emu Generative Pretraining in Multimodality]] — Emu trains one 14B autoregressive model on interleaved image, video and text, predicting text tokens with cross-entropy and continuous visual embeddings with l2 regression, and decodes embeddings to pixels with a separate fine-tuned Stable Diffusion; all figures self-reported.
- [[EnCodec Neural Audio Compression]] — EnCodec is a streaming encoder-decoder codec with residual vector quantization, a multiscale STFT discriminator and a gradient-fraction loss balancer, plus an optional Transformer LM for entropy coding.
- [[EVA Masked CLIP-Feature Regression]] — EVA pretrains a 1.0B vanilla ViT to regress CLIP vision features of masked patches on 29.6M public images, then reuses it to initialise a 1.1B CLIP that trains stably.
- [[Faster R-CNN]] — Faster R-CNN adds a fully convolutional Region Proposal Network that shares convolutional features with the detector, cutting proposal cost to 10 ms per image and reaching 5 fps with VGG-16 (self-reported).
- [[Flamingo Gated Cross-Attention VLM]] — Flamingo keeps a pretrained vision encoder and a Chinchilla language model frozen and trains a Perceiver Resampler plus tanh-gated cross-attention layers on interleaved web data, giving few-shot prompting on image and video tasks (all numbers self-reported).
- [[Flickr30k Entities]] — Flickr30k Entities adds 244k coreference chains and 276k boxes to the Flickr30k captions and defines a phrase-localisation benchmark; a CCA plus detector, size and colour baseline reaches 50.89 Recall@1, yet barely improves image-sentence retrieval (self-reported).
- [[Florence UniCL Vision Foundation Model]] — Florence pretrains a two-tower CoSwin-H and text transformer on 900M curated image-text pairs with the UniCL contrastive loss, then adds small adapters for detection, vision-language tasks and video.
- [[Food-101 and car-197 Fine-Grained Datasets]] — Food-101 (101,000 images, 101 dish classes) and car-197 (16,185 images, 197 car models) are two fine-grained recognition datasets from 2013-2014 papers; both papers' own methods were hand-engineered and, on the main sets, did not beat a CNN or a plain 2D baseline (self-reported).
- [[Frechet Inception Distance and the Two Time-Scale Update Rule]] — TTUR gives a GAN's discriminator and generator separate learning rates, with a convergence argument to a local Nash equilibrium, and the paper introduces FID, a Gaussian distance between Inception features of real and generated images.
- [[Frozen Visual Prefix Few-Shot Learning]] — Frozen trains only a vision encoder, through a frozen 7B language model, to emit a two-token visual prefix on Conceptual Captions, then prompts with interleaved images and text for few-shot VQA and classification (all numbers self-reported).
- [[GIT Generative Image-to-Text Transformer]] — GIT uses one contrastively pretrained image encoder and a 6-block text decoder trained with a single language-modelling loss on 0.8B pairs, casting captioning, VQA, classification and scene-text tasks as caption generation (all numbers self-reported).
- [[GLIDE Text-Guided Diffusion]] — GLIDE is a 3.5B text-conditional diffusion model plus upsampler that compares CLIP guidance with classifier-free guidance, finds humans prefer the latter, and is fine-tuned for text-guided inpainting.
- [[GQA Benchmark]] — GQA builds 1.7M balanced compositional questions from Visual Genome scene graphs and adds consistency, validity, plausibility, distribution and grounding metrics; the best 2019 model, MAC, scores 54.06% against 89.3% for humans (self-reported).
- [[HuBERT Masked Prediction of Hidden Units]] — HuBERT predicts offline k-means cluster labels for masked speech frames, with the loss on masked frames only and the cluster targets refined over iterations.
- [[ILSVRC ImageNet Challenge]] — The ILSVRC paper documents how the ImageNet challenge was built, how classification, localization and detection entries improved from 2010 to 2014, and a two-annotator human comparison with GoogLeNet (self-reported).
- [[ImageBind Emergent Cross-Modal Alignment]] — ImageBind aligns audio, depth, thermal and IMU encoders to frozen OpenCLIP image embeddings using only image-paired data, and text-to-modality alignment emerges without any modality-text pairs.
- [[Imagen Frozen Language Model Text Conditioning]] — Imagen conditions a cascade of diffusion models on a frozen text-only T5 encoder, adds dynamic thresholding for large guidance weights, and introduces the DrawBench prompt set.
- [[Improved Techniques for Training GANs]] — Salimans et al. propose five heuristics for stabilising GAN training, a K+1-class semi-supervised GAN, and the Inception score as an automatic sample-quality metric (self-reported results on MNIST, CIFAR-10 and SVHN).
- [[InstructBLIP Instruction-Aware Query Transformer]] — InstructBLIP instruction-tunes only the Q-Former of a BLIP-2 model on 13 held-in datasets and feeds it the instruction too, improving zero-shot results on 13 held-out datasets.
- [[InstructPix2Pix Instruction-Based Image Editing]] — InstructPix2Pix trains a conditional diffusion model on synthetic before/after image pairs, built with GPT-3 and Stable Diffusion plus Prompt-to-Prompt, to edit a real image from a written instruction in one forward pass.
- [[InternVL Scaled Vision Encoder with QLLaMA]] — InternVL scales the vision encoder to 5.9B parameters and bridges it to an LLM through QLLaMA, a LLaMA-initialised middleware with 96 queries, using contrastive-then-generative alignment (all numbers self-reported).
- [[Kosmos-2 Grounded Multimodal LLM]] — Kosmos-2 continues KOSMOS-1 training on GRIT, a web-scale set of grounded image-text pairs, writing bounding boxes as discrete location tokens so one language model can ground phrases and accept boxes as input (all numbers self-reported).
- [[LAION-5B Open CLIP-Filtered Image-Text Dataset]] — LAION-5B is an open set of 5.85B web image-text links filtered by CLIP ViT-B/32 similarity, released with safety tags and used to reproduce OpenCLIP models, with explicit warnings that it is research-only and unfinished (all numbers self-reported).
- [[LENS Frozen LLM with Vision Modules]] — LENS converts an image to text with frozen tag, attribute and captioning modules and has a frozen Flan-T5 answer from that text, with no multimodal training.
- [[LLaMA-Adapter V2 and MultiModal-GPT Parameter-Efficient Visual Tuning]] — Two cheap routes to a visual instruction model: LLaMA-Adapter V2 early-fuses visual tokens into a frozen LLaMA with bias tuning (about 14M trainable parameters), and MultiModal-GPT trains LoRA adapters on OpenFlamingo (all numbers self-reported).
- [[LLaVA Visual Instruction Tuning]] — LLaVA connects a CLIP vision encoder to Vicuna through a small trained projector, aligns it on caption pairs, then instruction-tunes it on GPT-4-written image conversations; LLaVA-1.5 swaps in an MLP connector, VQA data with format prompts and 336px input (all numbers self-reported).
- [[LPIPS Perceptual Similarity]] — Zhang et al. build the 484k-judgment BAPPS dataset and show deep features from supervised, self-supervised and unsupervised networks agree with human similarity judgments better than L2, SSIM and FSIM; calibrated features give LPIPS (self-reported).
- [[LXMERT]] — LXMERT pretrains a three-encoder cross-modality Transformer on 9.18M image-sentence pairs with five tasks and, among the models the paper compares against, sets state of the art on VQA, GQA and NLVR2 (self-reported).
- [[Make-A-Scene Scene-Based Text-to-Image Generation]] — Make-A-Scene conditions a 4B-parameter autoregressive transformer on text plus optional segmentation (scene) tokens, uses region-aware tokenizer losses, and applies classifier-free guidance to logits.
- [[Make-An-Audio Text-to-Audio Latent Diffusion]] — Make-An-Audio generates audio with a spectrogram latent diffusion model conditioned on CLAP text embeddings, using distill-then-reprogram pseudo prompts to exploit audio without captions.
- [[Masked Autoencoders Pixel Reconstruction]] — MAE masks 75% of image patches, encodes only the visible ones and reconstructs masked pixels with a small decoder, which makes pretraining of large ViTs cheap and strong for fine-tuning.
- [[MaskGIT Masked Generative Image Transformer]] — MaskGIT generates images by iteratively predicting all VQGAN tokens in parallel with a bidirectional transformer, keeping the most confident and re-masking the rest on a cosine schedule, in about 8 to 12 steps.
- [[MedGemma 1.5 Medical Vision-Language Model]] — MedGemma 1.5 4B keeps the Gemma 3 architecture and 400M MedSigLIP encoder but adds 3D CT/MRI via capped slice stacks, whole-slide pathology via capped patch sets, localization and lab-report extraction; gains on new tasks came with regressions on some older ones (self-reported).
- [[MERLOT Multimodal Script Knowledge]] — MERLOT pretrains on YT-Temporal-180M, 180M frame and ASR-segment pairs from 6M YouTube videos, with contrastive matching, attention-masked language modeling and frame reordering to learn script knowledge.
- [[MERLOT Reserve Audio-Video-Text Pretraining]] — MERLOT Reserve pretrains on video frames, subtitles and audio with a contrastive span-matching objective, and transfers to VCR, TVQA, Kinetics-600 and zero-shot tasks.
- [[Microsoft COCO]] — Microsoft COCO is a 2014 object dataset of non-iconic Flickr images with per-instance segmentations (91 categories, 2.5M instances, 328k images); a DPM detector lost about half its AP moving from PASCAL VOC to COCO (self-reported).
- [[MiniGPT-4 Linear Projection Alignment]] — MiniGPT-4 aligns BLIP-2's frozen image encoder and Q-Former with a frozen Vicuna through a single trainable linear layer, then repairs unnatural captions with a 3,500-pair detailed-description stage (all numbers self-reported).
- [[MM-Vet]] — MM-Vet is a 218-question open-ended benchmark that tags each question with the capabilities it needs (recognition, OCR, knowledge, generation, spatial, math) and scores answers 0 to 1 with a few-shot GPT-4 judge; GPT-4V scores highest (self-reported).
- [[MMBench]] — MMBench is a 3,217-question bilingual multiple-choice benchmark over 20 abilities that scores a model only if it answers every circular shift of the choices (CircularEval) and uses GPT-4 to extract the chosen option (all figures self-reported).
- [[MME Benchmark]] — MME scores multimodal LLMs on 14 subtasks (10 perception, 4 cognition) with manually written yes/no question pairs per image, reporting accuracy and the stricter per-image accuracy+ across 30 models (self-reported).
- [[MMMU]] — MMMU is 11.5K college-level multimodal questions across 6 disciplines and 30 subjects; GPT-4V scores 55.7% on the test set and the best human expert 88.6% on validation (self-reported).
- [[MultiInstruct Multimodal Instruction Benchmark]] — MultiInstruct is a 62-task multimodal instruction tuning benchmark with five expert-written instructions per task; tuning OFA on it improves zero-shot results and lowers sensitivity to instruction wording.
- [[Multimodal Architectures Survey (Image-Text)]] — A compact digest of the textbook chapter surveying image-text architectures to about 2022: captioning, text-to-image generation, images helping language models, CLIP-family contrastive models, data2vec, ViLBERT and Flamingo, with where the chapter's prose and tables disagree.
- [[Multimodal Fusion and Multipurpose Models]] — A textbook digest on how deep to fuse modalities (early, joint, late), the structured-plus-unstructured data findings, and multipurpose multitask models (MultiModel, UniT, OFA, Gato, LIMoE, muNet), with the chapter's caveats about missing numbers.
- [[NFNet Adaptive Gradient Clipping]] — NFNets are normalizer-free ResNets trained with unit-wise Adaptive Gradient Clipping, which lets them train at large batch and strong augmentation; the paper reports F1 matching EfficientNet-B7 about 8.7x faster and 86.5% ImageNet top-1 without extra data (self-reported).
- [[OBELICS Interleaved Web Document Dataset]] — OBELICS is an open dataset of 141M filtered Common Crawl documents with 353M images and 115B tokens, used to train the Flamingo-style IDEFICS models and to show interleaved documents beat image-text pairs for few-shot use (all numbers self-reported).
- [[OCR-VQA]] — OCR-VQA-200K has 207,572 book-cover images and about 1M template-based questions about title, author, genre, year and edition; an OCR-plus-CNN baseline reaches 48.3% accuracy (self-reported).
- [[OK-VQA]] — OK-VQA is a 14,055-question VQA benchmark on COCO images whose questions need outside knowledge; 2019 models score 25-28 and Wikipedia retrieval adds little (self-reported).
- [[Omnilingual ASR]] — Omnilingual ASR scales a wav2vec 2.0 encoder to 7B parameters over 1,600+ languages and adds an LLM-style decoder that can transcribe unseen languages from a few in-context examples.
- [[PaLI and PaLI-X Jointly Scaled VLMs]] — PaLI feeds ViT patch features to an mT5 encoder-decoder and scales vision and language together on the multilingual WebLI dataset; PaLI-X pairs a 22B ViT with a 32B encoder-decoder and a 13-objective mixture (all numbers self-reported).
- [[PaLM-E Embodied Multimodal Language Model]] — PaLM-E injects continuous sensor inputs (state vectors, ViT features, object-centric scene representations) into a pretrained PaLM's embedding space as multimodal sentences, so one model handles robot planning, VQA and language tasks (all numbers self-reported).
- [[PICa GPT-3 Knowledge VQA]] — PICa turns an image into a caption and tags, then prompts a frozen GPT-3 with a few examples; with 16 shots it reports 48.0% on OK-VQA against 39.4% for the supervised state of the art (self-reported).
- [[Pivotal Tuning Inversion]] — Pivotal Tuning Inversion inverts a real face to a W-space pivot code in StyleGAN2, then fine-tunes the generator around the pivot with a locality regularizer so latent edits still work.
- [[pix2pix Conditional Adversarial Image Translation]] — pix2pix uses a conditional GAN with a U-Net generator and a 70x70 PatchGAN discriminator plus an L1 term as one general recipe for paired image-to-image translation across tasks.
- [[POPE Object Hallucination Probing]] — POPE asks a vision-language model yes/no questions about whether chosen objects are in an image, with absent objects sampled randomly, by popularity or by co-occurrence; it varied less across prompts than caption-based CHAIR in the authors' tests (self-reported).
- [[Prompt-to-Prompt Cross-Attention Editing]] — Prompt-to-Prompt edits a generated image by changing only the text prompt while copying or rescaling the diffusion model's cross-attention maps, so layout is preserved without masks or training.
- [[Qwen-VL Fine-Grained Perception VLM]] — Qwen-VL pairs an OpenCLIP ViT-bigG and a 256-query cross-attention adapter with Qwen-7B, trained in three stages on caption, box and OCR data to add grounding and text reading to a vision-language generalist (all numbers self-reported).
- [[RA-CM3 Retrieval-Augmented Multimodal Modeling]] — RA-CM3 prepends up to two documents retrieved by a frozen CLIP retriever to a 2.7B CM3 generator, so it can retrieve and generate both text and images; on MS-COCO it reports better FID and CIDEr than vanilla CM3 at under 30% of DALL-E training compute (self-reported).
- [[Region-Based Single-Stream VLP (UNITER, VL-BERT, Unified VLP)]] — Three 2019-2020 detector-region pretraining models compared: UNITER's conditional masking and optimal-transport alignment, VL-BERT's finding that sentence-image matching hurt, and Unified VLP's single shared encoder-decoder switched by attention mask (all numbers self-reported).
- [[RT-1 Robotics Transformer]] — RT-1 is a real-time Transformer policy for language-instructed robot manipulation: an ImageNet-pretrained EfficientNet conditioned on an instruction embedding through FiLM, TokenLearner compression, and discretised actions, trained by behaviour cloning on about 130k real episodes (all numbers self-reported).
- [[SAM 2 Promptable Video Segmentation]] — SAM 2 extends Segment Anything to video with a streaming memory over an MAE-pretrained Hiera encoder and a 50.9K-video SA-V dataset; it reports better accuracy with over 3x fewer interactions than SAM plus tracker baselines (self-reported).
- [[SAM 3 Promptable Concept Segmentation]] — SAM 3 detects, segments and tracks every instance of a noun-phrase or exemplar concept using a presence head and a human-plus-AI-verifier data engine; it reports cgF1 54.1 on SA-Co/Gold and LVIS mask AP 48.8 zero-shot (self-reported).
- [[ScienceQA]] — ScienceQA is 21,208 K-12 multimodal science questions with annotated lectures and explanations; few-shot GPT-3 with chain of thought is the best model and gold explanations in the prompt lift it to 94.13 (self-reported).
- [[SDEdit Guided Image Synthesis]] — SDEdit noises a user guide image to an intermediate time t0 and runs the reverse SDE of a pretrained diffusion model, trading faithfulness to the guide against realism through t0, with no training.
- [[Seamless Expressive and Streaming Translation]] — The Seamless family adds SeamlessM4T v2, expressive speech translation with a prosody-aware generator, and EMMA-based streaming translation, plus red teaming and watermarking.
- [[SeamlessM4T Multilingual Speech Translation]] — SeamlessM4T is one multitask UnitY model for S2ST, S2TT, T2ST, T2TT and ASR across up to 100 languages, built on w2v-BERT 2.0 pretraining, an NLLB text model and mined SeamlessAlign data.
- [[SEED-Bench]] — SEED-Bench is a 19K-question multiple-choice benchmark over 12 image and video dimensions, with human-verified answers and scoring by option likelihood; in its July 2023 tests the best of 18 models reached 53.37% (self-reported).
- [[Segment Anything SAM and SA-1B]] — Segment Anything defines promptable segmentation, an MAE-pretrained ViT-H model with a light mask decoder, and a three-stage data engine that produced the 1.1B-mask SA-1B dataset.
- [[ShareGPT4V Detailed Caption Data]] — ShareGPT4V replaces short captions with long GPT4-Vision captions (100K directly, 1.2M from a distilled captioner) in both pretraining and SFT of a LLaVA-1.5-style 7B model, and reports gains from caption quality alone (all numbers self-reported).
- [[SigLIP Sigmoid Loss for Language-Image Pretraining]] — SigLIP replaces the batch-softmax contrastive loss with an independent sigmoid loss on every image-text pair, which removes global normalisation, helps at small batch sizes and shows batch-size gains saturating near 32k.
- [[SimVLM PrefixLM Pretraining]] — SimVLM trains one encoder-decoder end to end with a single prefix language-modeling loss on image patches and noisy web pairs, replacing detector features and multi-loss pretraining.
- [[SoundStream Neural Audio Codec]] — SoundStream is an end-to-end trained codec built from a causal convolutional encoder, a residual vector quantizer with quantizer dropout, and an adversarially trained decoder.
- [[SPADE Spatially-Adaptive Normalization]] — SPADE injects a semantic segmentation mask into a GAN generator through per-pixel scale and bias predicted for each normalization layer, instead of feeding the mask as the input.
- [[SPAE Semantic Pyramid AutoEncoder]] — SPAE turns an image into a pyramid of words from a frozen LLM's own vocabulary, coarse semantics first, so a frozen LLM can classify, caption, answer questions about and generate images by in-context learning; understanding peaks at 21 tokens (self-reported).
- [[SpecAugment Spectrogram Augmentation]] — SpecAugment applies time warping, frequency masking and time masking to the log mel spectrogram during training, turning ASR overfitting into underfitting.
- [[Spectral Normalization for GANs]] — Spectral normalization divides each discriminator weight matrix by its largest singular value, estimated with one power-iteration step, to stabilise GAN training more cheaply than gradient penalties and without collapsing weight rank.
- [[Speech and Audio Generation and Recognition]] — A map of the speech and audio methods in the wiki by job: self-supervised speech encoders and ASR, neural codecs, generation of speech and sound, speech translation, and speech inputs to language models.
- [[SR3 and Palette Image-to-Image Diffusion]] — SR3 conditions a DDPM-style U-Net on a low-resolution image to do super-resolution; Palette reuses the idea, with the source image concatenated to the noisy target, for colorization, inpainting, uncropping and JPEG restoration.
- [[StyleCLIP Text-Driven StyleGAN Editing]] — StyleCLIP edits StyleGAN images from text with a frozen CLIP, in three ways (latent optimization, a per-prompt residual mapper, and an input-agnostic global direction in style space); evaluation is mostly qualitative (self-reported).
- [[StyleGAN Style-Based Generator]] — StyleGAN rebuilds the GAN generator around a mapping network and per-layer style modulation; StyleGAN3 removes aliasing to fix texture sticking, and Self-Distilled StyleGAN filters uncurated data and truncates toward cluster centres.
- [[Swin Transformer Shifted Windows]] — Swin builds a hierarchical vision transformer with patch merging and window self-attention whose windows shift between blocks, giving cost linear in image size and strong dense-prediction results.
- [[Textual Inversion and DreamBooth Personalization]] — Two ways to make a text-to-image model render a user's own subject from 3-5 photos: Textual Inversion learns one new word embedding in a frozen model, DreamBooth fine-tunes the model around a rare-token identifier with a prior-preservation loss.
- [[TextVQA and LoRRA]] — TextVQA is a crowd-sourced VQA set on Open Images photos whose questions need reading text in the image; the LoRRA model adds frozen OCR and a copy mechanism and reaches 27.63% test accuracy (self-reported).
- [[unCLIP Hierarchical Text-Conditional Generation]] — unCLIP first generates a CLIP image embedding from a caption with a prior, then decodes it to pixels with a diffusion decoder and upsamplers, gaining image diversity at a small cost in caption match.
- [[V-MoE Sparse Mixture of Experts for Vision]] — V-MoE replaces a subset of ViT MLP blocks with sparse expert layers (32 experts, top-k routing per patch) and adds Batch Prioritized Routing, which drops the least useful patches first when expert capacity is small (all numbers self-reported).
- [[VALL-E Neural Codec Language Model TTS]] — VALL-E treats text-to-speech as conditional language modeling over EnCodec codes, using a 3-second acoustic prompt for zero-shot voice cloning.
- [[Video Diffusion Models]] — A Gaussian diffusion model with a space-time factorized 3D U-Net generates video, trained jointly on images and video and extended in time or space with reconstruction-guided sampling.
- [[Video-ChatGPT Video Conversation Model]] — Video-ChatGPT fine-tunes a LLaVA-style model on 100K video-instruction pairs, training only a linear projection over spatially and temporally average-pooled CLIP frame features, and proposes a GPT-judged video conversation benchmark (all numbers self-reported).
- [[ViLBERT Co-Attention Two-Stream Pretraining]] — ViLBERT runs separate BERT-style text and image-region streams that exchange keys and values through co-attention layers, pretrained on Conceptual Captions and transferred to four vision-language tasks.
- [[ViLT Patch Projection Vision-Language Transformer]] — ViLT embeds images with a linear patch projection straight into a single-stream transformer, with no CNN or region detector, cutting visual-embedding cost sharply at some accuracy cost on VQA.
- [[VIOLET Masked Visual-Token Modeling]] — VIOLET is an end-to-end video-language transformer that uses a Video Swin encoder for temporal modelling and masked visual-token modeling over dVAE tokens as a pretraining task.
- [[ViperGPT Visual Inference via Python Execution]] — ViperGPT has a code model write a Python program per visual query against an API of pretrained vision modules, then runs it with the interpreter; no training, and zero-shot results on grounding, image QA and video QA that stay well below supervised models.
- [[Vision-Language Model Architectures]] — Family hub for how vision-language models join an image encoder to a language model: frozen-component bridges, deep fusion, detector-region encoders, jointly scaled encoder-decoders, early-fusion token models, grounding and fine-grained models, video and embodied extensions, with the data and backbone pieces they rely on.
- [[Visual Commonsense Reasoning and Adversarial Matching]] — VCR is a four-way multiple-choice dataset of movie-scene questions, answers and rationales built with Adversarial Matching; the R2C model reaches 44.0% on the staged answer-plus-rationale task against 85.0% for humans (self-reported).
- [[Visual Genome]] — Visual Genome densely annotates 108,077 images with region descriptions, objects, attributes, relationships, scene graphs and about 17 QA pairs per image, canonicalised to WordNet synsets; it is the base for GQA (all figures self-reported).
- [[Visual Instruction Tuning and Tool-Using VLMs]] — Four ways to give an LLM vision compared by what is trained: LLaVA and Emu train a connector or the whole model, SPAE trains only a tokenizer into a frozen LLM's words, and ViperGPT and Visual ChatGPT train nothing and call tools; benchmarks and a medical and a planning variant round out the family.
- [[Visual Question Answering Benchmarks]] — A comparison of VQA datasets and the multimodal-LLM evaluations that followed, grouped by what each isolates (language priors, compositional reasoning, outside knowledge, reading text, commonsense, expert knowledge), with the models and grounding datasets they build on.
- [[VizWiz Grand Challenge]] — VizWiz is a VQA dataset of 31,173 questions that blind users asked about their own phone photos; about 28.63% are unanswerable, and VQA v2-trained models score about 0.14 accuracy on it off the shelf (self-reported).
- [[Voicebox Flow-Matching Speech Infilling]] — Voicebox is a non-autoregressive flow-matching model trained to infill masked Mel frames given a phone transcript, so zero-shot TTS, denoising and editing become different context masks.
- [[VPRL Visual Planning with Reinforcement Learning]] — VPRL plans in pure image sequences: a video-pretrained model generates the next-state image, and a GRPO stage rewards moves that shorten the path to the goal; on three small grid-navigation tasks it averages 80.6 exact match against 53.6 for text SFT (self-reported).
- [[VQA v2 Balanced Dataset]] — VQA v2 pairs every question with two similar COCO images that have different answers, so a model cannot succeed on language priors alone; models trained on the original unbalanced set lose accuracy on it (all figures self-reported).
- [[VQGAN Taming Transformers]] — VQGAN compresses an image into a grid of discrete codebook indices using perceptual and patch-adversarial losses, then an autoregressive transformer models the index sequence, with a sliding window for megapixel images.
- [[W2V-BERT Contrastive and Masked Speech Pretraining]] — W2V-BERT trains wav2vec 2.0's contrastive task and a masked prediction task over the quantizer's token IDs together, end to end, for self-supervised speech pretraining.

## Infrastructure

- [[AutoMix Self-Verification Routing]] — AutoMix has a small model draft an answer, check it against the context by sampling its own verdicts, and hands the noisy score to a POMDP router that decides whether to keep the answer or call a larger model; the authors report gains over FrugalGPT and HybridLLM on context-grounded tasks (self-reported).
- [[Bounding Agents Rather Than Trusting Them]] — Constrain what the agent can do instead of trusting it to behave — four bounds (type/IO schemas, explicit state, escalation as an outcome, termination conditions) plus why guardrails without trajectory eval are unattributable.

- [[Agent Security Risk Taxonomy]] — The sixteen agentic security risks in five families, and the architectural claim that mitigation belongs in the component that owns the risk rather than in a central supervisory layer.
- [[Agent Management Layer]] — The six systems a production agent needs beyond the agent itself — evaluation frameworks, fallback/escalation, drift monitoring, HITL checkpoints, audit logging, and a defined handoff protocol — argued as 60% of the deployment, with HITL corrections doubling as the training-data pipeline only if they are logged.
- [[Cheap-First Gating and Model Cascades]] — Send each request to a cheap model and let a gate decide whether it escalates; published savings run from modest to large by task and date, simple baselines often match learned routers, and routers fail on unfamiliar or adversarial queries.
- [[Chunked Prefill and Adaptive Batching]] — Chunked prefill splits a long prompt into pieces so decode steps can interleave, and adaptive batching tunes batch size and delay to load; the source rules of thumb are mostly unmeasured.
- [[Clipper Prediction Serving]] — Clipper is a 2016-17 serving layer that sits between applications and unmodified ML frameworks, adding per-model caching, AIMD adaptive batching against a latency target, and bandit-based model selection; batching raised throughput up to 26x under a 20 ms target (self-reported).
- [[Confidence-Gated Escalation]] — A gate that escalates when a cheap model's confidence is low needs its threshold set on one labelled sample and checked on another, because thresholds overrun their error budget on held-out data and a score measured on one task may not hold on the next.
- [[Declarative Attention]] — Declarative Attention prompts a model to declare, in its own output, whether each step attends globally, to chosen chunks, or only locally; a decode-time mask on block tables then cut attended tokens 45.8% on Gemma-4-31B at 1.40 points accuracy cost, with no training (self-reported).
- [[Deep Compression Pipeline]] — Deep Compression chains magnitude pruning, trained weight-sharing quantization and Huffman coding to shrink CNNs 35x to 49x with no reported accuracy loss; speed and energy gains were measured only on the pruned layers.
- [[DeepSeekMoE Fine-Grained Expert Segmentation]] — DeepSeekMoE splits each feed-forward expert into many smaller ones, activates proportionally more of them, and keeps a few shared experts always on; the paper reports a 16B model matching dense 7B models at about 40% of the computation.
- [[DeepSpeed Inference and ZeRO-Inference]] — DeepSpeed Inference combines fused small-batch GPU kernels, tensor and pipeline parallelism for dense models, expert/data/tensor parallelism for mixture-of-experts models, and ZeRO-Inference, which streams weights from CPU memory or NVMe to run huge models on few GPUs.
- [[Disaggregated Prefill-Decode Serving]] — Disaggregated serving runs prompt prefill and token decode on separate GPU pools so long prefills stop delaying other requests' decode steps; the sources give routing, KV-transfer and sizing guidance, with figures mostly quoted from other work.
- [[FlashAttention and FlashAttention-2]] — FlashAttention computes exact attention block by block in on-chip memory with a running softmax, avoiding the full score matrix in HBM; FlashAttention-2 reworks work partitioning to approach matmul-level GPU utilisation.
- [[Goodput and Inference Metrics]] — Reference for the serving metrics the inference sources use (TTFT, TPOT, throughput, goodput, MFU, MBU): two different goodput definitions, worked examples, and the advice to report percentiles.
- [[GPU Cluster Host, Network and Storage Tuning]] — Synthesis of one book's chapters on NVL72 rack hardware, host OS and container tuning, NCCL and collective communication, and GPU storage I/O; the figures are vendor specifications, unsourced ranges or illustrations.
- [[GPU Kernel Tuning Workflow]] — Checklist-style synthesis of one book's CUDA chapters: occupancy, memory access, warp efficiency, arithmetic intensity, pipelining, streams and graphs, plus AI-generated kernels; almost all numbers are the book's own illustrations or unsourced rules of thumb.
- [[Intelligence per Watt]] — Intelligence per Watt (IPW) divides mean task accuracy by mean power and Intelligence per Joule adds latency; Saad-Falcon et al. use both to compare local and cloud inference, reporting a 5.3x per-watt gain from 2023 to 2025 and a per-watt and per-joule picture that disagrees (self-reported, with several internal inconsistencies).
- [[Jev Decision Model]] — TypeSafe AI's proprietary hosted model returns typed decisions with probabilities instead of text; its speed and price gains are partly confirmed independently against named comparators, while accuracy and calibration vary by task, and no peer-reviewed evaluation exists yet.
- [[KV Cache Reduction Methods]] — The KV cache grows with batch, sequence length, hidden size and layers, so reduction methods cut it along different axes (tokens, precision, channels, layers, sparsity); this page maps the axes and records the survey's reported numbers and its caution that sparse attention does not by itself shrink KV memory.
- [[LazyLLM Dynamic Token Pruning]] — LazyLLM prunes prompt tokens layer by layer during prefill using the last token's attention, lets pruned tokens return later, and cut time to first token 2.34x on multi-document QA with under 1% score loss on Llama 2 7B, without training (self-reported).
- [[LLM in a Flash]] — A method for running a model larger than DRAM by keeping weights in flash and loading only the feed-forward neurons that are active, using windowing and row-column bundling; up to 4x CPU, 7x Metal and 20x GPU speedup over naive loading when half the model fits (self-reported).
- [[LLM-Blender PairRanker and GenFuser]] — LLM-Blender runs 11 open-source LLMs on every input, ranks all candidate pairs with a cross-attention ranker, and fuses the top three with a generator; it beats any single model but costs more than one call, so it is an ensemble, not a cost-saving router.
- [[LLM.int8 Outlier-Aware Quantization]] — LLM.int8() quantizes transformer matrix multiplications to 8 bits with per-vector scaling and routes the rare large-magnitude outlier feature dimensions through 16-bit, which the paper reports preserves quality up to 175B parameters.
- [[LLMSelector Per-Module Model Assignment]] — LLMSelector picks a different model for each module of a fixed multi-call pipeline by coordinate search with an LLM diagnoser that scores each module separately; the authors report 5-70 point gains over the best single model on six tasks, with two synthetic tasks driving the top end (self-reported).
- [[Lookahead Sparse Attention]] — Lookahead Sparse Attention (FlashMemory-DeepSeek-V4) keeps only the compressed KV chunks a small Memory Indexer predicts will be needed over the next 64 decode steps on the GPU and mirrors the rest in CPU memory; GPU KV fell to 13.5% of full with average accuracy 77.5 against 76.9 on DeepSeek-V4-Flash (self-reported).
- [[Medusa Decoding Heads]] — Medusa adds several small feed-forward heads on the model's last hidden state, each predicting a further-ahead token, and verifies a tree of candidates in one pass with a tree attention mask; no separate draft model is needed.
- [[Model Compression Landscape]] — A survey-level map of language-model compression, pruning, quantization, distillation and low-rank methods, plus two measurement studies, post-training quantization across 1B to 405B Llama models and dropping layers from pre-trained encoders.
- [[MoE Expert Offloading]] — Running Mixtral-8x7B on a 12-16 GB GPU by keeping an LRU cache of experts on the GPU, prefetching with the next layer's gate, and quantizing experts harder than attention; 2.1-3.1 tok/s with 2-bit experts at batch 1 (self-reported).
- [[Multi-Head Latent Attention]] — Multi-head latent attention (MLA) caches one low-rank key-value latent per token plus a small decoupled position key, shrinking the cache far more than grouped-query sharing; DeepSeek-V2 pairs it with DeepSeekMoE and reports large cache and throughput gains over its dense predecessor.
- [[Multi-Query and Grouped-Query Attention]] — Sharing one key-value head (multi-query) or a few key-value groups (grouped-query) across all query heads shrinks the key-value cache and the data loaded per decoding step; grouped-query can be reached by uptraining a multi-head checkpoint on a small fraction of pre-training compute.
- [[Nemotron Puzzle Hybrid MoE Compression]] — Static, hardware-aware pruning of a hybrid Mamba/MoE/attention model by Puzzle search plus distillation, then quantization and MTP drafting; the 75B-A9B result reaches 2.03x server throughput at matched user speed on 8xB200 for about 1.2 points lower average accuracy (self-reported).
- [[Observability — LangFuse vs LangSmith Decision]] — Decision to use LangFuse first for RAG observability — native ragas/deepeval integrations, self-hostable, GDPR-friendly, and highest weighted score (8.58/10) for [client]'s AWS-hosted, high-compliance context.
- [[Cloud Run + Cloud SQL Pattern]] — Single-container Cloud Run service (FastAPI + SPA) connected to Cloud SQL via the built-in Auth Proxy unix socket — no public IP, no SSL config, private GCP-internal networking by default.
- [[Cloud Service Deployment]] — A long-running 24/7 hosted service — the same container as single-service plus monitoring, health checks, and env management, chosen when the system must be available without anyone starting it.
- [[Deployment Topology Ladder]] — Five deployment topologies (local → single service → cloud service → split service → serverless) ordered by who can access the system, with cost and ops-complexity as the selection axes rather than technical capability.
- [[Input Guardrails Pipeline]] — 7-stage deterministic safety pipeline (normalise → size check → domain classify → injection detect → PII redact → XML envelope → advisory) that runs before every LLM call — LLM-free by design.
- [[Langfuse ADK Tracing Patterns]] — Two-layer Langfuse instrumentation for ADK agents — OpenTelemetry auto-instrumentation plus manual @observe decorators produce a single unified trace tree; session grouping, RAG path tagging, and first-class Scores are the critical operational additions.
- [[Langfuse Platform]] — Langfuse is an open-source LLM engineering platform for tracing, prompt management, and evaluation — chosen by [client]'s AI teams as the observability standard, with SSO and governance pending before production rollout. Instrumentation patterns vary by framework (lf.trace() for ADK, CallbackHandler for LangGraph, @observe for FastAPI).
- [[LangSmith Platform]] — LangSmith mechanics — auto-instrumentation for LangGraph vs manual @traceable wiring for ADK, datasets, evaluator functions as thin adapters over your own graders, annotation queues, and experiment comparison.
- [[LLM Inference Economics]] — Why prompt tokens and output tokens have different unit costs — the prefill/decode split — and the four levers (quantization, distillation, speculative decoding, output-length discipline) that move serving cost and latency.
- [[LLM System Bottleneck Table]] — The five recurring bottlenecks in a production LLM system — token overload, queue congestion, vector index bloat, model cold start, rate-limited APIs — each with its cause and the mitigation that follows from the cause.
- [[Local-Only Deployment]] — The zero-infrastructure rung — the AI runs on a developer's machine with no hosting and no external access, chosen when the only user is the developer and iteration speed matters more than availability.
- [[Observability and Runtime Patterns]] — Observability tool choice (LangSmith vs Langfuse), tracing architecture, runtime topology and checkpointer alignment rules, trigger patterns, and key signals to monitor for assistant agents.
- [[Partitioning Layouts for Transformer Inference]] — Pope et al. (2022) pick how to shard a large Transformer across TPU chips with an analytical communication-cost model, choosing among 1D and 2D weight-stationary and weight-gathered layouts and sharding multiquery attention over batch; headline numbers are 29 ms per token decode, 76% MFU prefill and up to 32x longer context on PaLM 540B (self-reported).
- [[PaSS Parallel Speculative Sampling]] — PaSS drafts speculative-sampling candidates from the target model itself by appending a few learned look-ahead embeddings, training only those embeddings; with LLaMA 7B and 4 look-ahead tokens it gave up to 30% faster generation with unchanged HumanEval scores (self-reported).
- [[PGVector Migration Pattern]] — Migrating a vector store from in-memory NumPy arrays to PostgreSQL + pgvector — preserving the public API, using cosine distance operator, adding an IVFFlat index, and moving embeddings to Cloud SQL without re-embedding.
- [[PII Masking Approaches]] — Regex vs LLM-based vs hybrid PII masking for conversation data pipelines — contextual PII is the hard problem; compliance sign-off is a hard gate before data moves.
- [[Prefix Caching]] — Claude's automatic KV cache reuse for repeated prompt prefixes — cuts latency and cost by 90% for static system prompts and long tool schemas.
- [[Presidio PII Redaction for Langfuse]] — Presidio orchestration layer with spaCy fr_core_news_lg + CamemBERT NER + custom regex recognizers for French financial PII — wired into Langfuse via the SDK mask hook as a single interception point before traces leave the process.
- [[Production Hardening Patterns]] — Checklist of production hardening fixes for the Librarian service — P0/P1/P2 issues, async I/O safety, SQL injection prevention, CORS, and Docker packaging.
- [[Production Readiness Backlog]] — The pre-launch gap checklist for a RAG service going to managed cloud hosting — auth, CORS, structured logging, tests, CI gate, staging separation, probes, retries, migrations — each stated as current-state vs required.
- [[RouteLLM Preference-Trained Routers]] — RouteLLM trains a binary router on human preference data to send each query to a strong or a weak model; the paper reports large call savings on MT Bench but near-random routing on other benchmarks until the training data is augmented.
- [[S-LoRA Multi-Adapter Serving]] — S-LoRA serves thousands of LoRA adapters from one base model by batching the base computation, keeping adapters in host memory, and paging adapter weights and KV cache from one shared GPU pool; it held 7.61 req/s at 2,000 adapters on one A100 (self-reported).
- [[Safeguards Architecture — Five Protection Layers]] — Five-layer runtime safety pipeline for production agents — input guardrails, routing confidence, retrieval quality (CRAG), post-generation grounding check, and escalation routing — each with distinct latency cost and failure mode.
- [[Serverless Deployment]] — Functions that spin up on demand and shut down after execution — an off-ladder alternative chosen on traffic shape (bursty, stateless, sub-30s) rather than audience size, paying cold starts and timeout limits for zero idle cost.
- [[Single Service Deployment]] — One container running the AI backend, reachable by a small internal team — the rung where deployment first exists but auth, frontends, and independent scaling deliberately do not.
- [[SparseGPT and Wanda Post-Training Pruning]] — SparseGPT prunes GPT-scale models one-shot with a layer-wise Hessian-based solver, while Wanda scores weights by magnitude times input activation norm with no weight update; both reach about 50% sparsity with little loss and break down at high sparsity.
- [[Speculative Decoding]] — Speculative decoding has a cheap drafter propose several tokens that the target model verifies in one parallel pass, with the rejection rule keeping outputs identical; this page collects the survey's reported speedups and three drafter variants that avoid a second model.
- [[Speculative Decoding Variants]] — Speculative decoding drafts several tokens cheaply and verifies them with the target model in one pass; the sources list draft-model, EAGLE, Medusa, self-speculative and consistency variants with speedups quoted from other work.
- [[Split Service Deployment]] — Separately deployed frontend and backend sharing identity through a common auth provider — the only rung that supports external users and multi-tenancy, at roughly double the operational burden.
- [[Star Attention]] — Star Attention encodes a long context in two phases, local blocks each prefixed by an anchor block, then global attention for the query and generated tokens, and reports large speedups over Ring Attention on multi-host Llama inference with small accuracy loss.
- [[Streaming Output Scrubbing]] — Scrubbing secrets from a streamed LLM response in transit via a TransformStream with a carry window — the only seam that preserves streaming while guaranteeing scrubbed bytes are the only bytes the caller sees.
- [[StreamingLLM Attention Sinks]] — StreamingLLM keeps the cached keys and values of the first few tokens plus a rolling recent window, which the paper reports stops perplexity collapsing on streams of millions of tokens without fine-tuning; it does not extend the usable context.
- [[System Design — Shared Code-Index Service]] — Interview-format system design writeup of the DSSG shared indexer — centralized indexer + query API with MCP as a thin read-only client, DuckDB single-writer risk, and the pgvector escape hatch.
- [[Gate vs Ship Make Targets]] — `make gate` is the complete local pre-merge check (lint, types, tests, doc enforcement) separate from `make ship`; CI runs the same gate
- [[Pull-Only Event Transport]] — decision: poll GitHub from the local tick with gh + since/ETag; repository_dispatch is POST-only and unconsumable, state-branch is dominated, pull fails visibly where push fails quietly
- [[Migration Done Means Pushed and Probed]] — `IF NOT EXISTS` guards on name not definition, probes that triggers can intercept, migrations marked done with their column absent — and the derived drift check that replaced review prose with an invariant.
- [[ThinK Key Cache Channel Pruning]] — ThinK prunes the channel dimension of the key cache, keeping the channels with the highest query-key score per head; pruning 40% of key channels cut total KV memory 20% with LongBench accuracy kept or raised on top of H2O or SnapKV (self-reported).
- [[TiDAR Diffusion Drafting]] — TiDAR drafts tokens with one-step diffusion and verifies them autoregressively in the same forward pass using a structured attention mask, filling the idle token slots of memory-bound decoding; 4.71x (1.5B) and 5.91x (8B) decode speedup at batch size 1 on one H100 (self-reported).
- [[Torch Compile and Triton Stack]] — How the book describes PyTorch profiling and the torch.compile stack (Dynamo, AOT Autograd, Inductor, Triton, XLA): one illustrative MoE speedup, graph-break and dynamic-shape advice, and internal inconsistencies about compile modes.
- [[TurboTransformers Variable-Length Serving]] — TurboTransformers (PPoPP 2021) serves encoder transformers on GPUs with fused kernels, a sequence-length-aware memory allocator and a dynamic-programming batch scheduler; the paper reports gains on variable-length BERT but parity or losses against tuned fixed-length runtimes.
- [[Written-but-Inert RLS Policies]] — Policies written but never enabled, tables enabled out-of-band, a review queue reading 95 rows through the anon client and showing zero, policies gating on a value never written — diagnosed by one anon-vs-service-role probe.
- [[Multimarket — One Project, Market as Config]] — Decision: one database with market as config (column only when a second market exists) over a project per market, because N migration chains multiply the unapplied-migration failure the repo had already shipped twice.
- [[Rate-Limit Keying: Identity Over IP]] — key anonymous rate limits on a signed identity cookie, choose fail-open/closed per route, and never use in-process counters on serverless
- [[Two AI Cost Economies: Durable Once, Contextual On Demand]] — split LLM spend into one-time durable enrichment and per-request contextual calls; churn dominates model choice
- [[Scaling Axes and Customer-Triggered Cost Cliffs]] — users, catalogue, and team velocity scale independently; the expensive cliffs (SOC2, CI minutes) are triggered by customers, not load
- [[Silent Failure Catalogue]] — code-review checklist of the ways errors disappear (swallowed exceptions, empty-as-fallback, floating promises, retry exhaustion, default-filled absence) around the question 'who finds out, and how?'
- [[Hotlink-Not-Copy Image Posture]] — Show third-party images by direct load only, first-party sources, provenance and attribution, no circumvention, host privacy vetting, no-deploy kill switch
- [[Service Surface Defect Checklist]] — Ten concrete defect classes found live in a prototype HTTP service — ungated client ids reaching paths, uploads checked after reading the whole body, trusting declared MIME, blocking work on the event loop, TOCTOU across two opens, id-only mutation scope, blobs in listings, unfenced ingested documents, and splatted response models.
- [[A Trace Is Not a Number of Record]] — LLM traces are debugging evidence, not a ledger: they miss SDK-bypassing seams, are off without keys, and lack error class and retention, so spend/error numbers need an append-only call ledger at every seam
- [[Study-Scoped Pseudonymous Attribution]] — When an evaluation study needs to follow named testers across sessions, attribute only an explicitly enrolled, time-bounded study via a random per-account pseudonym on the parent record — not a user_id on all telemetry — and treat the pseudonym as personal data whose erasure must delete every linked row, replay copy and export, not just the mapping.
- [[Agent Credential Ceiling]] — an agent never holds a credential broader than the human it serves: user JWT + RLS for own data, tenant-scoped hashed service key for cross-system data, tenant scoping never in the prompt
- [[Right-Sizing a Document-to-LLM Pipeline]] — ingest→enrich→serve on serverless with dedup key + DLQ, typed loop over graph framework, HTTP 202-and-poll over MCP, explicit omit list
- [[Three-Surface Guardrail Asymmetry]] — block user input, neutralize retrieved content, scrub output before caller and tracer; fail open when disabled, closed when crashing
- [[RLS On, No Policy for Server-Mediated Tables]] — decision: harmless-per-row tables can leak in aggregate; with only admin-client readers, enable RLS and write no anon policy
- [[Log Drain Over Error Tracker at Pre-Scale]] — structured stdout logs plus a platform log drain over an error-tracking SaaS while the error rate is unmeasured; done means one error proven in the drain, client errors named as uncovered
- [[Restore Drill: Rebuild-from-Migrations Visibility Probe]] — rebuild from migrations alone, seed published+draft rows, diff anon vs service-role visibility against production; catches vacuous predicates a boolean RLS check cannot
- [[Gate Dependabot Auto-Merge on Branch Protection]] — `--auto` already honors branch protection; delete the wait-for-ci job instead of duplicating CI (decision)
- [[Polite Crawler Robots Compliance]] — Making an ingestion crawler honour robots.txt: hand-roll a deliberately minimal parser with its unimplemented boundary written in the docstring, fail open when robots.txt can't be fetched, raise a distinct error that the run log classifies as 'blocked' (we chose not to fetch), not 'failed', and only let Crawl-delay lengthen a source's configured rate limit, never shorten it.
- [[Transactional Email Sender Setup]] — auth-flow email via SMTP relay: sending subdomain, SPF/DKIM/DMARC, templates, rate limits; verify on a real message's Authentication-Results
- [[Per-Render Image Optimizer Opt-Out]] — stop next/image proxying third-party bytes with a per-instance `unoptimized` computed from the resolved src; global flag and pass-through loader both rejected
- [[Logger Redact Guardrail]] — logger-level redact list as a future-call-site guardrail; no depth wildcard, test the branch and destination that actually run
- [[RLS Gates Rows, Not Columns]] — a using(true) public SELECT policy publishes every new column; move audit metadata (actor, free-text reason) to an admin-only side table written in the same request
- [[Item Lifecycle Ledger as a New Table]] — a per-item lifecycle ledger as a new table rather than widening an ingestion log whose retention and best-effort writes serve its own alarm; zero at a writer-less stage means no writer
- [[Exposure-Tiered Dependency Triage]] — dependency updates routed by CI result and semver bump type; vulnerability alerts decided by whether the code path is deployed, not severity alone
- [[Uptime Probe Asserts the Journey]] — external probe must exercise the real user journey, not an always-healthy liveness route; done when a test alert arrived
- [[Paid Tiers Start at the Beta, Not the Alpha]] — buy each paid tier for the property it sells (posture, measured capacity, running scheduler, protected host) when that property becomes load-bearing
- [[Rollback Restores Code Only]] — host rollback never undoes migrations, rows or schedules; deploy invariants, SHA-correlated eval gate, runbook authored before the host exists
- [[Closed-Beta Access Mechanisms]] — host protection vs separate beta credentials for an alpha; shared password vs unguessable link vs invite code for an external cohort; noindex over robots.txt
- [[Backend Scaling Fundamentals]] — single server first, load balancing, stateless servers, cache TTL vs invalidation and stampedes, replication ≠ backup, sharding with consistent hashing; every fix creates the next problem
- [[API Design Fundamentals]] — entities→REST resources, REST outside/gRPC inside, GraphQL only for divergent client shapes (N+1), cursor pagination for feeds, identity from the token, idempotency keys
- [[Agent Trace Anatomy]] — A trace is one request as a tree of spans (invoke_agent, chat, execute_tool, retrieval) carrying inputs, outputs, tokens, latency and versions — complete when it answers six questions, standardized by OpenTelemetry GenAI conventions, and the shared raw material for offline evals, online scoring, cost attribution and new dataset rows.
- [[Three-Band Confidence Routing]] — Two per-category thresholds turn a score into allow / review / act, set from error costs — the shared decision layer under moderation, extraction and fraud scoring.
- [[Completion Trigger Gate]] — For inline completion, decide whether to call the model at all: debounce, parser-based suppression, and an acceptance-probability classifier, plus cancelling stale in-flight requests.
- [[Provider-Agnostic Model Adapter]] — Canonical request/response contract + per-provider adapters + logical-name registry with capability flags; drop only cosmetic provider params, never correctness-critical ones, and log every drop.
- [[ZeRO-Offload]] — ZeRO-Offload keeps fp16 parameters and the forward and backward pass on the GPU while gradients, optimizer states and the Adam update live on the CPU, which the paper reports lets one 32GB V100 train a 13B-parameter model.

## Interview Prep

- [[Agents Interview Study Guide]] — Exam-prep reference for agent architecture questions — workflow vs agent distinction, composable patterns, ACI tool design, harness engineering, long-horizon reliability, and memory taxonomy.
- [[AIE Code-Test Flaw Taxonomy]] — The eleven recurring defects in LLM take-home submissions — context overflow, naive chunking, missing retry/timeout, ungrounded generation, JSON drift, swallowed exceptions — each with its detection cue and the minimal fix that fits inside a one-hour timebox.
- [[Durable vs Performative Knowledge Split]] — One test — "is this true regardless of whether I'm being interviewed?" — sorts study material into durable technical knowledge and interview-performance technique, which decay at different rates and need different revision cadences.
- [[Evals and Observability Interview Study Guide]] — Exam-prep reference for eval and observability questions — vocabulary, grader types, three-tier taxonomy, pass@k vs pass^k, and the tracing-first discipline.
- [[LLM Fundamentals Interview Study Guide]] — Exam-prep reference for LLM theory questions — transformer architecture, training pipeline, adaptation menu, inference economics, and failure modes.
- [[RAG Interview Study Guide]] — Exam-prep reference for RAG architecture questions — component choices, architecture variants, production judgment, measured benchmarks, and terminology traps.
- [[Situation-Indexed Decision Tree]] — Replacing a flat component table (answers indexed by noun) with a branching tree indexed by situation — one memorized trunk fork into four system spines, each node carrying a discriminator question and a rehearsable sentence.
- [[System Design Interview Study Guide]] — Method guide for the ML/LLM/agent system design round — 5-step process, trade-off narration formula, LLM reference architecture, bottleneck table, and failure mode reflexes.
- [[Timebox-Scaled Deliverable Bar]] — Test coverage, file organisation, and observability expectations are a dial set by the assignment window rather than a constant — the one-hour "don't write tests" advice inverts at three hours, and the async ambiguity protocol inverts the live-round clarify-first reflex.
- [[Graded Rehearsal Drill Harness]] — coach/mock stances, spoiler gate, learner types everything, strict process rubrics, content-free append-only log keyed by stable qids
- [[AI-Assisted Take-Home Playbook]] — decide → walking skeleton → deepen → narrate, hand-roll the spike scaffold, visible rejection of AI output, a 'did not build' slide
- [[AI Role Interview Loop Map]] — Rounds each AIE/MLE/DS/FDE loop runs, the six round types a self-made list misses (recruiter screen, project deep-dive, customer simulation, debugging, take-home defense, reverse interview), and a role × topic weighting.
- [[Behavioral Interview Study Guide]] — STAR plus a behavior-change Reflection close and audience re-angling of one story bank; ten story categories with their traps; failure-story rules and zero-score anti-patterns.
- [[Case Study Interview Study Guide]] — Live/take-home/defense case method: the eight-section template, baseline-first number-anchored objectives, three ROI formulas, assumptions-first writeup, five-step defense response.
- [[Code Review Interview Study Guide]] — Orient on contracts, read in consequence order, severity and confidence as independent axes, recurring agent-PR defect classes, and the prose-only safeguard check.
- [[Coding Challenge Interview Study Guide]] — Live coding as AIE loops run it: stdlib-only offline practicals, trap-shaped prompts graded on the critique, a cold pattern set with a 25-minute self-test, and security minimums.
- [[Programming Interview Study Guide]] — coding-screen reference: DSA pattern checklist, Python stdlib moves, recurring SQL idioms (window functions, dedup-latest, funnel/retention), answer sketches
- [[ML Foundations Interview Study Guide]] — classical ML round: leakage discipline, model menu with reasons, metric chosen by the decision it drives, structuring-ML-projects habits, SQL screen
- [[Context Engineering and Cost Interview Study Guide]] — context rot, five context layers, compaction retention priorities, prefix vs semantic caching, rank-ordered cost levers, cost per successful task
- [[Security and Safety Interview Study Guide]] — agent threat model by name, source→sink containment over detection, nine-layer defence stack, operational boundaries, compliance quick answers
- [[Data Engineering and MLOps Interview Study Guide]] — idempotency and backfills, batch vs streaming, MLOps maturity ladder, drift monitoring, LLM-era mapping (embeddings are ETL, prompt versioning is a registry)
- [[Product and Delivery Interview Study Guide]] — user→problem→metric→constraint opener, ROI arithmetic, where AI belongs, metric trees, mission-driven constraints, three-audience pitch
- [[Customer Simulation Interview Study Guide]] — FDE role-play round: six habits that survive improvisation, Problem → Ownership → Options → Recommendation → Next-step, and discovering that the stated target is a proxy for a different constraint.
- [[Project Deep-Dive Interview Study Guide]] — Seniority reads as decisions defended: three altitudes per project, a constraints-at-the-time decision template, a five-part incident story where isolation is hardest to fake, calibrated ownership language.
- [[Leadership Rounds Interview Study Guide]] — CTO/HoP/founder rounds graded on alignment and trust: judgment threads that update on information not pressure, a 90-day answer avoiding hero and passive mode, the reverse interview as evaluated signal plus due diligence.
- [[Recruiter Screen Playbook]] — The recruiter screen tests narrative coherence and logistics fit, not technical depth. It is rehearsed, not studied: a 90-second past-pivot-present-bridge narrative, three project one-liners, a researched comp range closed with 'is that in band?', logistics decided before the call, a concrete AI-tool-fluency answer, and one question that brings early doubts to the surface.
- [[Technical Breadth Round Playbook]] — The ML/LLM breadth round grades the follow-up, not the definition. Rehearse every concept as a 60-second answer plus a different layer one level deeper. Use the honest-gap move instead of bluffing. Drill trade-off pairs by their deciding axis rather than their verdict. Audit every resume claim two levels deep.
