- [[System Design Scored Behaviors]] — The in-room behaviours a system design round actually scores, beyond the diagram: scope aggressively, turn requirements into APIs and numbers, justify every box causally, design from access patterns, evolve from the simplest working system, split services only for different scaling, name your own bottlenecks and forgotten durability, and show depth on one technology rather than adding boxes.
- [[AI System Design Interviewer Rubric]] — How an applied-AI system design round is scored from the interviewer's side: 11 rubric dimensions, five vague phrases that always draw a probe, an 11-phase adaptive flow, seniority bar, and three personas that change tone but not the rubric.
- [[AI System Design Walkthroughs]] — Nine phase-by-phase AI system design problems reduced to a shared five-phase board layout and the one non-obvious call each teaches.
- [[AI Engineering Take-Home Landscape]] — Field data on what AI-engineering take-homes ask for and how they are graded; eval-harness-first is the biggest differentiator; recurring hidden constraints to rehearse.
- [[Multi-Level Implementation Round]] — A coding-round format where one problem is revealed in 2–3 levels over 45–90 minutes; the grade is mostly whether the level-1 data shape absorbs later levels without a rewrite.
- [[Agent Engineering Question Chains]] — Real AIE technical rounds ask a main question about a concrete system, then a follow-up that multiplies a dimension; answer kernels for nine recurring themes.
- [[AI Engineering Field Question Bank]] — A questions-only bank of ~560 candidate-reported AI-engineering interview questions used for cold recall, plus the round guidance that travels with it — production thinking over theory, decisions-not-tool-names in deep dives, how AI system design differs from classic ML design, and which take-home asks are red flags.
- [[Drill Gym Tracks and Schema]] — How interview practice reps are organised as a gym separate from the curriculum — language/topic tracks, a one-file-per-drill schema whose `concepts` draws concept→drill graph edges, a spoiler-safe body order, one append-only log read live by Practice mode, and a per-track gap list.

## Meta

- [[AI Engineering Curriculum Structure]] — The two-wave model of the learn-ai-engineering corpus — generative-ai as the application wave (seven pillars) and ai-engineering as the discipline wave (six foundations) — plus the dependency ordering behind the pillar sequence.
- [[Agile Workflow Definitions]] — Definition of Ready, Definition of Done, WIP limits, weekly cadence, and ceremony-to-skill mapping for the Claude Code workflow system.
- [[Claude Code Hook Architecture]] — Claude Code lifecycle hooks — PreToolUse/PostToolUse events, exit-code protocol (0=pass, 2=block), and the hook suite pattern used to enforce code quality automatically without mid-task reminders.
- [[Claude Workflow System]] — Personal Claude Code harness — global skills, PreCompact hook, phase checkpoints, and session notes — that automates context management across multi-phase engineering workflows.
- [[Code Review Drill — SANYI]] — Code-review interview drill using a real SANYI review as the worked example — a two-line diff that lints clean but violates the change contract, and the reviewing method that catches it.
- [[Documentation Boundary — Machine vs Human Docs]] — Who writes what — machine-consumed docs (CLAUDE.md, skills, plans) vs human-consumed docs (READMEs, wiki, design docs), with the akira dao exception.
- [[Karpathy LLM Wiki Pattern]] — The compiler analogy for personal knowledge bases — raw sources in, LLM compiles them into structured interlinked wiki pages, no vector infra needed.
- [[No-Placeholder Plan Discipline]] — A plan handed to an implementing agent carries every file's content in full, and a gap is treated as a defect in the plan rather than license for the agent to invent — with verification steps that check structure, not behavior, when the deliverable is markdown.
- [[Plan-Doc Status Enum]] — Nine-member Status enum for plan docs (7 in-flight, 2 terminal) with a forbid-and-relocate suffix policy — the Status line carries exactly one token, everything else moves to named fields.
- [[Puffin Consciousness Development Skills]] — A chained Claude Code skill family in the `guacamayo` project (renamed from `puffin` 2026-07-17) (genesis → wake → grow → reflect → synthesize → dream) that walks a user and Claude through a staged, multi-session self-development process — grounded in "user seed material" collected from prior conversations.
- [[SANYI Change-Contract System]] — Three-layer change-contract system (变易/简易/不易) for agent architectures — classifies every component into ever-changing, simple, or invariant, then enforces cross-layer discipline via init/review/audit modes.
- [[Session Insights]] — Compiled insights from 42 facet-analyzed Claude Code sessions — friction patterns, recurring themes, skill candidates, and learning outcomes.
- [[Session Knowledge Capture Patterns]] — Patterns for capturing, enriching, and classifying session knowledge — output type taxonomy, pre-compact enrichment, and the session-as-source-of-truth approach.
- [[Skill-Knowledge Information Flow]] — How knowledge flows between the four parallel systems — global skills, ai-project-template, learn-ai-engineering, and the librarian wiki — and the sync contracts between them.
- [[Wiki Graph Engineering — Edge Quality Over Edge Count]] — Why the link graph under-connects, why the typed-relationship subgraph is the layer worth querying, and the pinned edge-count denominator behind one-hop retrieval expansion.
- [[Three-Doc Spec Split]] — scope / requirements / roadmap as three docs with stable prefixed IDs (PR-, CR-, TR-, D-, RK-) that plans and PRs cite
- [[Evidence-Gated Skill Pipeline]] — chained single-purpose skills over one evidence store, each refusing to run without the upstream analysis section, with a [VERIFY] tag carrying uncertainty across stages
- [[Wiki Health Metrics]] — The six deterministic numbers this wiki tracks and what each band means; every threshold is a local heuristic, with degree 8–20 and stub under 200 words set from this wiki's first snapshot and the rest second-brain-os's uncited convention.
- [[Workflow Pipeline Telemetry Audit]] — What a week of ledgers on a triage → build → review agent pipeline showed: ledgers that do not join, a dead retry counter, 3/3 unscoped-to-plan items invalidated vs 0/44 scoped, logging that dies with the loop, and a BUILT row that outran its evidence.
- [[Design Evidence Annex]] — Keep mechanisms on a design-of-record's plates under a line budget, move audit-grade evidence verbatim to an annex, draw each figure once, and leave `<span id>` stubs for every removed anchor.
- [[Capability vs Phase Skills]] — phase skills advance Status: and write the plan doc; capability skills write their own decisions artifact and never touch Status:, enforced by hook scope
- [[Job Verb Taxonomy for Skills]] — one job verb per skill (frame/size/decide/design/derisk/report/run) chosen by what the output changes; gaps recorded with reasons
- [[Void, Don't Close, an Invalid Measurement]] — ledger rows that measured the wrong thing or used a broken instrument are voided with a reason in a flag file, and skills get an explicit entry point to it
- [[Archived Skills Convention]] — retired skills move below the loader with ARCHIVED.md (date/reason/destination); fold criteria and the salvaged rules worth keeping
- [[Prototype Pipeline Handoff Signals]] — four signals a proto pipeline should emit: design-doc split, GATED/UNGATED named at execute time, SANYI skeleton at pipeline exit, audit before plan
- [[Skill Admission Test: Workflow With a Gate]] — A Claude Code skill earns its existence only if it is a workflow with a gate — facts go to refs, durable descriptions to docs, deterministic operations to Makefile/scripts, and judgment passes to agents — and its `description` is its entire discovery surface.
- [[Four-Stage Design Pipeline with Backflow]] — A design-of-record pipeline in four stages (initiative → product → system → roadmap) that re-enters at the earliest stale stage, detects staleness from a backflow digest of decisions added downstream, starts with a tree-first build-state audit, and treats phases as hypotheses with failure branches.
- [[Retro Open-Hypotheses Loop]] — A setup retro becomes a loop only when each applied change records an expected signal and a re-runnable command, and the next retro's first job is to re-run those commands and mark each row verified or failed — with findings ranked by cost × recurrence, a check preferred over a rule, and a control lane to separate real effects from reporting artefacts.
- [[Prototype Hardening Pass]] — A pre-demo tool (not a pipeline phase) that walks six defect classes distilled from ~1,900 lines of fixes to a prototype whose 300 tests were green — seams, runnable demo, service surface, eval falsifiability, doc parity, recurring patterns — and fixes each gap in-session with a failing test, never committing and never touching Status.
- [[Prototype Genesis Interview]] — A gated prototyping interview that routes AI-shaped vs ML-shaped work before loading any spec (and says out loud which specs it skipped), forbids reading the data until the framing conversation ends, runs demo and automated modes with opposite defaults, and ends with a mandatory "what did I not know how to do" gate that writes gaps back to the strongest enforcement layer.
- [[Skill Discovery Failure Modes]] — Why a Claude Code skill silently fails to trigger or gets ignored — the shared ~15k-character description budget that drops skills with no warning, disable-model-invocation removing the description entirely, auto-formatted multi-line YAML, vague descriptions, name/casing mismatches, compaction forgetting — plus how to tune a description with near-miss negative trigger evals.
- [[Design Decisions Migrate Into Plans]] — When design skills run once at initiative start, the real design decisions migrate into per-issue plans (a decision register grew 30 → 124 rows with zero design passes) because design output is the wrong granularity, shape and trigger for a one-issue-at-a-time pipeline — the fix is to turn design content into checklist rows the workflow reads, and measure "upstream-silent" questions per plan.
- [[Confirm vs Decide Decision Packet]] — exec decision format: (confirm) facts vs (decide) calls with If YES/If NO and a recommendation so "agreed" is a complete answer; architecture split from product decisions
- [[Automated Paper Discovery Pipeline]] — arXiv feed + non-blocking Semantic Scholar enrichment, three-band relevance gate, arXiv-ID dedup, hard daily cap against overwhelm
- [[Topic-Lifecycle Docs Layout]] — group agent docs by topic and lifecycle (scope/build/archive) with a review-to-plan write-back, not by artifact type
- [[Chain Length Beats Point Total in Sprint Planning]] — a sprint can hit its point target yet be unfinishable because a within-track dependency chain exceeds the sprint in days
- [[Cost-Leveled Review Ladder]] — A cross-repo review entry point with three cost levels (L1 mechanical diff + lint with no agents, L2 adds scanner agents and tests, L3 adds a full contract audit for one repo only), no sub-flags, delegation to each repo's own checks, and headless runs that never ask but write a Needs-input section instead.
- [[Stability-Graded Canon Writing]] — rank canon gaps by damage, write emerging topics at their settled level (levers, taxonomy, threat model), promote findings after three implementations, wire refs into the Refs: line
- [[Knowledge Base Recompile Stage]] — A compiled LLM wiki needs a recompile stage, not just a compile stage — a periodic, dry-run-first ritual that merges, retires (tombstone + supersedes) and compresses pages from age, size, similarity and retrieval-telemetry evidence, so precision does not decay as the KB grows.
- [[Evidence-Driven Wiki Compaction]] — manual monthly recompile: evidence-backed merge/retire/compress, dry-run, tombstones
- [[Canon Repo, Symlinked Consumers]] — Every shared skill, spec and hook lives in exactly one owning repo; ~/.claude and rendered templates reach it through symlinks, never copies — copier follows symlinks so generated projects get real files, and the only permitted fork is a .jinja twin kept line-for-line with its root.

## Projects

- [[Atlas Project]] — Time-series forecasting and customer-segmentation agent system — Planner/Forecaster/Evaluator/Learner loop over ARIMA and Chronos models, an HDBSCAN-with-KMeans-fallback segmentation pipeline, and a Neo4j knowledge graph linking customers to segments and merchants.
- [[Change-Contracts Rollout]] — 2026-07-17 decision record — SANYI promoted to the global skills reservoir, SANYI.md contracts drafted for playground/librarian/atlas, contract checks wired into review skills, template seeds contracts at scaffold time; akira rollout blocked on hardcoded scan paths.
- [[Librarian Repo Feature Notes]] — Dated notes on how the librarian repo's UI panels, skill verbs, retrieval eval gate and code index work and why they are built that way, moved out of CLAUDE.md so it loads lighter each session.
- [[NYC-DSSG Project]] — NYC Data Science for Social Good — platform engineering for a nonprofit serving 600+ nonprofits via 300 volunteers; building knowledge base, project templates, and PM agent.
- [[Librarian Graph Explorer]] — Local React Flow wiki graph explorer — multi-dimensional edge types (wikilink/semantic/tag-shared), UMAP semantic layout, and agent chat with graph highlighting and wikilink write-back. Addresses the gap where Obsidian cannot do multi-edge toggling or embedding-based spatial layout.
- [[Librarian KB — Build Plan]] — Phased build plan for the Librarian KB — Phases 1–5 complete, Phase 6 (connectors) active, Phase 8A+B (React Flow UI) done, Phases 9–15 future.
- [[Librarian Project]] — The Librarian service — a LangGraph CRAG-based RAG pipeline for knowledge retrieval, deployed as a Python FastAPI service with evaluation harness.
- [[Listen-Wiseer Project]] — Spotify recommendation agent with ENOA taste-map personalisation — LangGraph ReAct + Chainlit UI, LightGBM classifiers, DuckDB vss RAG, and three-tier eval harness.
- [[Parallax]] — An evidence-driven PR review system that combines multiple review perspectives into one explainable merge decision — the judging third of the Akira/SANYI/Parallax triad, deliberately optimizing against comment count.
- [[Kiez Project]] — Berlin venue map and assistant: LangGraph open-data pipelines, replayable ranker, no-spend-by-default model use, Supabase deploy gotchas
- [[learn-ai-engineering Project]] — The self-paced AI and data engineering curriculum repo — six subjects (programming, data analytics, data engineering, data science, generative AI, AI engineering), three learning paths, a depth/summary/reference layering with exam-prep guides kept in a separate repo, a five-criterion definition of done, and a structural freeze as of 2026-10-28.
